#2262. Bari, Rastelli crede nella rimonta: "Vinceremo il campionato attraverso un percorso tortuoso"

Massimo Rastelli, tecnico del Bari, è intervenuto in conferenza stampa in vista della sfida di lunedì sera in casa del Picerno: "È una gara che deve vederci offrire una prestazione diversa dalle ultime tre fatte. Partendo dalla prestazione aumentano le possibilità di vincere la gara, ed è quello che ci serve per rilanciare l'andamento delle ultime uscite". Il tecnico considera il Picerno un avversario particolarmente insidioso, anche per la crescita mostrata nelle ultime settimane: "È un cliente scomodo, come lo sono tutte. È una squadra che da anni fa campionati di alto livello, consolidando il lavoro precedente. Non sono partiti benissimo quest'anno, ma hanno ritrovato fiducia, verve e con il cambio di allenatore, uniti alle caratteristiche del campo, sarà una gara molto insidiosa".

Come riportato in conferenza stampa, Rastelli ha difeso il percorso compiuto finora dal gruppo, pur riconoscendo la necessità di trovare maggiore continuità. Il lavoro quotidiano offre indicazioni positive, ma il rendimento in partita non sempre rispecchia quanto mostrato durante gli allenamenti: "Ci sono tantissime cose positive perché conosco il percorso fatto dall'inizio e le difficoltà affrontate per essere il Bari. Rivedendo le partite vedo lati positivi e aspetti da migliorare, su cui lavoriamo settimanalmente. In allenamento i ragazzi fanno cose eccellenti, ma in partita fatichiamo a mantenere quella continuità nel fare sempre la cosa giusta. Il mio compito è proprio correggere questo aspetto". Nonostante le difficoltà, l'allenatore mantiene fiducia nella possibilità di raggiungere l'obiettivo stagionale: "Io mi sento di dire che questo Bari vincerà il campionato attraverso un percorso tortuoso, difficile, di sofferenza e sacrificio. Quello che siamo riusciti a fare in tre mesi tra mille difficoltà è difficile da capire dall'esterno. Vorrei qualche punto in più e prestazioni più continue, ma so che i ragazzi ci stanno mettendo l'anima".

Il tecnico ha poi sottolineato il rapporto con la tifoseria, la cui passione rappresenta uno stimolo ulteriore per la squadra: "Con i ragazzi parliamo sempre, analizzando gare e prestazioni. Chiedo sempre di fare lo step di crescita individuale, che porta a quella collettiva. C'è grande disponibilità, anche se in campo non esprimiamo sempre il massimo delle potenzialità. I tifosi stanno soffrendo e la loro passione è uno sprone che ci dà ulteriori motivazioni per fare ciò che ci viene richiesto". Sul piano tattico, Rastelli ha spiegato di aver inizialmente dovuto adattare le proprie idee agli uomini disponibili e alla condizione fisica della rosa: "All'inizio ho dovuto adattarmi agli uomini a disposizione e alla condizione fisica. Ora che stiamo recuperando tutti, dobbiamo trovare nuovi equilibri e sintonie per dare continuità alle prestazioni, che finora sono state a corrente alternata".

In vista della gara, arrivano indicazioni positive anche sul fronte dei recuperi. Maiello si è allenato regolarmente, così come Mantovani e Gercic, mentre le convocazioni saranno definite dopo la rifinitura. Possibile anche una maglia da titolare per Colangiuli, apprezzato per il contributo offerto nelle ultime apparizioni: "Si è sempre messo in mostra sin dall'inizio, dando un contributo eccellente quando subentra. In Coppa Italia e sabato scorso è stato tra i migliori. È un ragazzo che prendo sempre in grande considerazione e potrebbe anche partire dall'inizio". Quanto alla classifica e ai sette punti di distanza dal Potenza, Rastelli invita a non perdere lucidità: "Siamo a inizio novembre, manca tantissimo e i campionati si vincono ad aprile, non a ottobre o novembre. La storia è piena di squadre che hanno recuperato tanti punti. Dobbiamo pensare solo a noi stessi e crescere partita dopo partita". Infine, un'indicazione sull'approccio richiesto sul sintetico di Picerno: serviranno rapidità di pensiero e reattività per superare la pressione avversaria.

#2261. Hearts star admits 'I need to do better' after nightmare week

Stuart Findlay wants Hearts and himself to go into the international break on a high after a damaging double defeat this week.

The injury-ravaged Gorgie side were knocked out of the Premier Sports Cup semi-finals by Edinburgh rivals Hibernian at Barclays Hampden last Sunday.

Then on Thursday, the 31-year-old former Kilmarnock, Philadelphia Union and Oxford United centre-back headed in a late consolation goal in the 3-1 Conference League loss to Thun in Switzerland.

The deflating defeat left Wouter Vrancken’s side, who had 10 players missing through injury going into the game before Stephen Kingsley had to be replaced in the second half, with one point from nine in the competition ahead of the William Hill Premiership game against Falkirk on Sunday.

Asked what Vrancken’s message was to the team afterwards, Findlay told TNTSports: “That’s obviously a couple of tough defeats in a row for us, losing the derby, then Thursday night.

“But we’ve got a massive game again on Sunday and we need to make sure we rally together, we need to be around each other.

“We know it can be difficult times. And when it’s difficult times, that’s when we need to pull through, show our character and make sure we turn around and have a good result going into the break.

“Personally, I know I need to do better if the manager gives me another chance on Sunday. I’ll make sure I do better than what I did on Thursday and all I need to take from it is to move on with the rest of the team and make sure we put things right on Sunday when we play Falkirk.

“It wasn’t what we were hoping for, obviously. We had a game plan that we obviously didn’t stick to, the way that we wanted to do it.

“I thought, we sort of didn’t get going, personally, there was a lot of duels at was back that I should be doing a lot better to understand.

“It was a really disappointing night. It’s a night that we expected more from. So we came away very disappointed.”

#2260. FacileRent Molinari Napoli, presentata la stagione 2026/27: sport, territorio e nuove ambizioni

– Passione, ambizione e vicinanza al territorio. All’insegna di questi aspetti la Molinari Napoli ha presentato il nuovo anno sportivo ed in particolare la stagione della squadra che competerà in Serie B2 femminile. Un evento – quello tenutosi sabato 7 novembre al PalaVesuvio – che ha coinvolto società, istituzioni, partner, staff tecnico e atlete, per raccontare obiettivi, ambizioni e prospettive di un progetto che vuole continuare a investire nello sport e nel territorio.

«Lavoriamo sul territorio da anni, avvicinando i giovani di Ponticelli allo sport. Da cinque anni sentiamo la vicinanza dell’Amministrazione comunale che ci ha aiutato in qualsiasi circostanza», ha dichiarato il Presidente della Molinari Napoli, Gennaro Nappo. «Per la Serie B2 c’è tanta ambizione, voglia di crescere e di essere d’esempio per il settore giovanile. Abbiamo costruito una squadra competitiva e speriamo che il campo ce lo dimostri».

Presenti alla conferenza l’On. Borrelli e l’Ass. Ferrante

«Io non credo più all’idea del centro e delle periferie per come si sta evolvendo il sistema sociale del nostro territorio. Ogni luogo come questo (il PalaVesuvio di Via Argine n.d.r.) è oro colato da valorizzare», ha dichiarato l’Onorevole Francesco Emilio Borrelli, presente alla conferenza stampa. «Lo sport è qualcosa di straordinario non solo perché ci insegna la disciplina, il rispetto del prossimo e l’impegno, ma anche perché ci spinge a fare attività fisica. Noi siamo in questo momento la Regione più obesa d’Italia per quanto riguarda i bambini. Bambini che non fanno sport: è importante fargli fare un’attività pulita e sana». Borrelli ha concluso il suo intervento sottolineando la vicinanza alla squadra: «Verrò sicuramente a vedere una delle vostre partite: tiferò per voi».

«Lo sport è una leva fondamentale, anche se a volte viene considerato secondario, soprattutto per quanto riguarda i supporti economici», ha dichiarato l’Assessora allo Sport ed alle Pari Opportunità del Comune di Napoli, Emanuela Ferrante. «Cinque anni fa, con grande difficoltà, ho provato a recuperare delle somme per riqualificare gli impianti, senza i quali non si può andare da nessuna parte. A conclusione di questo anno, che ha visto Napoli essere Capitale Europea dello Sport, posso dire con fierezza e con grande gioia che abbiamo investito 35 milioni di euro su tanti gli impianti sportivi. Partendo dagli impianti riqualificati si può ambire a realizzare quello che penso sia il diritto di tutti i ragazzi: fare sport».

L’Assessora si è rivolta poi direttamente alla squadra. «Siete una testimonianza ed un esempio per tutte le ragazze ed i ragazzi di Ponticelli. Questa stagione sarà una straordinaria esperienza e cercheremo di far riempire la palestra ogni volta che giocherete in casa».

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2259. The Millwall team Alex Neil should start vs West Brom to recover from 3-0 midweek defeat

Millwall were well-beaten on Tuesday night as Birmingham City scored three goals inside 30 minutes to secure the victory. The loss sees the Lions pick up just four points from their last five games.

Alex Neil’s side had an xG greater than BCFC’s at 1.42, but errors cost them any potential result. The South London outfit were no match for the Blues’ superior defence, who have conceded just 13 times this term.

Heading to The Hawthorns on Saturday, Millwall need to pick up a result. With just one point per game on average on the road this campaign, the Lions have a lot to do against a West Brom side who are averaging 1.79 points from their last five matches.

Alex Neil will make changes after the defeat to Birmingham City. The Lions’ head coach should revert to a 4-2-3-1 shape after the 4-4-2 formation proved unstable at St Andrew’s. Max Crocombe is currently the only true senior goalkeeper that Millwall have right now.  His poor form continued in midweek, where he prevented -1.31 goals. He will retain his place as will the rest of the back line.

Jordi Osei-Tutu is certain to play at right-back following another injury to Tristan Crama. The rest of the back four will also be the same, with a partnership of Jake Cooper and Caleb Taylor, with Zak Sturge at left-back. Millwall’s defence are averaging 1.71 goals conceded per game, and Neil will be hoping for a better performance on Saturday.

Jenson Metcalfe should come back into the starting XI after his suspension, and he will partner Derek Mazou-Sacko, who made just one accurate long ball against BCFC. Ahead of them in the advanced midfield role should be Camiel Neghli, who has been the Lions’ best performer this season. He has created 26 chances for the South London outfit and made 20 successful crosses.

Mark Sykes should come in on the right wing to bring some more stability to Millwall’s shape. The Irishman has made 29 defensive contributions and 12 clearances this campaign. On the left side, Kyrell Lisbie must retain his place to give the Lions that attacking quality. Despite his xG of 0.25 on Tuesday, the summer signing has won his side 13 fouls this term.

Up top, Neil has options to choose from with Mihailo Ivanovic and Josh Coburn back from injury. However, the Serbian was arguably rushed back against Birmingham City, and Lyndon Dykes should lead the line at The Hawthorns.

The Lions are averaging 1.29 points from their last five games, and it has seen them drop to 13th in the table and four points from the top eight. Their defeat to Birmingham City proved that Millwall need a reset to come back stronger.

Neil’s side entered the last break just a point from the play-off places, and they could enter the next one seven points away. The South London outfit had 25 points at this point last season and were sitting in 6th.

Millwall are clearly suffering from their number of injuries at the start of the campaign. Once key players like Coburn are back to full fitness, the Lions will have some catching up to do.

#2258. Borussia Dortmund vs Elversberg - prediction, team news, lineups

By Lewis Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 21:01

Borussia Dortmund must maintain the pace at the top of the Bundesliga on Saturday, when they welcome Elversberg to Signal Iduna Park.

Black and Yellow are second in the table with 18 points and currently trail Bayern Munich due to goal difference, while newly-promoted Elversberg are in seventh place with 13 points.

Dortmund deserve credit for coming away from Allianz Arena on October 31 with a point, drawing 2-2 with Bayern in their most difficult league game of the season thanks to Fabio Silva's 89th-minute equaliser.

Boss Niko Kovac should be delighted with the result, but it would be understandable if he was frustrated by the fact his side have now drawn three of their last four Bundesliga outings.

A ninth second-placed finish in 15 seasons would be disappointing, though BVB supporters should still be looking forward positively given their team boast the best defensive record in the division this term having conceded just six goals.

The hosts' defensive resilience was on display against Real Betis in the Champions League on Wednesday, when they won 2-1 thanks to second-half strikes form Silva and Konstantinos Karetsas.

That victory was Dortmund's sixth win at Signal Iduna Park in their last seven fixtures at the stadium, a period in which they avoided defeat.

Elversberg returned to winning ways on October 30, beating Mainz 05 4-1, a game in which they led 3-0 at half time and managed to score a fourth before conceding in the 92nd minute.

The success that the visitors have experienced in the Bundesliga should not be underestimated, especially as 30 points were enough to guarantee survival in the top flight in 2025-26, and the club are on track to accumulate 55 points.

Manager Vincent Wagner may have overseen two wins in his team's past three league clashes, but he will still be concerned that his side have lost lost four and drawn one of their last seven matches in all competitions.

Schwarz und Weiss will hope to avoid suffering a third consecutive loss on the road, with the visitors having won only one of their four away Bundesliga contests.

Elversberg netted eight goals in those four away games, while all four of the goals they have scored on the road in the league came in a 4-3 victory against Borussia Monchengladbach in matchweek one.

Borussia Dortmund form (all competitions):

Dortmund's injury list is starting to clear up, with only Emre Can (knee), Justin Lerma (muscle) and Giannis Konstantelias (knee) confirmed to be on the sidelines, while the first two are set to return this month.

Kovac is likely to field a three-man defence consisting of Joane Gadou, Waldemar Anton and Nico Schlotterbeck, stationing the trio behind Jobe Bellingham and Felix Nmecha.

Striker Serhou Guirassy is a slight doubt, but even if he is available, it may be better to avoid any risks with his fitness by starting Silva up front.

As for Elversberg, the absence of defenders Jan Gyamerah (hamstring) and Luis Seifert (ankle) could lead to the selection of Luca Sirch, Lukas Pinckert, Maximilian Rohr and Lesse Gunther in the backline.

Centre-forward David Mokwa registered an assist against Mainz, though he will be keen to find the back of the net himself on Saturday.

Borussia Dortmund possible starting lineup:

Kobel; Gadou, Anton, Schlotterbeck; Ryerson, Bellingham, Nmecha, Svensson; Beier, Chukwuemeka; Silva

Kristof; Sirch, Pinckert, Rohr, Gunther; Keidel, Poreba; Petkov, Onyeka, Krattenmacher; Mokwa

Elversberg will almost certainly prove to be challenging opponents given their impressive display last time out.

However, their record away from home is far from perfect, while BVB have been excellent at home, so perhaps Dortmund will be able to keep the pressure on Bayern.

#2256. St Mirren sick of Old Firm 'hard luck' stories as Rangers come calling

Craig McLeish does not want the Old Firm to be a “hard luck story” for St Mirren as he prepares for the visit of Rangers in the William Hill Premiership on Saturday night.

Since moving from the youth set-up to become Buddies boss in March – initially on a temporary basis before making the move permanent in June – McLeish’s side have run both Celtic and Rangers close on several occasions.

Most recently, St Mirren lost to a last-gasp goal in a 1-0 defeat at Ibrox, following a 2-1 home defeat to Celtic and a 5-1 defeat away to Rangers in the Premier Sports Cup.

In his dugout debut for Saints in March, Rangers won 1-0 in Paisley, before St Mirren went down 1-0 at Celtic Park before taking the Hoops to extra-time in the Scottish Cup semi-final, only to eventually lose 6-2.

McLeish said: “We don’t want to be the hard luck story every time we play against them and you get plaudits like, ‘oh well done you played well but unlucky again’.

“We want to be able to go toe-to-toe with anyone in the league and come out with positive results and against Rangers and Celtic, we have not managed to do that.

“A couple of the games have got away from us and that’s what you’re trying to fight against and make sure that the games do go right to the end and you’re always in with a chance.

“That’s the bit now for us as a group and confidence within the group, to go and get a positive result against one of them – that’s probably the next step for us.

“We’ve had a look at Rangers twice (this season) as well, and we obviously have to change some bits just because of personnel available to us.

“You’re just trying to constantly learn and adapt from the games that you’re playing against teams.

“You have to take those experiences on and just tweak little bits to it, so there will be little bits that we try and throw at Rangers that they’ve not seen from us before as well.

“That’s what we have to try to do. We have to try and be unpredictable with it.”

#2255. Valencia vs Real Madrid - prediction, team news, lineups

By Matt Law | 05 Nov 2026 21:19, Last updated: 07 Nov 2026 11:09

Real Madrid will be bidding to post an eighth straight win in all competitions when they continue their La Liga campaign against Valencia at Mestalla on Saturday night.

Jose Mourinho's side will enter the match off the back of a 3-2 victory over AEK Athens in the Champions League, while Valencia recorded a much-needed win in their last league fixture, beating Elche 1-0 away from home.

There is no getting away from the fact that it has been a tough campaign for Valencia, with a total of nine points from 11 matches leaving them down in 19th spot in the La Liga table.

Los Che have the joint-worst attacking record in the division this term, netting just eight times, and also have the look of a team that could be facing a battle against the drop.

Umar Sadiq struck the only goal of the match in a 1-0 victory over Elche last time out, and Valencia will now aim to make it back-to-back wins in La Liga for the first time since May.

Javier Aguirre's side do have another game after this one ahead of the November international break, tackling Tavernes in the Copa del Rey on November 10.

Valencia lost both league games against Real Madrid during the 2025-26 campaign, including a 2-0 reverse at Mestalla last time out, but they do have a recent victory over Los Blancos to their name, triumphing 2-1 at Bernabeu in April 2025.

Real Madrid lead the overall head-to-head record between the two sides, having posted 114 wins to Valencia's 59, while 43 of their 216 matches have finished all square.

Los Blancos have been far from perfect this season, but they are currently in excellent form, winning each of their last seven matches in all competitions, including their last four in the league since the 2-1 reverse to Atletico Madrid ahead of the last international break.

Mourinho's side are currently third in the La Liga table, three points behind the leaders Barcelona, with their record in the league standing at nine wins and two defeats from 11 games.

Real Madrid posted a 3-2 win over AEK Athens in the Champions League on Wednesday to make it four victories in the league stage of the 2026-27 competition, and they are already in an excellent position to reach the next round of the tournament.

Los Blancos' 2-1 win over Barcelona in La Liga at the end of October was so important, and another success here would see Mourinho's team join the current leaders on 30 points.

Valencia will be without the services of Sergi Canos (knee), Diego Lopez (knee) and David Otorbi (hamstring) through injury on Saturday, while Jose Manuel Copete is a doubt, as he continues to recover from a knee issue.

Sadiq is now back from a hamstring injury, and having netted the winner off the bench against Elche last time out, the striker should now earn a spot in the XI.

Harvey Elliott is also available again after serving a suspension, but it remains to be seen whether the on-loan Liverpool attacker is brought back into the squad.

As for Real Madrid, Rodrygo (knee), Raul Asencio (leg) and Ferland Mendy (hip) remain unavailable, while Vinicius Junior will serve the final game of his domestic suspension.

Arda Guler and Aurelien Tchouameni were left out entirely against AEK in the Champions League but have already been confirmed as starters in this match alongside Dean Huijsen and Ibrahima Konate, with the duo set to feature in the middle of the defence.

Kylian Mbappe and Jude Bellingham will once again be included in the starting side, while there should also be a return to the team for Federico Valverde, who scored Real Madrid's late winner in the five-goal thriller with AEK.

Dimitrievski; Martinez, Tarrega, Pepelu, Gaya; Rodriguez; Maffeo, Mayol, Guerra, Rioja; Sadiq

Courtois; Valverde, Konate, Huijsen, Cucurella; Tchouameni, Bernardo; Guler, Bellingham, Diomande; Mbappe

Valencia can make this a tricky match for Real Madrid, and we are backing the hosts to score, but Los Blancos' attacking quality should allow them to secure all three points.

#2254. Plymouth Argyle must eye January transfer move for 10-goal EFL striker: Opinion

Plymouth Argyle will get the opportunity to freshen up their ranks this winter with some reinforcements. PAFC are sat in 5th place in the League One table after their first 14 fixtures as they eye promotion back to the Championship.

The Pilgrims should target a move for Walsall striker Aaron Pressley to add more competition and depth to their attacking department. At the age of 25, he has already picked up a decent amount of EFL experience and he has potential to grow and develop further down the line.

Pressley, who is an ex-Scotland youth international, is a key player for the Saddlers and has proved in League Two that he knows where the back of the net is. He has made 15 appearances in all competitions for Lee Grant‘s side in this campaign, 12 of which have come in the league, and he has scored 10 goals already.

Plymouth Argyle are in the play-offs along with Oxford United, Luton Town and Reading as they chase down Huddersfield Town and Stockport County. They take on non-league Alvechurch at home in the FA Cup this weekend.

Plymouth Argyle sold key attacker Lorent Tolaj to Bristol City in the summer. PAFC have since missed his presence and goal scoring touch.

Welshman Will Evans was brought in as his replacement from Mansfield Town. However, he has only scored three times and hasn’t been prolific in Devon.

The Pilgrims are the lowest scorers in the top six and need to be more ruthless in front of goal. Therefore, signing another forward in January has to be a priority.

Pressley fits the bill for Plymouth Argyle and will be full of confidence at the moment. He is under contract at Walsall until June 2028 meaning he still has just under two years left to run down.

Standing at 6ft 4inc, Pressley is solid in the air and would give Plymouth Argyle another dimension going forward. He would be useful for PAFC to throw on if they needed a goal.

The Edinburgh-born man moved down to England from Hearts as a youngster and had spells at Aston Villa and Brentford in the Premier League. He has since had stints at AFC Wimbledon, Accrington Stanley, Stevenage and Barrow.

His move to Walsall in 2025 has worked out well for all parties and he is one of their most prized assets. The Saddlers could lose him soon though and it wouldn’t be a surprise to see other teams sniffing around him.

#2252. Derek McInnes delivers 'unbelievable' verdict on unsung Rangers standout

Derek McInnes is happy for goalkeeper Ivor Pandur to stay under the radar at Rangers.

Along with centre-back Owelthu Makhanya, the 26-year-old Croatian goalkeeper has been the Light Blues’ most consistent player this season following his move from Hull City on a four-year deal in the summer.

Pandur has brought a sense of calm to the Gers defence, which was demonstrated again in the 1-0 Premier Sports Cup semi-final win over Aberdeen at Barclays Hampden on Saturday.

Ahead of the William Hill Premiership clash with St Mirren in Paisley on Saturday night, an appreciative McInnes said: “I think he’s done so well.

“The fact I don’t really get asked a lot about Pandur, he’s just so quietly effective, and I think the players hold such a high regard for him and take confidence from him.

“There’s nothing worse — I’ve been there as a player when you’re not sure about your goalie and it affects a lot of what’s happening on the pitch. But when you know you’ve got a good one there, it sends out the exact opposite.

“The confidence that he sends throughout the team I think is clear, whether it’s dealing with things at his feet, in the air.

“His shot-stopping is unbelievable. I watch him in training – every shot has the same importance. He just wants to keep the ball out of his net and, for me, they’re the best types.

“He doesn’t try harder for one shot than he does the next one. Everything is ‘You’re not beating me’, I think he plays the game that way. He’s been brilliant.”

Rangers will face Hibernian in the Premier Sports Cup final next month and McInnes stressed the positives of taking the penultimate step towards the first trophy of the season and of his career as Gers boss.

The former Rangers midfielder said: “It’s been really as you’d expect. I think you’ve got to take the good for the game.

“It’s not easy getting to a cup final, and the fact that we’re there now, you’ve got to try and be pleased with that.

“I think the whole club — I was at the Charity Foundation ball on Sunday — and everybody’s so excited about going to the cup final, and that’s where we should be.

“It should be the norm that we should be feeling that we’re turning up at cup finals and putting ourselves in a position.

“We’ll look forward to it when it comes around, but I think it’s important now that we just really focus on this game before we get into the break, because it can easily dissipate, all that excitement and optimism, on the back of the next result.

“That’s how fickle it is. You’ve got to stay on the job.”

#2251. Bromley vs Rotherham United - prediction, team news, lineups

By Sam Varley | 05 Nov 2026 22:06

In just their second ever competitive meeting, Bromley and Rotherham United will do battle at the Copperjax Community Stadium on Saturday in the FA Cup first round.

The hosts are under the new management of Karl Robinson, who inherits a side sat bottom of League One, while their visitors are in their own battle at the bottom end of League Two.

Bromley will set out to make a winning start to life under Karl Robinson in their first FA Cup game of the campaign on Saturday after a tough start to 2026-27.

In a memorable season last time around, they earned automatic promotion from League Two, but the Ravens failed to carry their momentum into the new season with two-time promotion-winning manager Andy Woodman departing in October after overseeing 10 league games in which his side managed eight points.

They were unable to bounce back under the temporary care of Byron Webster, only managing a draw against Peterborough United before 4-1 and 3-2 away losses to MK Dons and Wigan Athletic respectively last week.

That leaves the Ravens bottom of the third tier with nine points on the board, having scored just 13 goals in 13 games and conceded 33 - 10 more than any other team in the division.

Now on the back of the midweek appointment of Karl Robinson as their new manager, who will be tasked with maintaining their third-tier status this term, Bromley will hope to make a winning start to progress in the cup.

Their visitors, meanwhile, are also seeking a confidence-boosting cup win to lift spirits in the midst of a tough campaign.

Rotherham United swapped places with their hosts last season, suffering a second relegation in three seasons to drop to League Two.

They began the term under the new management of Alex Bruce and are yet to reverse their fortunes, currently sitting 22nd on just 12 points from 12 games having won two and lost six of those while conceding 25 - the second most in the league.

The Millers are now winless in nine games in all competitions, including seven league games which culminated in a 2-1 loss to Swindon Town, before hosting Newcastle United Under-21s on Tuesday and losing 1-0 in the EFL Trophy.

With pressure growing at the AESSEAL New York Stadium, as they now only lead the League Two relegation on goal difference in the midst of their rut, Rotherham United will be desperate to put a win on the board on Saturday when they turn to cup action against new third-tier opposition.

Rotherham United form (all competitions):

Bromley are set to remain without goalkeeper Shamal George, defender Chanse Headman and midfielder Ashley Charles, who is yet to feature this season after playing a key role in their 2025-26 promotion.

Adam Jackson was also forced off in the first half at Wigan last weekend, meaning Omar Sowumni may take his place in defence alongside Kamarl Grant.

Victor Adeboyejo, Ethon Archer and JJ McKiernan are among those pushing to start after being reduced to substitute appearances last time out, with veteran Michael Cheek having led the line with support from Jodi Jones and Mickel Miller.

Rotherham United will likely remain without Ossama Ashley, Jamie Jellis, Fabio Tavares and Marvin Kaleta on Saturday due to ongoing injuries.

Defenders Dominic Iorfa and Jili Buyabu were sent off in last week's loss to Swindon but are available in the FA Cup, while Kian Spence was also back involved in midweek after a suspension.

Paul Mullin and Tom Bradshaw will both compete to come back into the attack from the outset, with the former having scored eight league goals so far this season.

Smith; Ifill, Sowumni, Grant, Pinnock; Saville, Hondermarck; Jones, McKiernan, Archer; Cheek

Rotherham United possible starting lineup:

Eyestone; Hall, Iorfa, Aljofree, Williams; Nemane, Spence, Cover, Lolos; Mullin, Bradshaw

Both teams will be desperate for a fresh start with a much-needed cup win on Saturday, and we give an edge to the hosts, who may feel a boost under new management against a depleted Rotherham team.

#2250. De Laurentiis. “Nessun caso, ho preferito bloccare la conferenza. Giochiamo ogni tre giorni”

Aurelio De Laurentiis, presidente del Napoli, scrive su X in merito all’annullamento della conferenza stampa: “In casa Napoli non esiste nessun caso. Quando mi sono accorto che stavamo per fare l’ennesima conferenza ho preferito bloccarla. Perché? Perché non credo che le conferenze stampa servano prima delle partite, considerando che giochiamo ogni tre giorni. Siamo in ritiro e bisogna evitare qualunque distrazione per i calciatori dalla loro concentrazione alla partita. Quando si va in ritiro la tutela di tutti deve essere totale. Aurelio De Laurentiis”.

I Am Naples Testata Giornalistica - aut. #2249. Manchester City in crisi? Maresca: "Niente panico, nella sosta capiremo cosa non va"

Cosa succede al Manchester City? L'inizio di stagione sembrava perfetto, nonostante il caos fuori dal campo di gioco per le questioni di giustizia sportiva: sette vittorie nelle prime sette gare di Premier League. Poi la frenata e l'inversione a U: tre sconfitte nelle ultime tre gare di campionato, l'ultima oggi per mano del Nottingham Forest, che è passato 3-1 su Donnarumma e compagni.

Il tecnico del City, Enzo Maresca, ha però frenato le analisi troppo drastiche spiegando ai microfoni della BBC che proverà ad invertire nuovamente la rotta durante la sosta per le nazionali. Queste le sue parole: "Niente panico. Dobbiamo capire cosa non va, ma niente panico. Dobbiamo usare la sosta per le nazionali per capire cosa non va e cercare di evitare di ripetere gli errori in futuro. Siamo sempre preoccupati per le sconfitte, ma abbiamo bisogno di equilibrio, prima abbiamo centrato sette vittorie di fila e poi tre ko di fila.

Poi ha aggiunto: "Abbiamo provato a reagire sul 2-0 ma era troppo tardi. Ne abbiamo segnato uno, probabilmente avevamo potuto segnare di più, ma in transizione ne abbiamo concesso un altro. Perché l'intensità era bassa? Ho bisogno di analizzarlo. La partita di oggi è probabilmente la prima a cui ci manca l'intensità nei primi 45 minuti. Il secondo tempo è andato meglio. Non è mai successo prima - dobbiamo capire il motivo e cercare di non lasciare che accadano di nuovo certe cose".

#2248. Derby County need to target winter swoop for 25-year-old after Blackburn Rovers summer interest: Opinion

Derby County are in the Championship relegation zone and have struggled for points. DCFC will get the chance to bolster their squad when the January transfer window opens up for business.

Dundee United left-back Will Ferry was heavily linked with a move to Blackburn Rovers in the summer. However, nothing materialised on that front in the end and he remained in Scotland beyond the deadline in September.

The Rams should now turn their attention to the defender and try and lure him to Pride Park this winter. Ferry, 25, is out of contract at the end of the season and his current club risk losing him for free in June 2027 if they don’t sell him in January, assuming he doesn’t pen an extension anytime soon.

The Bury-born man has made 91 appearances for Dundee United in all competitions to date, eight of which have come in this campaign, and he has chipped in with five goals and an eye-catching 15 assists from the back. He has a nice left foot and should move back down to England soon for a new challenge in his career.

Derby County currently have Craig Forsyth and Derry Murkin in the treatment room. Therefore, DCFC could do with another option in their position on the left flank of defence.

The Rams have had to play the likes of Ryan Hedges and Ryan Nyambe there over recent times. Ferry would be ideal for them now and could plug a gap for the time being.

The Republic of Ireland international, who has one cap to his name, has proved himself up in Scotland. He likes to put crosses into the box as much as possible and attacking players like Carlton Morris and Patrick Agyemang (when he’s fit) will benefit from his delivery.

Ferry rose up through Southampton’s academy before he was loaned out to Crawley Town as a youngster to get some experience under his belt. He then had two permanent years at Cheltenham Town before Dundee United came calling for his services.

Dundee United will want to receive a fee of some sort for Ferry and not lose him for nothing next year. Derby County should try and test their resolve.

The Rams can’t afford to get their recruitment wrong in January and need the right type of characters. At 25, Ferry’s next move is a big one and he needs to go somewhere he is guaranteed regular minutes.

Up next for Derby County is a clash away against Ferry’s old team Southampton after the international break. The Saints are eyeing promotion to the Premier League under Tonda Eckert.

#2247. Raphinha responds to Flick fears after Ancelotti talks

By Naim Beneddra | 05 Nov 2026 22:12

Barcelona captain Raphinha has moved to ease Hansi Flick's concerns over his upcoming international commitments, revealing that he has already discussed his fitness and playing time with Brazil manager Carlo Ancelotti.

The 29-year-old, who signed a contract extension until June 2030 on Thursday, admitted that he is still not fully fit after suffering another muscular problem while representing his country earlier this season.

His comments come just days after Flick publicly questioned the demands of another lengthy international trip, particularly given that Raphinha returned injured from Brazil's previous training camp.

However, the former Leeds United attacker insists that Ancelotti understands his situation and will take his recovery into account during November's friendlies against Japan and Singapore.

The reassurance comes at an important time for Barcelona, with Raphinha enjoying the most prolific start to a season of his career and several demanding fixtures approaching.

Speaking to reporters following the announcement of his new Barcelona contract, Raphinha addressed the concerns surrounding his participation in Brazil's upcoming matches.

The forward confirmed that he had already spoken with Ancelotti about his physical condition and the importance of managing his workload.

"Ancelotti, as a coach, knows he has to distribute playing time," Raphinha explained, as quoted by Barca Blaugranes.

"He knows that an injury would be complicated for Brazil too, and he knows how to manage minutes.

"We've spoken; he knows I'm recovering from an injury. I'm fine, but not 100%. Barça fans shouldn't worry because I'm taking good care of myself."

The admission that he has yet to regain full fitness is particularly significant given his recent injury history.

Raphinha suffered a hamstring problem during Brazil's international commitments in October, forcing him to return to Barcelona for treatment.

The setback ruled him out of two club matches, adding to concerns surrounding a player who has experienced several muscular injuries over the past year.

He also suffered an injury while representing Brazil against France in March, subsequently missing Barcelona's Champions League quarter-final against Atletico Madrid.

Another physical problem during the 2026 World Cup prevented him from featuring in the latter stages of Brazil's campaign.

Those setbacks explain why Flick was uneasy about the forward undertaking another long-distance journey so soon after his latest recovery.

Speaking on October 30, the Barcelona manager acknowledged that he had discussed Raphinha's situation with sporting director Deco.

"After the last international break, he came back injured, which wasn't ideal," Flick explained.

The German coach also made it clear that he would welcome a cautious approach from Brazil's coaching staff.

"If he can manage his playing time well, I'll be happy. Travelling to Singapore isn't ideal, but it's beyond my control."

The comments reflected Barcelona's concern over the demands of international football rather than any suggestion that Raphinha should abandon his commitments to Brazil.

Ancelotti's side are scheduled to face Japan on November 14 before meeting Singapore three days later, with both fixtures taking place in Singapore.

Raphinha's latest remarks offer some reassurance that his condition has been discussed with the Brazil manager, although he did not disclose any specific agreement regarding the number of minutes he will play.

The timing of the international break is especially sensitive for Barcelona because of Raphinha's extraordinary influence this season.

The Brazilian has scored 19 goals in just 13 appearances across all competitions, including 15 in La Liga and four in the Champions League.

His performances have helped Flick's side establish themselves at the top of the Spanish league, while his adaptation to a more central attacking role has made him an even greater threat in front of goal.

Previously deployed primarily on the wing, Raphinha has increasingly operated as a centre-forward, taking advantage of his movement and finishing ability inside the penalty area.

His form was rewarded on Thursday when Barcelona officially confirmed a contract extension keeping him at Camp Nou until June 2030.

The agreement provides further evidence of his importance to the club, particularly after a period in which his future had previously been the subject of transfer speculation.

However, Barcelona's immediate priority will be ensuring that their captain remains available during a demanding period of the campaign.

Flick's side travel to Atletico Madrid on Sunday for a crucial La Liga encounter, with only two points separating the clubs at the top of the standings.

They also have a Champions League meeting with Manchester City to negotiate after the international break, increasing the importance of Raphinha returning from Brazil without another setback.

The forward's situation presents a delicate balancing act for both managers.

Ancelotti will want to make use of one of Brazil's most productive attacking players, while Flick is understandably reluctant to risk losing a footballer who has become central to Barcelona's ambitions.

For now, Raphinha's discussion with the Brazil manager suggests that his recent fitness problems have been taken into consideration.

Nevertheless, his acknowledgement that he is still below full fitness means Barcelona will be watching his involvement in the November friendlies particularly closely.

#2246. SC Paderborn vs Eintracht Frankfurt - prediction, team news, lineups

By Anthony Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 22:17

Hoping to distance themselves from the Bundesliga's relegation zone, SC Paderborn will welcome European hopefuls Eintracht Frankfurt to Home Deluxe Arena on Saturday.

The hosts are searching for their second win in six games, while Die Adler could earn a third on the bounce.

Ralf Kettemann's Paderborn have performed admirably since making the step up from the second division, but they will be looking over their shoulder ahead of a tough challenge this weekend.

Saturday's hosts are currently 14th in the Bundesliga with nine points, sitting just two above 16th-placed Union Berlin in the relegation playoff spot.

Last time out, Kettemann's side were held to a 0-0 draw by Borussia Monchengladbach, failing to score for the second successive match.

In fact, only Schalke 04 have netted fewer than Paderborn's six goals in the German top flight this season, a concerning trait that the manager will be need to address in order to stave off the threat of demotion.

That being said, the promoted club have maintained an impressive defensive record so far, with Borussia Dortmund and Bayern Munich the only teams to have conceded less than their nine in eight matches.

Another positive for the hosts is the fact a win could lift them into the top half, considering they are level on points with every club up to 10th-placed Augsburg, and are just one behind ninth-placed Mainz 05.

Meanwhile, Adi Hutter's Frankfurt have been inconsistent for the majority of the 2026-27 campaign so far, though the club will be keen to prove they have turned a corner.

Die Adler beat Hamburger SV 3-1 at home last time out, and by the same score away in the DFB-Pokal just days earlier, marking the first time they have recorded back-to-back wins this season.

That latest success has Hutter's men eighth with 11 points, just two shy of sixth-placed Bayer Leverkusen in the race for Europe.

Heading into Saturday's showdown, Frankfurt have now won three of their last four outings across all competitions, only succumbing to a 1-0 defeat at Dortmund on October 24.

However, the visitors have come out on top in just one of their four Bundesliga away games this term - downing Mainz 3-1 in mid-September - while conceding 11 goals in the process.

Eintracht Frankfurt form (all competitions):

Paderborn will be without Marcel Hoffmeier due to a thigh injury, as well as fellow centre-back Nyamekye Awortwie-Grant, who returned to training late last month but is still building fitness after a lengthy layoff.

In their absence, Tjark Scheller, Mattes Hansen and Jano ter Horst look set to start in Kettemann's three-man defence, flanked by wing-backs Laurin Curda and Raphael Obermair.

Elsewhere, midfielder Timur Gayret remains sidelined with a knee injury, though Santiago Castaneda, Tom Baack and Gabriel Vidovic should be on hand to operate in the centre of the park.

As for Frankfurt, they are missing long-term absentee Jessic Ngankam, who is continuing his recovery from a thigh injury, while attacking midfielder Mario Gotze is a doubt due to an ear infection that caused him to miss training on Wednesday and Thursday.

With that in mind, striker Jonathan Burkardt will be supported by a trio of Ritsu Doan, Can Uzun and Fares Chaibi on Saturday.

There are also question marks bout the fitness of centre-half Robin Koch, who only trained partially this week after picking up a knock to his knee, but Lilian Brassier and Jeremiaha Maluze will be ready to line up at the heart of Hutter's defence if he is unavailable.

Noll; Scheller, Hansen, Ter Horst; Curda, Vidovic, Baack, Castaneda, Obermair; Tigges, Marino

Eintracht Frankfurt possible starting lineup:

Atubolu; Knauff, Brassier, Maluze, Baum; Onyedika, Aseko-Nkili; Doan, Uzun, Chaibi; Burkardt

Paderborn have struggled to score this season, but given their commendable record at the back, they are unlikely to suffer a thrashing this weekend.

Frankfurt will be buoyed by their improved results in recent weeks, though they will need to defy their form on the road to secure all three points, so expect to see a close-fought game.

#2245. SPFL star targets goal rush after finally breaking his Premiership duck

Joel Cotterill is determined to add to his goal tally at St Johnstone after breaking his scoring duck for the Perth side last weekend.

The 22-year-old attacking midfielder, who signed for Simo Valakari’s side from Swansea City in September, notched a dramatic leveller on his eighth appearance as the 10-men Perth side fought out a 2-2 William Hill Premiership draw with Kilmarnock at Rugby Park.

Ahead of Saturday’s visit from Dundee, for whom Cotterill played on loan last season, the former Wales Under-21 player said: “It’s something that I want to build on massively. There’s no better feeling than getting it in that kind of game as well.

“I just need to build on that now and try to get more as the season progresses. I think that’s a big part of my game that I’m missing at the moment: goals. I think I get into the right area sometimes, but it’s just something that I need to get better at in my game.

“That weekend is a good stepping stone for me now to just carry on and get as many as I can.”

St Johnstone have had four draws in the last six games to sit in seventh place, and Cotterill knows the next step is obvious and important. He said: “If you look at the games where we’ve had draws, we’ve been very unlucky. We had a sending off last weekend.

“But the togetherness we have and that spirit we have as a group, I think if we have 11 players on the pitch, we could give anyone a good game in this league, and we’ve shown that already.

“So I think we need to turn these draws that we’re having into getting the three points and coming away with a win. Dundee are very aggressive. Really good on the ball when the ball’s on the floor in play.

“I think they’ve got very, very good outlets and wingers, very quick, very direct. But I’m excited, I’m really excited for the game.

"I think it’s going to be a really good test for both teams.”

#2243. 3 players who Birmingham City should offload in January, including Lee Buchanan

Birmingham City have enjoyed a fine season to date after a slightly different approach in the summer transfer window. BCFC made seven new signings, two of whom had already been at the club, so it was a window of evolution not revolution as opposed to previous squad rebuilds.

But, with Chris Davies‘ side firmly in the Championship promotion conversation, the January window offers a chance to strengthen. The Blues can shift out some fringe players to make way for additions who can push them over the line in the bid to make the Premier League.

With that in mind, we put forward three players who should be leaving Birmingham City in the winter…

Somewhat surprisingly, Scottish winger Scott Wright stayed at St Andrew’s in the summer. The 29-year-old spent the second half of last season on loan at Dundee and the strong competition for minutes on the Birmingham City wings meant minutes were bound to be limited.

That has proven to be the case with Wright making only two Championship matchday squads and playing just one minute of senior football in a brief substitute appearance against Swansea City in the Carabao Cup.

Wright sees his contract expire in 2027, but to get back to playing regularly, he should leave BCFC in the winter. A return to Scotland or drop to League One seem like his most likely options.

Another player who has been on the fringes at Birmingham City is Lee Buchanan, although his situation is a little different. There was talk of a potential summer loan move as the left-back rebuilt his fitness after a serious knee injury, but he ended up staying put.

Alex Cochrane‘s emergence in the starting XI and the signing of Kristoffer Lund have left Buchanan as third choice in his favoured position. The Blues have been eager to be careful with his minutes given how long he was out, but by the time January comes around, he should be ready to play more regualrly.

There may be hesitance to cash in right away, so a loan feels more likely for January. Then, his situation can be reassessed in the summer, as he has shown he can be a Championship option before.

Last but not least is Marvin Ducksch, who has made eight Championship appearances off the bench. The German striker showed signs of promise in his debut campaign at Birmingham City, but a drink driving charge brought doubts over his future.

A summer move failed to materialise, but it is still widely expected that the 32-year-old moves on this season. A switch back to Germany is most likely, and could see Ducksch return to impressive form given his record of 71 goals in 138 2. Bundesliga appearances, and 34 in 130 in the Bundesliga.

August Priske is the lead striker at BCFC with Jay Stansfield and Luis Vasquez as the other options. Another centre forward may be needed if Ducksch is sold, but that seems like a logical switch.

#2242. Barnsley vs Aldershot Town - prediction, team news, lineups

By Sam Varley | 05 Nov 2026 22:18, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 09:00

Aldershot Town will set out to pull off an FA Cup first-round upset in South Yorkshire on Saturday, when they visit Oakwell to take on third-tier Barnsley.

The hosts enter at this stage while their visitors took part in the final qualifying round and needed a replay to progress.

Barnsley head into FA Cup action for the first time this season on Saturday in need of a victory to arrest a slump in the early stages of 2026-27.

The Reds reached the third round of this competition last year, falling in a trip to Liverpool, but begin their new campaign with just four wins from 17 competitive matches so far this term with all of those coming in the first six outings.

They did enjoy a positive start to life back under returning boss Daniel Stendel but are now winless in 11 games since early September, losing nine of those including four straight league defeats heading into the weekend.

Stendel's men were, at least, able to end that rut at home to Oxford United on Saturday, drawing 1-1 thanks to Vimal Yoganathan's opener having been down to 10 men inside 10 minutes, but the South Yorkshire side still sit 23rd in League One with just nine points on the board from 12 games having conceded 22 goals.

With positives to take from the weekend's display but real cause for concern around their recent months, Barnsley will be desperate to avoid an upset at the weekend and taste victory at Oakwell.

Their opponents, meanwhile, make the trip north having scraped through the final qualifying round in the FA Cup.

Aldershot Town entered this year's competition in the fourth and final round of qualifying, taking on ninth-tier Tadley Calleva, but they were only able to draw 2-2 at home before prevailing in a 2-0 triumph on the road in the replay.

That eventual cup win broke up a five-game losing run in the National League for the Shots, who also headed into the weekend in the midst of a rut, but they bounced back to winning ways at home to Gateshead, as Brody Peart scored the only goal inside five minutes.

Scott Davies's side climbed back up to 14th spot in the fifth tier with that result, and now on the back of a midweek National League Cup loss to Ipswich Town Under-21s, they will hope to defy the odds at Oakwell on Saturday to reach the FA Cup second round for just the second time since 2015.

Barnsley are set to remain without attackers Ben Hamed Toure and Fabio Jalo on Saturday due to ongoing injuries.

Stendel should field a near full-strength XI with the Reds desperate for a win to turn their fortunes round, meaning star man Reyes Cleary should continue on the wing, while Tobi Adeyemo was injured early on Saturday and may be replaced up front by Rylan Brownlie.

Veteran and recent signing Daniel Johnson may be afforded a start in midfield alongside either Luca Connell or Patrick Kelly, while Jack Shepherd is available at the back despite being sent off last time out in the league.

Aldershot Town will field a similar starting XI from last week's league win over Gateshead.

Marcus Dinanga and Marcus Wyllie will both hope to start in attack, but if they continue with a 4-2-3-1 shape the line should again be led by Tristan Abrahams, who has scored six goals so far this season.

A summer of plenty of change added several key men to their ranks, with Michael Stickland and Kiki Oshilaja now mainstays in defence and Dominic Odusanya and Conor Lawless likely to continue a midfield partnership if the latter is fit having been forced off last weekend.

Slicker; Kay, Gale, Shepherd, Larkeche; Connell, Johnson; Yoganathan, McGeegan, Cleary; Brownlie

Hayes; Stickland, Oshilaja, Dyce, Harfield; Lawless, Odusanya; Peart, Tuma, Sandah; Abrahams

Barnsley could certainly be vulnerable to a cup upset at home, given their poor league record and struggles at both ends of the pitch and growing concern around the club, but needing a win to start turning their form around, we do expect Stendel's men to outclass their non-league visitors.

#2241. Charlton Athletic vs Sheffield United - prediction, team news, lineups

By Sam Varley | 05 Nov 2026 22:29

Aiming to continue their climb up the Championship table and break into the playoff places, Sheffield United will head to The Valley to take on Charlton Athletic on Saturday.

Back-to-back victories have seen the visitors move within a point of the top eight, while their visitors sit four points above the drop zone after four straight losses.

Charlton Athletic return to action at the weekend aiming to end a dismal run of Championship form and arrest a drop towards the relegation zone.

Following a 19th-placed finish in their first season back in the second tier last time around, the Addicks won their first three league matches of the new term but have since drawn five and lost six of 11 matches.

In those games, Nathan Jones's side have scored just six goals and conceded 20, dropping to 21st spot in the process, just one place and four points above Burnley in the drop zone.

They have now suffered four straight defeats heading into their final match before the international break, falling to Watford, Norwich City and Middlesbrough before they hosted newly-promoted Bolton Wanderers on Tuesday and again came away empty-handed as Sam Dalby scored the only goal inside 20 minutes at the Valley.

With pressure surely growing on Jones, and the international break arguably coming at a decent time to arrest Charlton's slump, they will be desperate to put a win on the board to increase their lead over the drop zone ahead of the return to action.

Their visitors, meanwhile, head to London aiming to carry an upturn in form into the break.

Unlike the hosts, Sheffield United are now bouncing back from a slow start to the Championship season, having set out to challenge for promotion this term only to pick up just nine points from their first eight matches before the opening break of the season.

Chris Wilder's men have reversed their fortunes since, though, putting 13 more points on the board in six outings courtesy of four wins and a draw alongside a solitary away defeat to Portsmouth.

Following a 1-0 home victory over Wrexham on Friday, the Blades headed to strugglers Preston North End on Tuesday and came from 1-0 down to win 3-2 thanks to Sydie Peck's first-half hat-trick, leaving them on 22 points from 14 matches with just five teams conceding fewer than the 17 goals they have allowed.

Now in a much better position in ninth spot, and just one point behind eighth-placed West Ham United, Sheffield United will look to make it three straight wins with the chance to head into the second break of the campaign in the playoff places.

Charlton Athletic remain unable to call on winger Rob Apter, who has been sidelined since early September.

He is unlikely to be rushed back, though, and Wilder should field a similar starting XI from their midweek away win, meaning Jairo Riedewald may again partner Peck, who decided that game with his hat-trick, while Liam Kitching should come straight back into the defence after serving a one-match suspension on Tuesday.

In a 4-4-2 shape, they may again be flanked by Romelle Donovan and Tahith Chong with Ben Brereton Diaz, Tom Cannon, Tyrese Campbell and Oliver Arblaster competing for attacking spots in Bamford's absence.

#2240. Aberdeen suffer major double blow as Stephen Robinson confirms injury lay-offs

Aberdeen boss Stephen Robinson has been dealt a double injury blow ahead of the William Hill Premiership clash with Hibernian at Pittodrie on Saturday night.

Centre-back Mats Knoester and left-back Emmanuel Gyamfi will be out for at least 10 weeks with respective tendon and thigh injuries, stretching the Dons’ squad further.

Knoester picked up his injury at training before the recent 3-2 defeat away to Dundee United while Gyamfi had to go off in the first half of last weekend’s Premier Sports Cup semi-final defeat by Rangers at Barclays Hampden.

Robinson said: “We received some bad news on Mats (Knoester). He is going to be out for a minimum of 10 weeks.

“The same with Emmanuel (Gyamfi) after he got a scan. They’re two big blows to us when they were just coming back and Mats was finding really good form.

“They (Mats and Emmanuel) just can’t seem to get a run of games, unfortunately. I feel for them mentally as well. It’s so, so tough when you’re just back.

“Emmanuel had an injury previously before he came to the club, a thigh injury, and it’s gone again, unfortunately. So it’s just constant muscle injuries with him. Mats is a tendon injury, which is a big blow for us.

“The illness side of things hasn’t cleared, Ayoub Mouloua won’t be available. He’s picked up the bug now as well, so he probably won’t have any involvement on Saturday.

“We were hoping he’d be in today, but he’s still not better from it. So at the minute, he’s the only new casualty, and we hope that’s the end of it, the last of it, fingers crossed.

“Good news on Brad Lyons. He took a bad knock which looked bad, but actually didn’t transpire into anything serious. He’ll train today, so he’ll be fine for tomorrow.

“Dan (Happe) is good. He’s trained the last two days. Obviously, he had the virus and couldn’t play on Saturday. We have Toyosi (Olusanya) available again as well. He wasn’t available on Saturday.

“Some positives within that as well, and it is what it is. We just have to deal with it.”

After losing three games in a row in all competitions, Aberdeen are 10th in the table, just three points above bottom club Hibs.

Robinson said: “It’s a big game. We need to win. As I’ve said previously, every game you go into as Aberdeen manager, you need to win.

“I believe that, to give us a little bit of breathing space and let us get into the international window to reset ourselves, and hopefully maybe get Chris Cadden back and a couple of others closer to fitness, we need to win the game.

“Every game with Aberdeen, you need to win. There’s no excuses. You’re expected to win, especially home games. That comes with the territory. We’ll pick as positive a team as we possibly can.”

#2239. Padova, Artistico: "Sono contento del gol, ora ripartiremo con il dente avvelenato"

Gabriele Artistico, attaccante del Padova, ha parlato in conferenza stampa dopo il pareggio ottenuto con l'Empoli:

“Ho fatto un bel goal, provato molto in allenamento, e sono contento. Penso che siamo partiti molto forte, poi la tattica tante volte prende momenti che non riesci a gestire, il risultato finale crea rammarico ma credo che è un punto che ci porta ancora più cattiveria per dare di più.

Onestamente penso che siamo soddisfatti dell’andamento preso, ora andremo con la giusta cautela alla pausa per riprendere con il dente avvelenato. Penso che bisogna dare anche merito agli avversari, loro hanno preso coraggio nel finale, c’è stato un calo fisico per le tante partite giocate e loro sono usciti bene, cercheremo di lavorare di più per trovare l’episodio che porterà i tre punti.

Abbiamo sposato la filosofia del mister, tutti hanno disponibilità nei confronti dello staff, siamo tanti nuovi e col tempo possiamo solo migliorare. Fisicamente stiamo bene, quindi penso che a livello fisico siamo cresciuti, siamo contenti di questa situazione dobbiamo continuare così. Indipendentemente dal modulo, è importante mettersi a disposizione del compagno, mi trovo bene con tutti, la dedica del goal va a mia nonna che è mancata la scorsa settimana”

#2238. Data estimates how much Sheffield United star Sydie Peck is worth as midweek hattrick continues excellent start to the season

Sheffield United continued their impressive run of form on Tuesday night, securing a 3-2 victory away at Preston North End. SUFC are now unbeaten in four matches and have won four of their last six Championship outings.

It was an impressive all-round team display, but Sydie Peck was undoubtedly the standout performer. The 22-year-old midfielder produced a superb hat-trick in a man-of-the-match performance, becoming the first Sheffield United player to score a competitive first-team hat-trick since Billy Sharp against Aston Villa in February 2019.

Peck’s display was another reminder of his growing importance to the Blades, having also been the subject of recent exit speculation. The midfielder was linked with a move to now fellow Championship side West Ham over the summer.

Peck arrived at Bramall Lane from Arsenal’s academy as a free agent in 2021 and has made 105 appearances for the first team after a brief National League loan at Oldham Athletic.

SUFC will likely not allow Peck to go for a small fee, with a fee of £15m as a baseline the likely ball-park. Whether sides keen on the England U21 midfielder would value him at that is unknown.

FootballTransfers.com’s Estimated Transfer Value (ETV) model places him at €16.2 million (approximately £13.5m at current rates), with a range of €14.6m–€17.8m. This sits above the current FootballTransfers valuation of Divine Mukasa (around €12.8m) and is higher than most Championship players of a similar age-range.

Peck’s age as well as his status as an England youth international will see his value be higher than some other Championship midfielders.

Peck made his senior breakthrough during the 2024/25 campaign before taking another significant step forward last season, registering five goals and five assists in 41 appearances across all competitions.

The London-born midfielder attracted interest during the summer, but he made clear his desire to repay those who have shown faith in him at Bramall Lane, including manager Chris Wilder.

Peck remains happy at Sheffield United, and his performances this season suggest staying put has done little to slow his development. However, if he maintains his current level, further interest is inevitable, potentially making a move away increasingly difficult to turn down.

Sheffield United are keen to secure a new contract for Peck, with his existing agreement due to expire in 2028. The Blades would be wise to act sooner rather than later, with his recent displays likely to attract plenty of attention from elsewhere.

#2237. Minnesota United vs San Jose Earthquakes - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Ojele | 05 Nov 2026 22:31, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 09:18

Minnesota United and San Jose Earthquakes draw the curtain on their 2026 Major League Soccer regular season campaign when they lock horns at Allianz Field on Sunday morning.

While the home side head into the league finale needing maximum points to stand a chance of securing back-to-back playoff appearances, San Jose will aim to finish the campaign strong and gather momentum ahead of the post-season quest.

A strong showing at the business end of the season has put Minnesota United in contention for playoff qualification ahead of a crunch final-day showdown, with the Loons securing a 3-1 win over Los Angeles FC on Monday to maintain their newfound form.

Joaquin Pereyra turned in an eye-catching display at the BMO Stadium, netting on either side of Cherrion Valerius’s 34th-minute strike to put the visitors three goals up, before Sergio Vasquez pulled one back for Los Angeles FC in the 89th minute.

Coming off a 3-1 victory over Sporting Kansas City on October 29, Minnesota United have now picked up three wins in their most recent four outings and are unbeaten in six straight matches (3W 3D), having lost each of the five games preceding this run.

This late-season resurgence has seen Cameron Knowles’s men surge into 12th place in the Western Conference standings with 41 points from 33 matches, just two points behind ninth-placed Colorado Rapids in the final playoff qualifying spot.

Attacking improvements have been integral in Minnesota’s upturn in form, with 2+ goals scored in five of their last six games, and they will also hope to make home advantage count against an opposing side who have failed to win their last six league visits to Allianz Field, losing four and claiming two draws since a 3-1 victory in May 2018.

Having seen their form take a dip in recent weeks, San Jose Earthquakes finally rediscovered their winning touch last Saturday when they held on to see out a 2-1 victory over Real Salt Lake and record their first victory since late September.

In an action-packed first half at PayPal Park, Ousseni Bouda and Benjamin Kikanovic netted in a 15-minute spell to put the Quakes in a commanding lead, and after Juan Sanabria halved the deficit with a 23rd-minute strike, the home side did well to repel Real Salt Lake and leave with all three points.

Prior to that, San Jose saw their nine-game unbeaten league streak come to an end on October 25, when they were hammered 3-0 by FC Dallas at the Toyota Stadium, four days before playing out a 2-2 stalemate with Colorado Rapids on home turf.

After enduring a disappointing 2025 campaign that saw them finish 10th in the West and narrowly miss out on the playoffs, Bruce Arena’s men have enjoyed a significant turnaround this season, collecting 52 points from 33 games to sit third in the conference standings, three points behind second-placed Dallas.

With playoff qualification already assured, San Jose will be keen to finish the regular season on a high, but a run of just one win in eight matches since August suggests they still have work to do before the campaign concludes.

Minnesota United Major League Soccer form:

San Jose Earthquakes Major League Soccer form:

Experienced midfielder Wil Trapp has missed each of Minnesota United’s last three matches due to the MLS concussion protocol, and the 33-year-old will play no part in Sunday’s finale.

American youngster Kayne Rizvanovich was left out of the team list against Los Angeles FC last time out, and the 19-year-old goalkeeper is also expected to sit out this weekend's matchup.

Kelvin Yeboah has been a standout performer for Minnesota this season, and the Italian striker, who has racked up 18 goals and four assists in 29 league starts, will be one to keep an eye on.

San Jose Earthquakes will be without several players down the spine of the team, including Darius Johnson and Jonathan Gonzalez, who have been ruled out through injuries.

Nigerian forward Nonso Adimabua is currently recuperating from a muscle problem while DeJuan Jones continues his lengthy spell on the sidelines through an Achilles tendon injury.

Minnesota United possible starting lineup:

San Jose Earthquakes possible starting lineup:

Edwards Jr.; Kikanovic, Munie, Roberts, Romney; Harkes, Fernandez; Bouda, Tsakiris, Ricketts; Judd

Buoyed by their victory over Real Salt Lake, San Jose Earthquakes will head into the season finale in high spirits, but they have their work cut out against an in-form Minnesota United side who have their sights set on a top-nine finish.

However, San Jose’s form on the road leaves a lot to be desired, and we predict Minnesota will outscore Arena’s men this weekend and extend their impressive home record in this fixture.

#2236. Dallas vs Colorado Rapids - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Ojele | 05 Nov 2026 22:48, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 09:14

Dallas and Colorado Rapids will look to close out the 2026 MLS regular season campaign on a strong note when they go head to head in the season finale at the Toyota Stadium on Sunday morning.

Both sides head into the game having something to play for, with the hosts in a two-man race with San Jose Earthquakes for second place in the Western Conference standings, while Colorado will be aiming to seal their spot in the playoffs.

Having finished 11th and seventh in the 2024 and 2025 MLS seasons respectively, Dallas have taken another significant step forward this term and are closing in on their highest conference finish since their 2016 title triumph.

With just eight clean sheets and 50 goals conceded in the league, improvements must be made at the back ahead of their post-season campaign, but Eric Quill’s men can take pride in their performance at the attacking end of the pitch, where they have netted a club record 59 goals so far.

Dallas' defensive shortcomings were on display last Saturday when they were held to a 1-1 draw by Sporting Kansas City at Children's Mercy Park, with Jansen Miller netting a 45th-minute equaliser to cancel out Herman Johansson’s 19th-minute opener and force a share of the spoils.

Having suffered a 2-1 defeat against Columbus Crew at the Toyota Stadium last Thursday, the Bulls have now dropped five points in their last two matches, with their recent dip in form dealing a significant blow to their hopes of securing the conference title.

Dallas head into the season finale with 15 wins and 10 draws from their 33 MLS matches to collect 55 points and sit second in the Western Conference standings, six points adrift of first-placed Vancouver Whitecaps.

Like the hosts, a slump in form at the business end of the season has threatened to derail Colorado Rapids’ campaign, with Matt Wells’s men dropping three more points in a 4-2 home defeat at the hands of Chicago Fire last Sunday.

Second-half strikes from Robert Lewandowski, Puso Dithejane, and a brace from Jonathan Bamba inspired Chicago Fire to all three points at Dick's Sporting Goods Park, with Morgan Whittaker and Paxten Aaronson getting on the scoresheet for the home side.

The Denver outfit have now failed to win eight of their most recent nine matches, losing three and claiming five draws, a run which saw them crash out of the US Open Cup in the semi-finals following a 3-0 defeat against St. Louis City on September 17.

Following their recent struggles, Saturday’s tie is a must-win for Colorado Rapids if they are to secure playoff qualification, as they sit ninth in the Western Conference standings, one point off seventh-placed LA Galaxy and two points above 12th-placed Minnesota United.

Colorado Rapids Major League Soccer form:

Logan Farrington has missed each of Dallas’ last five matches since sustaining an injury in October and the 24-year-old forward is out of contention for this weekend's finale.

He is joined on the club’s injury table by Brazilian defender Geovane Jesus, who continues his long road to full recovery from a severe knee injury.

With 22 goals under his belt, Petar Musa has been a standout performer for Dallas this season and the Croatian striker, who sits second in the league scoring charts, will look to deliver the goods once again.

Colorado Rapids have their fair share of injury concerns, with 28-year-old defender Reggie Cannon and the Ghanaian duo of Ali Fadal and Ibrahim Sadiq all ruled out of this weekend matchup.

Experienced goalkeeper Zack Steffen has been sidelined since May through a shoulder injury, while Frenchman Youssef Maziz has missed the last 10 games since sustaining a leg injury against Austin FC back in September.

Sirois; Johansson, Urhoghide, Ibeagha, Abubakar; Valiente, Kaick, Ramiro, Binyamin; Job, Moreno

Colorado Rapids possible starting lineup:

Defreitas-Hansen; Rosenberry, Holding, Williams, Navarro; Frederick, Whittaker, Ojediran; Aaronson, Yapi, Ku-DiPietro

Colorado Rapids find themselves in a heated six-team battle in the West for the three playoffs spots still up for grabs, but with just three points from eight games on the road, they are in for another tough 90 minutes at the Toyota Stadium.

Dallas have lost just one home game since August 6, and given Colorado’s away woes, we predict the Bulls’ quality across the pitch will see them emerge with all three points, killing off the visitors’ playoff dreams.

#2235. 3 midfielders Blackburn Rovers should target in January after Mowbray admission, including 2018/19 loan star

Blackburn Rovers only signed three players in the summer transfer window, and sights are already set on January targets. Tony Mowbray has voiced a need to bolster in central midfield, with Adam Forshaw’s absence leaving only Kristi Montgomery, Sam Morsy, Moussa Baradji and Jake Garrett to pick from.

Rovers aren’t flush for cash, so they will likely have to be shrewd in their winter business. A lack of experience in the absences of Morsy and Forshaw mean some extra pedigree would be welcomed too.

Here, we put forward three options for the recruitment team at Ewood Park to consider…

First up is someone who would be a highly popular signing at Blackburn Rovers. Midfielder Harrison Reed spent a highly successful loan spell at Ewood Park in the 2018/19 season, providing three goals and seven assists in 36 games while away from former club Southampton.

He would join Fulham off the back of his Rovers stay, and he has played 212 times for the club since. However, Reed is down the pecking order under Alvaro Arbeloa having failed to make a Premier League matchday squad since August, despite being fit and available.

A drop to the Championship would give him the game time he deserves. Reed would bring experience, leadership and a well-rounded skillset to Tony Mowbray’s side and would be a very impressive addition.

A player in a similar position to Reed is Alan Browne, who has bags of Championship experience to his name but is currently struggling for minutes in the Premier League with Sunderland. The Irishman has only played in cup competitions and it seems highly unlikely that he makes a resurgence at the Stadium of Light.

Browne has been a player of interest to Blackburn Rovers before, and his vast second tier experience would make him an influential player on and off the pitch. The 31-year-old would bring leadership in the dressing room, offering an energetic box-to-box presence in midfield.

This paired with a strong technical game and versatility made Browne a regular in Middlesbrough’s push for the Championship play-offs while on loan last season.

Last but not least is a more left-field option, but Julius Magnusson would be a great player for Blackburn Rovers and potentially a real hit in the Championship. The 28-year-old Iceland international is a key man for Swedish side Elfsborg, and he has stood out over their 2026 season.

Magnusson is a very well-rounded player, chipping in with six goals and three assists in 30 games across all competitions this year, as per Fotmob. Amongst Allsvenskan midfielders, he ranks amongst the best in defensive contributions (200 – top 1%), interceptions (53 – top 3%), aerial duels won (55 – top 1%), line-breaking passes (217 – top 13%) and touches (1,659 – top 8%).

Those stats reflect his wide skillset, and at his age, he likely wouldn’t break the bank. Magnusson could come in and be a real hit for Rovers if they are happy to pay a fee to pry him out of his contract until 2029.
