#2233. Mainz 05 vs Bayern Munich - prediction, team news, lineups

By Lewis Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 22:48

Bayern Munich will hope to maintain their place at the top of the Bundesliga by beating hosts Mainz 05 on Saturday at Mewa Arena.

The Bavarians' tally of 18 points is the same as second-placed Borussia Dortmund, whose inferior goal difference keeps them behind heading into matchweek nine, whereas Mainz are ninth with 10 points.

Mainz's form has been dire, with the club losing four of their last five games in all competitions, including three of their past four league matches.

Manager Urs Fischer will be alarmed by the nature of his team's 4-1 defeat against Elversberg on October 30 given the victors led 3-0 by the interval and led 4-0 by the time they conceded in second-half stoppage time.

The club may be in ninth place, but only three points separate them from 16th-placed Union Berlin, who occupy the division's relegation playoff spot.

Matters will not get any easy for the hosts, who have to face the current top four in their next seven Bundesliga fixtures.

Die Nullfunfer are yet to win at home this season, with Mainz having taken one point from a possible 12 at Mewa Arena, and a loss on the weekend would be their fourth consecutive defeat at the stadium.

Bayern's clash is taking place at exactly the same time as Dortmund's with Elversberg, though they could have entered the weekend ahead of their title rivals on points had they beaten them at Allianz Arena on October 31.

The leaders were held to a 2-2 stalemate by BVB despite twice going ahead, but they conceded an 89th-minute equaliser to draw for the second time in four league games.

Vincent Kompany's side have now drawn four of their past eight matches in all competitions, a period in which they remained unbeaten, while they managed to win 2-0 against Atletico Madrid in the Champions League on Tuesday.

That clash was the Bavarians' eighth game of the campaign played on the road, and it was their fifth triumph in that time.

The club scored at least two goals in all but one of those matches, but it was also just their second clean sheet away from home this term.

Bayern's record against their hosts is predictably strong, with the visitors having won five of their past seven meetings with Mainz, only losing on one occasion.

Mainz will hope that centre-backs Danny da Costa, Fabio Gruber and Kacper Potulski are at their best, especially as they cannot call upon Dominik Kohr or Stefan Posch, the latter of whom is suspended after being sent off against Elversberg.

Using fewer than three midfielders in midfield could give far too much space to Bayern, so expect to see Jae-Sung Lee, Kaishu Sano and Nadiem Amiri start together on the weekend.

Bayern still have several key injuries in their squad, with forwards Luis Diaz (hamstring) and Serge Gnabry (hamstring) ruled out, while Jamal Musiala (hamstring) is a doubt.

There is no doubt about the selection of Harry Kane, who will be keen to celebrate his new contract with his fifth Bundesliga goal of the season, and he could be supported by an attacking cast of Michael Olise, Lennart Karl and Alphonso Davies.

Joshua Kimmich and Aleksandar Pavlovic are set to patrol the middle of the pitch ahead of centre-backs Dayot Upamecano and Kim Min-Jae.

As for the manager himself, Kompany is still taking an enforced break away from football following his collapse earlier in October that required a hospital visit, meaning assistant Rene Maric will lead the team once again.

Zentner; Da Costa, Gruber, Potulski; Caci, Lee, Sano, Amiri, Widmer; Tietz, Becker

Neuer; Laimer, Upamecano, Kim, Brown; Kimmich, Pavlovic; Olise, Karl, Davies; Kane

Bayern may have several injury issues to contend with, but Mainz have struggled considerably in recent weeks.

The visitors could enjoy a fruitful afternoon at Mewa Arena, and striker Kane will relish to opportunity to add to his tally.

#2232. Huddersfield Town vs Exeter City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Sam Varley | 05 Nov 2026 22:51

Aiming to continue an eight-match winning streak, Huddersfield Town will welcome Exeter City to the Accu Stadium on Saturday for an FA Cup first-round tie.

The hosts have climbed to the top of the League One with a hot streak, while their visitors saw an unbeaten run of their own come to an end last weekend and sit 12th in League Two.

Huddersfield Town will kick off their 2026-27 FA Cup bid at the weekend on the back of a strong run in League One.

The Terriers have failed to get past this stage in the previous two years, most recently losing to Bolton Wanderers last time around, but they do come in with plenty of confidence, currently sitting top of League One in their first full season under Martin Drury after falling short of the playoffs in their first two terms in the third tier.

In 14 league games since Drury was handed the reins permanently over the summer, the Yorkshire outfit have managed 29 points, winning eight and only losing one of those matches while scoring a league-high tally of 28 goals.

That solitary defeat came in mid-September, and the Terriers have since won six of eight games in all competitions and five in seven league outings while drawing the other two, most recently hosting fellow promotion-chasers Luton Town on the back of four straight triumphs in midweek and drawing 1-1 with thanks to Joe Taylor's second-half hat-trick.

Now turning their focus to the FA Cup, Huddersfield Town will hope to channel their league form into Saturday's first-round tie and continue their hot streak with another victory.

Their opponents, meanwhile, face a tough test in the first round having reached the fourth and third rounds in their prior two campaigns.

Exeter City enter this year's competition on the back of a mixed start to life back in League Two after relegation last term, having struggled to get up and running early on.

Matt Taylor's team earned just six points from their first eight games in England's fourth tier but reversed their fortunes in a near-perfect October, winning four and drawing one in a five-match run culminating in victories over Crewe Alexandra and Fleetwood Town to climb into the top half.

That run also came alongside an EFL Trophy tie against third-tier Oxford United, in which the Grecians drew and won the penalty shootout, but their unbeaten run came to an end at the weekend, visiting leaders Salford City and losing 2-1 having led through Sonny Cox and conceded second-half goals to Luke Garbutt and Will Aimson.

Still with plenty of optimism to take from their much-improved run in October, Exeter City will now bid to pull off an upset in Yorkshire to progress to the FA Cup second round.

Huddersfield Town form (all competitions):

After a busy and successful run in League One, Drury may opt to rotate his Huddersfield team at the weekend to rest some key men and afford opportunities for squad players, although Jack Whatmough, Herbie Kane, Marcus McGuane, Murray Wallace and Bojan Radulovic will likely remain sidelined by injuries.

Joe Taylor and summer signing Derensili Sanches Fernandes have both been pivotal in attack, with the former on 10 goals in 13 league appearances this term and the latter racking up seven goals and six assists in 14 league matches since his summer arrival, and they may both be handed a well-earned rest with Ashley Fletcher and Samson Tovide likely to come in.

The injuries leave the Terriers light in midfield, but David Kasumu can come in to relieve either Archie Collins or Cameron Humphreys, while Joe Low should return to the defence from the outset.

Exeter City are contending with a long injury list, as Jake Doyle-Hayes, Gwion Edwards, Ajay Matthews, Danny Rose and Daniel Watts sidelined.

Goals have been spread out across the squad this season with no one on more than two but six players tied on that number, including Cox who should again lead the attack after his opener against Salford.

Reece Cole may come in to support him alongside Anthony Scully and Taylor Perry, while Charlie Cummins a mainstay at the base of the midfield.

Huddersfield Town possible starting lineup:

Young; Low, Bowat, Roughan; Bronkhorst, Humphreys, Kasumu, Gooch; Ashia; Tovide, Fletcher

Bycroft; Samuel, Taylor, Fitzwater, Woodhouse; Kite, Cummins; Cole, Perry, Scully; Cox

Huddersfield Town are far more likely than their visitors to shuffle the pack on Saturday, but the Terriers have momentum and strength in depth, even despite their injuries, and should have more than enough to continue their strong run of form with squad players keen to impress.

#2231. Data estimates how much Cardiff City star Jack Moylan is worth ahead of potential January interest

Cardiff City have made a respectable start to life back in the Championship, although Brian Barry-Murphy’s side have struggled to find consistency in front of goal. Their problems in the final third have arguably left them lower down the table than their overall performances deserve.

However, those struggles have not affected summer recruit Jack Moylan. The Bluebirds decision to bring the attacking midfielder in from Lincoln City is already looking like an excellent piece of business.

The 25-year-old has quickly established himself as one of the Bluebirds’ most influential players, registering nine goals and three assists in his opening 14 Championship appearances. Moylan has also started every league fixture, highlighting just how important he has become to Barry-Murphy’s plans.

With Cardiff struggling towards the lower end of the table, his contributions have been particularly valuable. If he continues producing at this level, the Bluebirds may have to contend with growing interest in their summer signing when the January transfer window opens.

While the fee was reported as undisclosed upon Moylan’s announcement, reports have suggested that Cardiff City was an initial £3m which could rise to £5m.

The Bluebirds have tied Moylan down to a four-year deal running until the summer of 2028. With that in mind, if interest does arrive in January, they are in a strong position to ask for a hefty fee and profit.

However, the data suggests that Cardiff City may have overpaid to bring Moylan in this summer, which does not bode well for a potential sale. FootballTransfers’ estimated transfer value has the attacking-midfielder’s value at €2.2m, which is around £1.8m.

Off the current Cardiff City squad, Moylan has the second highest value with only Yousef Salech higher (€3.8m). Moylan’s value is also considerably lower than the only player who has more Championship goals then him Will Lankshear, who is valued at €20.7m.

A more direct comparison may be Blackburn Rovers striker Andri Gudjohnsen, who also has nine goals this campaign. His value is only €2.7m and much similar to Moylan’s, while being just a year younger.

Moylan’s statistics underline just how influential he has become for Cardiff City. His attacking numbers are particularly impressive, with 51 shots, 19 on target and 18 chances created.

He has also registered five big chances created, while his nine goals have come from an expected goals figure of 5.48, highlighting his clinical finishing and ability to outperform the underlying numbers.

The Irishman has delivered in some of Cardiff’s biggest performances, scoring twice in their 5-1 victory over Watford while impressing in a central striking role. He also scored a first-half hat-trick in the 3-3 draw with West Ham during the week.

Having progressed from Irish football to becoming a key figure in Lincoln’s League One title-winning campaign, Moylan has adapted impressively to the Championship.

#2230. Returning star makes 'up my game' admission amid striker battle

Joe Westley believes his battle for game time with fellow Dundee attackers will only benefit the Tayside club.

With just one defeat in the last five, the Dark Blues are in fifth place in the William Hill Premiership and the fourth-highest scorers, with Ashley Hay notching three in the last four games and Bradley Fink with four in the last six.

Westley missed three games with an ankle injury but replaced Fink in the 3-1 win over Kilmarnock last Wednesday and, ahead of the Tayside derby against St Johnstone at McDiarmid Park on Saturday, the 22-year-old, with five goals in all competitions this season, said: “I think it’s a healthy competition.

“Everyone wants to be playing football and be in the team. So when we’ve got Ash and Brad scoring, it means I need to up my game and score as many as I can when I’m given the chance because you know that when you’ve got the shirt, people are waiting to take it off you.

“It’s a healthy competition and I think we all drive each other to be the best versions of ourselves.

“I think when all the strikers are scoring and doing well, it’s good to be a part of an attack that’s doing well because you create for each other. If everyone’s firing and everyone’s doing well, then it increases your chance of getting goals and assisting as well.

“It’s a really good time to be an attacker at the club. I think it’s starting to click between everyone and whoever’s on up front has got a great understanding of what’s wanted from them. It gives them the best possible chance to score goals and win games.

“I think everything can come down to hard work. As a group everyone around the club works so hard day in, day out and we’re pushed in training, we’re pushed in the gym and I think we try and always gain the little one percent that we can every week.

“But also there’s the environment that the manager has created where everyone’s in it together. Everyone wants to do the hard yards for each other and I think the hard yards and the basics of football are things that can separate you from other teams in the league.

“I think so far this season we’ve been good at the basics and as long as we continue to do them we’ll be fine.”

#2229. Stuttgart vs Werder Bremen - prediction, team news, lineups

By Sebastian Sternik | 05 Nov 2026 23:00, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 09:25

Saturday night entertainment in the Bundesliga comes from the MHPArena as Stuttgart welcome a high-flying Werder Bremen side.

While the hosts look to bounce back from a disappointing Champions League defeat, the visitors will have their sights set on extending their unbeaten league run to eight games.

Stuttgart’s inconsistent start to the season continued on Tuesday night when Sebastian Hoeness and his men suffered a 2-0 Champions League defeat to Galatasaray.

That was the third defeat in six games for the Swabians, who also tripped up against Hamburger SV and Borussia Monchengladbach - results which leave them 11th in the Bundesliga table, with nine points from eight matches.

The majority of Stuttgart’s recent fixtures have come on the road, but their home form has offered little encouragement either, with the club failing to record a victory on their own turf since early September.

In reality, Stuttgart were never in the title conversation at the start of the season, though mounting successes over recent seasons have certainly lifted expectations.

However, with the Swabians sitting just two points above the relegation zone and nine adrift of the top two, pressure is beginning to mount on Hoeness and his players.

Trouble is, Stuttgart have only managed one win from five previous meetings with Werder Bremen. That sole success came last season, when the Swabians swept their rivals aside in a 4-0 romping.

Unbeaten in seven Bundesliga games and chasing a third successive league victory, Werder Bremen are undoubtedly the form team heading into Saturday's encounter.

Last weekend, Bremen comfortably dispatched Hoffenheim with a 2-0 victory, having beaten Mainz 05 a fortnight earlier thanks to a Niclas Fullkrug double.

Speaking of the current Bundesliga top scorer, the former West Ham man will be hoping to continue his momentum - especially when he goes head-to-head against his national team rival, Deniz Undav.

That adds an intriguing subplot to Saturday's contest, with Fullkrug potentially looking to prove Jurgen Klopp right after the national team coach selected the Bremen striker but left Undav out of his latest squad.

Another interesting angle concerns Bremen's away form, which has been considerably less impressive than their recent performances on home soil.

Daniel Thioune’s side have only managed two wins from six games on the road, and one of those was a DFB-Pokal victory over fifth-tier side Luneburger Hansa.

More concerning, though, is their defensive record on the road, having conceded 12 goals across their five away league trips.

Atakan Karazor is the big doubt for Stuttgart ahead of the weekend, with the midfielder struggling with a thigh injury.

According to Hoeness, a recent MRI scan revealed visible bleeding, meaning the 30-year-old is highly unlikely to start Saturday’s game.

Angelo Stiller, Karazor's usual midfield partner, has also been ruled out until the international break with an adductor problem, leaving the Swabians short of options in the middle of the park.

Justin Diehl (thigh), Leo Sauer (muscle), Josha Vagnoman (hamstring) and Dan-Axel Zagadou (hamstring) are also out of action.

As for Werder Bremen, the visitors will make the trip to Stuttgart without nine first-team players.

Experienced midfielder Mitchell Weiser became the latest addition to the injury list after picking up a hamstring problem against Hoffenheim.

Third-choice goalkeeper Markus Kolke was never in contention for a starting place on Saturday, though a training injury this week has left him unavailable anyway.

Felix Agu (muscle), Skelly Alvero (calf), Paul Erevbenagie (ankle), Salim Musah (hamstring), Moussa N'Diaye (groin), Jens Stage (thigh) and Keke Topp (knee) are all on the sidelines.

In more positive news, Niklas Stark could make his return after his name did not appear on the list of unavailable players during Thioune’s recent press conference.

Seimen; Jaquez, Chabot, Mittelstadt; Leweling, Spalt, Promel, Fuhrich; El Khannouss, Undav; Demirovic

Hein; Arthur, Pieper, Friedl, Deman; Reis, Regeer; Dinkci, Chuki, Grull; Fullkrug

Werder Bremen might have serious momentum under their wings, but their away form is a concern ahead of the trip to Stuttgart.

Two of the previous eight meetings between these sides have ended in 2-2 draws, and another stalemate would not be particularly surprising, especially given both teams' recent goalscoring form in the Bundesliga.

#2228. Eyupspor vs Kocaelispor - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Cole | 05 Nov 2026 23:09

Eyupspor will be looking to end their miserable run of league defeats when they welcome Kocaelispor to Esenyurt Necmi Kadioglu Stadium on Saturday in the Turkish Super Lig.

The hosts have lost seven consecutive league matches and sit bottom of the standings with just three points from their opening 10 fixtures, while the visitors occupy eighth place with 16 points after collecting five victories this season.

Eyupspor have endured a disastrous start to the campaign, losing nine of their 10 matches, with their latest league setback coming in a humiliating 5-1 defeat away to Amed SK.

However, Mustafa Dalci's side did secure a morale-boosting Turkish Cup victory in midweek, recording a 4-2 win away to lower-division side Fethiyespor on Wednesday.

The four goals they scored in that match were more than they have managed in the league all season, having found the net just three times, the lowest tally in the division, while their defensive problems are equally evident, with 28 goals conceded, the worst record in the competition.

Dalci has struggled to make an impact since replacing Ozhan Pulat earlier in the campaign, with the Turkish manager yet to record a league victory, while his team have scored just once and conceded 12 goals in the league under his leadership.

Their home performances have offered little encouragement either, having lost four of their five league matches at Esenyurt Necmi Kadioglu Stadium, although their only victory did come on home soil against Genclerbirligi.

Kocaelispor, meanwhile, have enjoyed a considerably better campaign and head into this fixture comfortably placed in the top half of the standings, having gone unbeaten in their last three matches across all competitions.

The visitors were held to a goalless draw by Caykur Rizespor in their most recent league outing, having previously beaten Mazidagi 3-0 in the Turkish Cup and Alanyaspor 2-1 in the league.

Selcuk Inan's side will be hoping to secure consecutive away victories for the first time since earning promotion to the top flight last season, and they will be confident of their chances given the struggles of their hosts.

Their away record is not flawless, having lost three of their five league trips, but two victories on the road suggest they have enough quality to take advantage of Eyupspor's defensive vulnerabilities.

The head-to-head record also favours the visitors, who have won their last three meetings with Eyupspor, including a 1-0 victory at Esenyurt Necmi Kadioglu Stadium in March 2026.

Eyupspor have one reported injury concern ahead of Saturday's encounter, with Talha Ulvan doubtful after picking up a muscle problem.

Yalcin Barasi is expected to retain his place as the central striker, with Leandro Pintor and Bilal Boutobba likely to provide support from the wider positions.

Kocaelispor, meanwhile, have more significant availability concerns, with Tanguy Zoukrou, Massadio Haidara and Bruno Petkovic all expected to miss out through injury.

Berkan Kutlu will be an important figure for the visitors after scoring four league goals this season, while Florian Aye and Goncalo Sousa should provide further attacking options.

Moldovan; El Yamiq, Jules, Yasar, Yavuz; Pintor, Yao, Costa, Akman, Demir; Barasi

Oztasdelen; Yildiz, Dijksteel, Ortakaya, Rivas; Susoho, Kutlu, Show; Baku, Aye, Sousa

Eyupspor's home advantage could offer some encouragement, particularly after their midweek cup victory, but their defensive record and lack of goals in the league remain major concerns.

With the hosts struggling to create chances and defend consistently, we expect Kocaelispor to exploit their opponents' weaknesses and claim all three points.

#2227. 19-year-old Southampton starlet showcasing exciting potential in European loan after early transfer interest

Southampton sent eight players out on loan this campaign. With a mixture of academy talents and players who are on their way out of St Mary’s, the Saints are seeing mixed success from the departed members of the squad.

Moses Sesay, Nicholas Oyekunle and Jay Robinson were the three teenagers to leave for the season. It’s the latter who Tonda Eckert will be monitoring closely after a breakout term last year in the Championship.

Robinson made 28 appearances for Southampton, scoring twice and providing an assist. Minutes were always going to be hard to come by for the 19-year-old this season. With a move to Monza secured, the attacker has plenty to prove in the Serie A.

Securing a loan move to Serie A is a positive sign of how much potential Robinson has to unlock. It’s safe to say that despite a slow start to life at Monza, the 19-year-old is becoming a key player for the Italian outfit.

The Southampton academy graduate had to wait until September to make his first start, and he impressed in a 1-0 win against Torino. Featuring in an attacking midfield role, he completed the most dribbles in the game with four and had nine touches inside the opposition’s box.

His first goal came in the next match, in which he completed 78% of his passes and was dispossessed only once. Former Saints boss Ivan Juric has shown full faith in Robinson, starting the teenager in three of the last four outings.

The 19-year-old’s best display came against Cagliari, where he scored once in a 4-0 win. Two out of his three dribbles were successful in the game, and he made three line-breaking passes. It has been an impressive loan stint for the youngster with Inter Milan registering their interest.

Southampton have the option to recall Robinson from his loan spell in January. However, given his promising spell so far, the Saints would be reckless to do so.

At left-wing, the South Coast outfit currently have the options of Leo Scienza and Sam Edozie, as well as Lewis Dobbin, who can play there too. Edozie is likely to depart in January, though, after putting in a transfer request late in the summer window.

This would ultimately leave Eckert’s side short of attackers in that position. However, they must avoid recalling Robinson to improve their depth. Even if Edozie were to leave, his exit would give Dobbin a better chance of breaking into the first team after just 409 minutes played.

Most importantly, it would do no favours to the 19-year-old’s development. The Southampton prospect has only recently become settled at Monza, and with his minutes increasing over the last couple of months, the South Coast outfit can’t afford to ruin his crucial playing time.

#2225. Celtic trigger Turkish TV meltdown as ‘rant of a lifetime’ takes bonkers turn

Celtic’s stunning Europa League comeback sent Turkish TV into meltdown as raging Besiktas pundits struggled to comprehend how their side had managed to lose in Glasgow.

Broadcaster Onur Yildiz absolutely savaged Martin O’Neill’s side and claimed they wouldn’t even finish in the TOP TEN in Turkey. And former Besiktas star Nihat Kahveci was hardly more complimentary, branding Celtic’s first-half display the worst he had ever seen from the Hoops and wondering how Besiktas possibly managed to turn such a comfortable night into defeat.

The Istanbul giants arrived in Glasgow unbeaten in the League Phase after a testing opening trio against Marseille, Hoffenheim and Crystal Palace. And for 45 minutes, they appeared to be cruising towards another positive result as Celtic struggled to lay a glove on them.

But the game was transformed after the break. The Hoops cranked up the tempo and deservedly came out on top to spark a furious post-mortem back in Turkey.

Read more: After missing out on another senior call-up, my Celtic form has been rewarded

And Yildiz led the inquest with an extraordinary rant which somehow stretched from Ottoman history to Vaclav Cerny’s infamous water bottle incident at Celtic Park.

Speaking on BTV, Yildiz fumed: “Samet, look. If Celtic played in our league, the Super Lig, and finished in the top ten, I’ll change my name.

“Look, Besiktas played against such a bad team today. During the first half, Nubel almost got pneumonia. The ball didn’t reach the man.

“So you take the lead in the second half. You’re saying it’s going to be like a tourist trip. A match you’ll win, and you’ll go home happy and cheerful. It’s like a friendly in an Austrian training camp.

“But something happens to Besiktas away from home, and suddenly the fans start tearing their hair out. Look. Corum, Kocaelispor, Kasimpasa. I dare say these are way, way better teams than Celtic.

“The guys came with a 0-3 record. They were coming to get their first point from us. They got their first win against us. So Besiktas played against such a bad team. It’s time we started talking about the shortcomings.

“So it’s over for Besiktas. The Tulip Period is over. First of all, Besiktas absolutely needed to sign a good central defender. The defence is falling apart. At the start of the season, Haric was the only kid who resisted. Apart from Emirhan, the defence is bad. Djalo is bad. Agbadou is bad. Besiktas conceded their first goal because of him.

“We were shouting from the rooftops that they needed a right-winger. There’s no right-winger either. Why did they keep Cerny on today? How did he last so long? He gets a bit more motivated in this game because of some past incidents.

“There’s an incident, I don’t know if you remember, Cerny was playing for Glasgow Rangers. Glasgow Rangers score a goal against Celtic, Cerny pulled a water bottle from the bench and sprayed it at the Celtic fans. That’s why he was booed every time the ball came to his feet.

“He was booed not only because he played for Rangers, but also because of that incident. But Besiktas’ flaws have now been revealed.”

#2223. Galatasaray vs Amed SK - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joshua Cole | 05 Nov 2026 23:10

Galatasaray will look to maintain their position at the top of the Turkish Super Lig when they welcome Amed SK, who will be hoping to leapfrog them in the standings, to RAMS Park on Saturday.

The Lions have collected 25 points from their opening 10 league matches but face a significant test against a side who sit second with 23 points and have lost just once in the competition this season.

Galatasaray head into this encounter in excellent form, having won their last five matches across all competitions since suffering a 4-1 home defeat to Barcelona in the Champions League.

Okan Buruk's side have responded impressively to that setback, recording domestic victories over Genclerbirligi and Fenerbahce before producing an entertaining 4-2 away win against Lille in Europe.

In their last league game, the Lions recorded a 4-0 victory over Konyaspor, scoring twice in each half to secure a comfortable result and maintain their position at the top of the Super Lig.

Galatasaray subsequently strengthened their confidence with a 2-0 Champions League victory over Stuttgart on Tuesday, giving them another important European result ahead of Saturday's domestic assignment.

That success also continued an impressive sequence at RAMS Park, where they have won five of their seven matches across all competitions, dropping points only against Corum in the league (2-2) and Barcelona in the Champions League.

The Istanbul giants will be facing the visitors in the top-flight for the first time, although they have faced each other twice in the Turkish Cup, winning one apiece, and they cannot afford to approach this fixture as a routine home assignment despite their opponents being a newly-promoted side.

Amed SK have emerged as one of the biggest surprises of the campaign, with the visitors flying high in their maiden Super Lig season and now presented with an opportunity to move top of the standings.

They have recorded seven league wins, drawn twice and lost just once, while also securing important victories over Besiktas and Trabzonspor, and their attacking output has been particularly impressive, with 25 goals making them the division's leading scorers ahead of Saturday's trip to Istanbul.

Their attacking confidence was evident in a 5-1 home victory over Eyupspor last time out, when they produced another convincing display to extend their winning run in the league to three matches and their unbeaten sequence to eight (W6, D2).

However, travelling to RAMS Park represents a different challenge, particularly given Amed's performances on the road this season, having recorded just one away win in four attempts, alongside two draws and one defeat.

Galatasaray will be without captain Abdulkerim Bardakci, who is suspended after receiving a red card against Fenerbahce, leaving Buruk to make an adjustment in central defence.

Davinson Sanchez is expected to retain his place, with Wilfried Singo likely to partner the Colombian, while Ismail Jakobs and Roland Sallai are options in the full-back positions.

Victor Osimhen should lead the attack after scoring nine league goals this season, with Yunus Akgun and Aleksey Batrakov expected to provide support in the attacking midfield positions.

Rafael Leao is also pushing for a starting place after featuring in recent fixtures, while Leroy Sane offers another option if Buruk decides to rotate following Tuesday's Champions League assignment.

Amed will be without Samuel Ballet, who is suspended for Saturday's encounter, while Dilhan Demir remains sidelined with a cruciate ligament injury.

Muhammed Hasan Yildirim is also a doubt with a muscle problem, leaving Besnik Hasi with some selection decisions ahead of the difficult trip to Istanbul.

Gift Orban will be the main attacking threat for the visitors after scoring nine league goals, matching Osimhen's tally.

Cakir; Sallai, Sanchez, Singo, Jakobs; Torreira, Lemina; Yunus Akgun, Batrakov, Leao; Osimhen

Lafont; Krasniqi, Yesil, Bates, Meras; Raveloson, Soyalp; Lutin, Saba, Sor; Orban

Amed's impressive attacking record and strong start to the campaign suggest they have enough quality to make this an uncomfortable afternoon for Galatasaray, but the home side's greater experience in high-pressure fixtures and impressive form at RAMS Park should ultimately see them claim all three points and preserve their position at the top of the table.

#2222. Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

Spain should withdraw as co-hosts of the 2030 World Cup if Morocco is awarded the right to stage the final, the European nation’s main opposition party leader has said.

The centenary finals are set to be co-hosted by Morocco, Spain and Portugal, with the opening three matches due to be staged in South America as a nod to Uruguay’s hosting of the inaugural tournament in 1930.

FIFA has not confirmed which of the three main co-hosts will be awarded the final, only saying that a decision will be taken in due course.

However, Alberto Nunez Feijoo, the leader of the opposition People’s Party, insists Spain should back out of hosting the tournament if it is not chosen to stage the final.

“If Morocco ends up hosting the final, Spain should leave the organisation (of the tournament),” he told El Chiringuito TV.

He added on X in a post quoting the El Chiringuito interview: “Restoring our country’s pride also means not letting anyone walk all over us.

“The World Cup final belongs to Spain. The world’s top footballing nation is not humiliated. I will defend my country’s interests. In this too.”

The revamped Bernabeu Stadium or Camp Nou would be worthy contenders to host, but Morocco are continuing to push for the Grande Stade Hassan II in Casablanca, with a projected capacity of 115,000, to stage the showpiece game.

Morocco is currently staging the Under-17 boy’s World Cup, held an emergency meeting of the FIFA management board in August when FIFA president Gianni Infantino looked under threat and will also be the venue for next year’s FIFA Congress, where Infantino is hoping to secure a fourth term as president.

#2221. Sky Germany: Krösche heading for Milan but compensation must be discussed – the situation

Sources in Germany are confirming that Markus Krösche should be announced by AC Milan in the coming weeks, ready to start in January.

It is seemingly not a very well-kept secret by this point that Milan are looking to appoint Krösche as their new Head of Football, having made an attempt to get him in the summer. Sources in Italy have claimed things are done, but their German colleagues have always been a bit more hesitant.

Now, Sky Germany confirm that Krösche’s future likely lies in Italy because a move to Milan is becoming increasingly likely. However, no deal has been finalised yet: officials from both clubs still need to hold talks as Krösche is only on leave from Frankfurt.

His contract, which runs until 2028, is currently still valid. The 46-year-old insisted on having a release clause included in his contract. Specifically, this means that any club wanting to hire him before the summer of 2027 will have to pay a fee of approximately €5-7m, or negotiate with Eintracht.

No advanced talks have yet taken place, but Sky do add that Krösche is expected to take on the role of Head of Football with Milan. A start date of January 1, 2027 – coinciding with the start of the winter transfer window – is being discussed.

Meanwhile, Eintracht Frankfurt have already settled the question of Krösche’s successor. Joti Chatzialexiou is coming from 1. FC Nürnberg and will take over the position of sporting director. So, it seems like just a matter of time.

#2220. Sporting Lisbon vs Moreirense - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adeyeye Oluwapelumi | 05 Nov 2026 23:19

Sporting Lisbon will be hoping to make a fresh start under new management when they welcome Moreirense to the Estadio Jose Alvalade on Saturday evening for their Liga Portugal Matchday 11 encounter.

The Lions sit third in the Primeira Liga table with 21 points from 10 matches, nine behind leaders Porto and four adrift of arch-rivals Benfica in second, while their visitors occupy ninth place.

Saturday's encounter marks the beginning of a new era at Sporting, with Paulo Fonseca set to take charge for the first time following his appointment as Rui Borges's successor, bringing an end to the latter's near two-year spell at the club on November 2 after a difficult run of results.

Borges departs having guided the Lions to the league title and Taca de Portugal triumph in 2024-25, but the club failed to add any silverware last season, while consecutive home defeats to LASK and Academico de Viseu contributed to his dismissal this term.

B-team coach Tiago Fernandes took temporary charge for the 1-0 Champions League victory over Shakhtar Donetsk in midweek before Fonseca was unveiled as Sporting's new head coach on November 4.

The former Lyon boss inherits a side that has won its last three matches across all competitions, having followed up a Taca da Liga victory over Maritimo and a 2-0 league success against Casa Pia with the midweek continental triumph.

With little room for further slip-ups if Sporting are to remain in the title race after finishing runners-up last season, Fonseca will be eager to collect maximum points on Saturday before turning his attention to the upcoming trip to leaders Porto on November 29.

The Lisbon outfit can also draw confidence from their recent record against Moreirense, having won seven of their last eight meetings (L1), including the last three, in which they scored exactly three goals on each occasion.

Moreirense, meanwhile, travel to the capital after recording consecutive league victories for the first time since January, having followed up their success at Estoril Praia with a 2-1 triumph over Estrela Amadora, courtesy of Nile John's stoppage-time winner.

Vasco Botelho da Costa will be pleased with his side's response after a three-match winless run across all competitions, which included a disappointing Taca de Portugal exit at the hands of lower-league AVS, alongside a league draw against Vitoria de Guimaraes and defeat to Gil Vicente.

Five points adrift of the European places, Os Verdes e Brancos know that taking points from difficult away fixtures against the likes of Sporting prove crucial in their pursuit of a historic continental qualification, although they have avoided defeat just once in their previous 15 league visits to the Lisbon club.

Moreirense have nevertheless been reasonably competitive on their travels this season, collecting seven points from a possible 15, with two victories in five away matches offering some encouragement ahead of Saturday's challenge.

Sporting remain without centre-back Zeno Debast and Nuno Santos, who are both recovering from injuries, although Rodrigo Zalazar has returned to action after making a second-half appearance in the Champions League victory over Shakhtar following a knee problem.

Interim boss Fernandes opted to deploy both centre-forward Fotis Ioannidis and Luis Suarez in attack, although it remains to be seen whether Fonseca will retain that approach for his first match in charge.

Suarez has now gone two consecutive appearances without scoring after finding the net in each of his previous 10 outings for Sporting, and the Colombian will be eager to rediscover his scoring touch this weekend.

For Moreirense, Francisco Domingues and Kiko Bondoso are expected to miss several weeks after sustaining muscle injuries in their left thighs.

Meanwhile, midfielder Joao Veloso and forward Alexandre Parsemain were left out of the travelling squad as they continue their respective recoveries from fitness problems, leaving Leonardo Vonic likely to lead the line once again.

Sporting Lisbon possible starting lineup:

Silva; Vagiannidis, Quaresma, Inacio, Araujo; Altimira, Doumbia; Catamo, Zalazar, L Guilherme; Suarez.

Ferreira; Pinto, Monteiro, Batista, Martinez; Liberato, John; Andrade, Mendonca, Landerson; Vonic

Sporting's managerial change adds an element of uncertainty, but the hosts still possess considerably more individual quality and should have enough to overcome a Moreirense side dealing with several injury absences.

The visitors have shown some improvement on their travels in recent weeks, although their poor record at the Jose Alvalade suggests that Fonseca could begin his tenure with a victory.

#2219. Milan’s Curva Sud taunt Inter’s Curva Nord after angry post-derby banner – photos

Inter’s ultras were not best pleased with the way their team played during the derby loss to AC Milan on Saturday, it would be fair to say.

All four goals came in the first half, with Christian Pulisic’s header and Adrien Rabiot’s flick from a corner putting Milan in dreamland less than 10 minutes in. Alessandro Bastoni halved the deficit, before Diego Moreira’s outstanding free-kick restored the two-goal lead.

As Calciomercato.it reveal, Inter’s ultras put up a banner after a 3-1 loss against Milan, criticising the players’ attitude during and after the game: “A derby lost without even playing. Not even the guts to say goodbye.”

#2218. Turki Alalshikh blame rejected as Derby County crisis deepens

It has been a nightmare start to the season for Derby County in the Championship.

After missing out on the play-offs on the final day of last season, Derby would have been hoping to challenge for promotion once again this campaign, but John Eustace's men currently find themselves at the wrong end of the table.

Derby owner David Clowes has made no secret of his desire to bring fresh investment into the club in recent years, and it looked as though a cash injection was imminent after Saudi billionaire Turki Alalshikh agreed to purchase a controlling stake in the club.

The proposed deal had been given approval by the EFL and the Independent Football Regulator, but Alalshikh unexpectedly withdrew from the takeover in August, claiming that, as the campaign had already started, he had "no time to prepare the team or the club for the season ahead".

While the Rams went on to make further signings after Alalshikh's takeover deal collapsed, including the permanent addition of midfielder David Ozoh for a fee of around £4 million, head coach John Eustace later claimed that the saga caused the club to lose "a lot of momentum on players that we were looking at".

After the off-field drama over the summer, Derby have made a disastrous start to the season on the pitch, and they head into the November international break sitting in the Championship relegation zone, with pressure building on Eustace as increasing numbers of supporters call for a managerial change.

When asked if Alalshikh's takeover falling through at the last minute is to blame for the club's poor start, FLW's Derby County fan pundit Shaun Woodward admitted that it is a contributing factor, as well as the recent injury crisis, but he insisted that a managerial change is the only way to get the Rams out of their current predicament.

"There is definitely an element of blame on Turki Alalshikh for pulling out so late in the day, but we can't be pinning it all on the takeover," Shaun told FLW.

"John Eustace still has a more than capable squad at his disposal.

"The majority of that squad was good enough to get us to an eighth-placed finish last season.

"Yes, it might have affected the transfers he had pencilled in, and we had to then go for plan B or plan C transfers, but the nucleus of that squad is still far too good to be in the position that we find ourselves in now.

"Yes, you can also blame injuries to our backline, but, again, the players that have stepped in should be doing better, they are still Championship players.

"They are not covering themselves in any glory whatsoever, and you can say the same about Eustace and his staff because they haven't been able to motivate the players, their tactics seem wrong, he doesn't sound very inspiring in his post-match interviews, and he looks defeated.

"The players look lost out on the pitch, but, all of that said, you can blame Eustace as much as you want, and he will lose his job because of this as the buck stops with him, the players have let him down massively as well.

"The last couple of performances especially at Millwall and at home to Norwich, the players have downed tools, they have given up.

"They are very much playing in individual mode, where they don't want to be made the scapegoat, they are hiding on the pitch, we can all see it as fans.

"No one is looking like they really care, and it's sad to see because there's still a nucleus of players out on that pitch that should be far better than 23rd in the league with the lowest goals scored and the highest goals conceded.

"We're not creating anything, we're gifting teams goal after goal, so I think there's an element of everything involved here.

"Yes, the takeover didn't help, maybe the transfers weren't the ones we wanted necessarily, but I still felt we had a good enough squad.

"Injuries haven't helped the backline, but the players that have come in haven't stepped up, and the more experienced players in our squad haven't led by example.

"It's just been a shambles, and, let's be real, the warning signs were there in pre-season, we looked lathargic and unfit, and that's carried on into the season.

"Until we get a new manager, I just can't see it changing, and, even then, it's going to be tough for someone to come in with the current group.

"The confidence is rock bottom, someone is going to have to come in and change formation, change personnel, rip up the script and start again because right now, we're only heading one way and that's League One."

While Alalshikh agreed a deal to invest in Derby and went through the EFL and IFR approval processes, Clowes and Eustace must have known that there was always a chance his takeover could collapse, particularly given that his previous interest in other Championship clubs, such as Bristol City and Millwall, had failed to progress.

Alalshikh's late withdrawal would inevitably have caused some disruption to the Rams' transfer plans, but Eustace still has a competitive squad at his disposal, which includes many of those who helped the club to an eighth-placed finish last season.

Can Turki Alalshikh's failed takeover be blamed for Derby County's poor start? Tell us in the comments below!

With that in mind, there is little excuse for Derby to be in their current position, even taking their extensive injury list into account, and it is difficult not to feel that Eustace will be fortunate to survive the upcoming international break.

#2217. Alverca vs Casa Pia - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adeyeye Oluwapelumi | 05 Nov 2026 23:29

Alverca will be looking to avoid a fourth consecutive defeat in all competitions when they welcome Casa Pia to the Complexo Desportivo FC Alverca on Saturday evening for their Liga Portugal Matchday 11 encounter.

Sergio Ferreira's side sit 10th in the Primeira Liga table, four points and six places above their visitors, who currently occupy one of the two outright relegation spots.

Alverca's second consecutive campaign in the Portuguese top flight has been characterised by spells of inconsistency, with a three-match winning run against Nacional, Rio Ave and Famalicao between mid-September and October sandwiched between winless periods.

Ferreira's men had failed to win any of their opening five fixtures before finding their feet, but they have since returned to familiar struggles after losing their last three outings without scoring, beginning with a Taca de Portugal elimination at the hands of lower-league Tondela before suffering successive 1-0 league losses to Arouca and Benfica.

Looking to avoid a third consecutive defeat in the top-flight for the first time since December 2025, the Ribatejo outfit must also find greater balance at both ends of the pitch, having scored 13 goals and conceded 14 in 10 Primeira Liga matches, with multiple strikes coming just once and only one clean sheet recorded during that period.

Alverca’s home form has offered little encouragement either, as Ferreira's side have managed just one victory in five matches on their own turf this season (D2, L2), with their most recent outing at the venue ending in defeat to Arouca.

The hosts can nevertheless draw some confidence from previous results in this fixture, having won both league meetings with the Geese last season, recording a 2-0 victory in Rio Maior before following it up with a 3-1 success on home soil in April.

Casa Pia find themselves in a similarly difficult position after finishing 16th last season and securing their top-flight status through the relegation playoff, with their disappointing start to the current campaign largely down to shortcomings at both ends of the pitch.

Filipe Coelho's men have scored a joint-league-low five goals in the Primeira Liga this term, while only Maritimo (20) and Rio Ave (23) have conceded more than Os Gansos' 19, with their lack of cutting edge in attack once again evident in last weekend's 2-0 defeat to Sporting Lisbon.

There are, however, signs of improvement compared to their opening six fixtures, during which the Geese went without a victory, having now won two of their last four league matches and three of their most recent five across all competitions, including a Taca de Portugal triumph over lower-league Alpendorada.

A return to the road this weekend could also work in the visitors' favour, considering all seven of their league points this season have come away from home, with those results arriving across their last three trips, including a 2-0 victory over Estrela Amadora on their most recent outing.

Alverca remain without Bastien Meupiyou and Sabit Abdulai, who are both sidelined through injury, while goalkeeper Jhonatan Luiz and midfielder Rayan Lucas are doubts after completing modified training sessions earlier this week.

Steven Baseya and Andre Vidigal are available again after serving their respective suspensions, providing Ferreira with two additional options as he considers his selection for Saturday's encounter.

For Casa Pia, goalkeeper Andre Gomes appears to be the only confirmed absentee, while Jeremy Livolant is pushing for a starting place after playing 45 minutes against Sporting.

David Sousa is also one booking away from suspension, having collected four yellow cards this season, and another caution on Saturday would rule him out of the Taca de Portugal fourth-round meeting with Atletico CP on November 21.

Henrique Araujo could retain his place as the central striker, although Coelho also has Alasanna Jatta and veteran forward Cassiano available as alternative options.

Mendes; Touaizi, Gomez, Baseya, James, Chissumba; Chiquinho, Luccas, D Gui, Figueiredo; Camara

Sequeira; Geraldes, Kaly, Sousa, Rosas; Perez, Ofori; Boukammiri, Rego, Pauwels; Araujo

Alverca have struggled to find the net in recent weeks, while their home record offers little reason to expect a convincing response, particularly against opponents who have collected all their league points on their travels this season.

Casa Pia have also lacked consistency in front of goal, but their recent away performances suggest that they are capable of making life difficult for the hosts, and a closely fought draw could be on the cards.

#2216. Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

Thomas Tuchel says Cole Palmer is paying the price for his controversial withdrawal from England’s last camp.

The Chelsea forward was a notable omission from Tuchel’s squad for next week’s Nations League games against Croatia and Spain.

He was originally named in Tuchel’s group for September and October’s matches but withdrew due to a minor injury, despite playing 90 minutes in Chelsea’s final match, much to the annoyance of the German, who said he “misses too many opportunities to impress”.

The 24-year-old then featured in all four of his club’s Premier League matches ahead of this camp, but was overlooked by Tuchel, who handed maiden call-ups to Jobe Bellingham and Josh King.

“I guess everyone is desperate to play for England, and Cole is no exception,” Tuchel said.

“We’re speaking now about the player who is not in camp, and it counts when you are in camp.

“And I said last time, he misses too many opportunities to impress. It was a long camp. It was two and a half weeks, and the players and the boys who were in camp built something extraordinary and that’s why the credit goes to them.

“The trust is for them. If you are in camp, this is your chance to impress.

“So it’s not personal, and of course we will never assume that Cole is not in the mindset to come and desperate to play for his country. But the competition is on, and the players who were in camp did an amazing job and kept their place.

“The door is always open, but nothing has changed, and we decided to do this camp without him.”

Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

Returning star makes 'up my game' admission amid striker battle

Aberdeen suffer major double blow as Stephen Robinson confirms injury lay-offs

Palmer was one of five withdrawals ahead of the last camp, with Declan Rice, Kobbie Mainoo, Tino Livramento and Marcus Rashford also pulling out.

Rice is the only one of that quintet recalled as Tuchel reiterated that he will always give preference to those who “walk the talk”.

“The best reactions to the withdrawals in the last camp from the players who were in camp was just to put the quality in and make it a successful camp,” Tuchel said.

“That’s the best foundation for the next camp, to have less withdrawals, which is actually happening now.

“So it’s always about the ones who are in camp.

“It’s always the ones who don’t withdraw and who walk the talk and come to camp and show that it matters and put the quality in. And this is always what we will reward.

“That was always the message, very, very early, since we took charge, that it matters if you are in camp and it matters what you do in camp and the last camp was an excellent one, so the trust and the belief is in the players that were there.”

Mainoo was also overlooked, despite returning to Manchester United’s side.

“I think Kobbie had a difficult spell now with his team and his club, and missed a lot, a lot of matches,” Tuchel said.

“And in the meantime, it was Jobe who impressed in the same level of time, and that’s exactly what we want.

“The door is always open for younger ones to overtake or to make their mark.”

#2215. RB Leipzig vs Augsburg - prediction, team news, lineups

By Anthony Nolan | 05 Nov 2026 23:32, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 05:12

Desperate to end their Bundesliga slump, Augsburg will travel to face RB Leipzig at Red Bull Arena on Saturday.

Die Roten Bullen are hoping to bounce back from their midweek defeat, with Die Fuggerstadter winless in six league games.

Martin Demichelis's Leipzig currently sit fourth in the German top flight with 15 points to their name, just three shy of Borussia Dortmund and leaders Bayern Munich.

The Saxons have also been flawless in the league at Red Bull Arena, where they are yet to concede in domestic competition, not to mention that only Bayern have bettered their 22-goal tally in the Bundesliga so far.

However, Demichelis is facing intense criticism from the media in Leipzig, who have directly questioned the manager's position, prompting him to state that if he does not achieve the club's aims then he will leave of his own accord at the end of the season.

Former Roten Bullen boss Marco Rose - now of Bournemouth - also spoke out about the level of pressure on Demichelis, noting that, "If you know the media landscape a bit, you already get the feeling that the thumbs are down."

The Cherries' head coach then went on to reveal how the end of his tenure at Red Bull Arena "Did something" to him, and caused him to lose a bit of his "Lightness."

Last time out, Leipzig were beaten 3-2 by Manchester City in a Champions League thriller, bringing a three-game winning streak to an end, and marking the first time the hosts conceded on home turf this season.

Victory this weekend would be a fourth in five matches for Demichelis, and may even put Die Roten Bullen level with Dortmund and Bayern, though another defeat is likely to trigger a new wave of criticism.

Meanwhile, Manuel Baum's Augsburg kicked off their season in strong fashion with back-to-back triumphs, but have since endured a dire winless run in the Bundesliga.

Die Fuggerstatder last took all three points by thrashing Eintracht Frankfurt 4-1 on September 6, losing three and drawing three of their league games since.

The only bright spot during that stretch was a 4-2 DFB-Pokal success against Grossaspach, though it was followed up by a 2-1 defeat to Freiburg at WWK Arena just four days later.

Heading into Saturday's showdown, Augsburg are 10th in the Bundesliga with nine points, putting them just one shy of the top half but level with every club down to 15th-placed Hamburger SV, and only two points above Union Berlin in the relegation playoff spot.

This weekend's visitors will arrive as the underdogs, but the more optimistic amongst the travelling support could take heart from the fact Baum's side have earned three of their four wins across all competitions away from home.

Leipzig will be without striker Marc Guiu once again as he recovers from a hamstring injury, while winger Antonio Nusa is a doubt after being substituted against Man City as a precautionary measure, with manager Demichelis saying that the 21-year-old "Was close to getting injured."

Nusa could start on the left of the Saxons' attack this weekend, but if he is not ready to feature, then Tidiam Gomis could take his place in the XI.

Elsewhere, left-back Max Finkgrafe is a doubt with a thigh issue, and midfielder Assan Ouedraogo remains sidelined due to a shoulder injury.

In their absence, David Raum should start on the left of the Saxons' defence, while Neil El Aynaoui and Rocco Reitz operate in Leipzig's double pivot.

As for Augsburg, they are light at the back given that centre-halves Noahkai Banks (knee), Calvin Brackelmann (concussion) and Abdulai Juma Bah (suspension) are all unavailable.

Filling the gaps, Baum could opt for Jeffrey Gouweleeuw, Yannik Keitel and Chrislain Matsima to form his defence, with Robin Fellhauer and Hennes Behrens as wing-backs.

Vandevoordt; Henrichs, Orban, Lukeba, Raum; El Aynaoui, Reitz; Gomis, Nkunku, Nusa; Romulo

Dahmen; Gouweleeuw, Keitel, Matsima; Fellhauer, Massengo, Rieder, Behrens; Kade, Ibrahimovic; Gregoritsch

Leipzig may have been beaten by Man City in midweek, but their record at Red Bull Arena speaks for itself, and they should be able to get the better of their struggling opponents on Saturday.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

It was a moment filled with emotion for all the fans present who chanted his name, but above all it was a moving moment for Kakà himself, who couldn’t hold back the tears as a montage video played on the screen.

“Milan is a second home to me: that’s the word that comes to mind when I think of this city. I’ve been truly fortunate to have so many great champions at my side, at AC Milan, in the national team, in Madrid: I’ve played with some truly brilliant players.

#2212. DC United vs New York City FC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adepoju Marvellous | 05 Nov 2026 23:38, Last updated: 05 Nov 2026 23:44

DC United and New York City FC bring the curtain down on their respective 2026 Major League Soccer campaigns with Saturday's clash at Audi Field.

With playoff football out of reach for both sides, there is little at stake, but the chance to finish the regular season on a high should ensure a respectable level of intent.

After reaching the postseason in each of the last two years, New York City have only themselves to blame for missing out this term, following a dismal run of just two wins from 15 outings since returning from Leagues Cup duty.

Pascal Jansen’s men would have gone into their season finale still in playoff contention with a win over Montreal - a result they looked set to secure thanks to Andres Perea’s opener - before Daniel Rios stunned Citi Field with a 91st-minute equaliser.

That was the 12th of 45 goals conceded by the Pigeons between the 75th minute and full time, a lack of late-game focus that Jansen must address next season, should he remain in charge.

While largely inconsequential, a win over DC United could marginally bolster the 57-year-old’s prospects of staying in the dugout, but his record against Saturday’s opposite number is poor, with two defeats from their previous two managerial head-to-heads.

Having lost each of the last two editions - one to this weekend’s opponents - NYCFC’s recent record in season finales is underwhelming as this weekend’s visitors seek to hand DC United a first defeat in five meetings.

As DC United prepare to close out their 2026 MLS campaign, they will hope never to endure another season as disappointing as this one, which easily ranks among the worst in the club’s history since returning to the top flight in 2008.

February’s 1-0 triumph over Philadelphia Union was expected to signal the start of something special for Rene Weiler’s men, but they have since suffered a downward spiral - not quite as severe as last season’s bottom-place finish in the Eastern Conference, but still disappointing.

Saturday’s 3-0 defeat to Columbus Crew marked a fifth consecutive loss and a 14th in 33 matches for Rene Weiler’s side, who have managed just seven wins this term, the second-fewest in the division, ahead of only Montreal’s tally of six.

With just 39 goals scored, the Eagles have been largely ineffective at the top end of the pitch despite Tai Baribo’s impressive return of 16 goals in 25 matches, without which things would have looked much worse from an attacking perspective.

Weiler’s 0.95 points per game in charge of DC United is his lowest average across all clubs managed since 2019. While his position appears safe for now, the Swiss coach will already be planning a turnaround next season, and avoiding defeat on Saturday would at least offer a positive note to end an otherwise disastrous campaign.

New York City FC Major League Soccer form:

Hosei Kijima and Louis Munteanu remain sidelined with leg injuries, while Gabriel Segal is unavailable for DC United due to an ankle problem.

First-choice goalkeeper Sean Johnson (knee) remains out, so Alex Bono should continue between the posts.

Top scorer Tai Baribo will not have the chance to add to his 16 goals due to a hip problem, rounding out the hosts’ list of absentees alongside Kye Rowles, who misses out through suspension.

New York City’s Australian duo Kai Trewin and Aiden O’Neill are out with ankle injuries, while fellow midfielder Maxi Moralez (knee) continues his spell on the treatment table.

Nineteen-year-old Drew Baiera has seen his debut campaign disrupted by injuries and will not feature this weekend, joining Arnau Farnos (leg) and Alonso Martinez (thigh) on the sidelines.

Bono; Kurokawa, Nealis, Bartlett, Hefti; Stroud, Dozzell, Peltola, Nono; Peglow, Ordaz

New York City FC possible starting lineup:

Freese; Gray, Martins, Sands, O’Toole; Shore, Perea; Ojeda, Fernandez, Traore; Magno

DC United have had the upper hand in this fixture recently, but their poor form will give New York City plenty of encouragement to travel to Washington in search of all three points.

An entertaining contest with both sides going full throttle is expected, but we are backing Jansen’s men to come out on top.

#2211. Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team

Neil McCann will continue his drive to see Kilmarnock “flourish” in the face of growing fan scrutiny.

The Ayrshire club are one point ahead of William Hill Premiership bottom side Hibernian, with nine points from 36 and having conceded 28 goals from 12 games amid concern and disgruntlement among a section of the Killie support.

McCann is preparing his players to take on a Celtic team who are four points ahead of Rangers at the top of the table, having played a game more and on the back of a morale-boosting 3-2 Europa League win over Besiktas on Thursday night

However, the Kilmarnock boss remains positive, and said: “Supporters will always give their opinion when they come into the stadium and they’ll voice it. They’re entitled to voice their opinion.

“My pressure, to be honest with you, is preparing our players tactically, physically, mentally.

Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission Spain told to walk away from 2030 World Cup if Morocco gets final

“And then when they take the field, I give them full autonomy to go and carry out all of those instructions, but play with the freedom and bravery that got us out of trouble last year.

“Nothing’s changed in my build-up. Nothing’s changed in my drive. Nothing’s changed in my approach to getting Kilmarnock to be a team that wants to put on a product on the pitch.

“A lot’s been made of ‘you can just win games and it’s all about winning games’. And I absolutely get that when it comes to management.

“Of course, it’s about results, very much, it’s a results-driven business. But I was given this job to get us out of bother last year.

“And we came in, the first thing wasn’t about, yes, results were important, but it was making sure that the team were capable and prepared to go and get those results.

“I think we are prepared to go and get those results. We just haven’t given our support enough good days to celebrate wins.

“But the work doesn’t stop. And it doesn’t deter me one bit from the work that I do on the training field.

“I’ll hopefully see a team flourish, get the results that I think their performances deserve. And then we can all come together with the unity that we showed last season and hopefully Kilmarnock will start climbing the table.”

#2208. Nacional vs Academico de Viseu - prediction, team news, lineups

By Adeyeye Oluwapelumi | 05 Nov 2026 23:44

Academico de Viseu will be out to secure their seventh league victory on the trot when they travel to Estadio da Madeira on Saturday to face a Nacional side still searching for stability following another managerial change.

The visitors sit sixth in the Liga Portugal table, just below the continental places on goal difference, while that same metric has the hosts 15th and only just clear of the relegation zone.

Nacional will hope that their third managerial appointment of the season can provide the stability needed to climb away from the lower reaches of the table following the arrival of Joao Henriques on October 26, after Alexandre Santos' brief spell in charge.

Santos suffered defeat in all four of his matches at the helm, including a shock Taca de Portugal exit to lower-league Sanjoanense, before Under-19s manager Bruno Abreu took charge and guided the side to a 1-0 victory over Braga, ending their six-match losing run in the league.

However, Henriques was unable to build on that result in his first game in charge, with the Alvinegros suffering a narrow 1-0 defeat away to Arouca, leaving the Funchal outfit with just two wins from their opening 10 Primeira Liga fixtures (D1, L7).

Both victories have come at Estadio da Madeira, offering some encouragement ahead of Saturday's encounter, knowing that taking maximum points at the weekend could hinge on defensive solidity, given their successes this term only came in games they kept clean sheets.

Supporters of the Alvinegros can also take some comfort from their side's record against Viseu in Funchal, having won three of the previous five meetings between the clubs (D1, L1) there, although their most recent home encounter ended in a 1-1 draw in Liga 2 in March 2024.

Viseu, meanwhile, have enjoyed an impressive start to life back in the top flight after failing to win any of their opening four fixtures, although an encouraging draw away to Benfica on matchday one offered an early indication of their potential.

Bruno Pinheiro's side have since recorded victories in each of their last six league matches, beating Gil Vicente, Vitoria de Guimaraes, Estrela Amadora, Estoril Praia and Sporting Lisbon before securing a 2-1 comeback triumph over Rio Ave last weekend.

That result means Academico have scored exactly twice in each of their last five top-flight outings, taking their league tally to 15 goals, with only the traditional big three of Porto (23), Benfica (27) and Sporting (23) finding the net more often.

Viseu have also alternated between league victories and cup defeats across their last four matches in all competitions, having been eliminated from the Taca de Portugal by lower-league Fatima before suffering Taca da Liga disappointment against Porto.

Pinheiro's men have also proved difficult to beat on the road, remaining one of only four sides yet to lose on their travels in the division, with three victories and two draws from five outings making them the league's fifth-best away team.

Academico de Viseu form (all competitions):

Nacional are expected to remain without long-term absentees Leo Santos, who is recovering from a knee injury, and Ulisses Rocha, who is sidelined with a broken ankle.

Brazilian duo Wesley Gasolina and Liziero, along with Filipe Soares, are all making progress in their respective recoveries, but Saturday's encounter is expected to come too soon for the trio.

Henriques could stick with a similar lineup to the one deployed in his first game in charge, with Abdel Njoya again expected to lead the attack as he looks to add to his two Primeira Liga goals this season.

Viseu are expected to have an almost clean bill of health for the trip to Madeira, although Igor Milioransa has not featured in a matchday squad since gameweek two and could miss out once again.

Andre Clovis provided the assist for Soufiane Messeguem's equaliser before scoring the winner against Rio Ave, and the striker will be keen to add to his five league goals when he leads the visitors' attack this weekend.

Loftus Cheek and Musah have been given three/four seasons of chances. Camarda rarely gets more than 10 minutes off the bench. I’m worried he goes the path of De Ketelaere and Liberali, develops and never comes back.

Italian clubs won’t play him. He should go to the championship in England. That will toughen him up.

Obviously the best choice for him. And it was obvious also in the summer. Strange decision from all parties.

Btw. Lontani, who played with Camarda in the Primavera looks much more mature in Parma. These things are hard to predict when they are young.

I say, give him Ferencvaros and Genoa and then make a call – Ramos has only scored twice in the league (1 pen) and a goal against Venezia in 890 mins! 75mil+ for that return deserves a turn on the bench and practice ground.

Loans don’t guarantee playing time or minutes. Look at Odogu. 0 minutes on the pitch after being loaned out to Toulouse.

Camarda will see even less of the pitch if they already have an established team and hierarchy at the club they loan him out to. Only desperate teams would then bring him in to play him (hoping to improve on bad results). It would be like Lecce all over again.

#2205. Ex-Celtic star tells Martin O'Neill why he's on receiving end of 'flak with love'

Joe Hart has told Martin O'Neill why he is getting "flak with love" amid Celtic's stuttering form.

Scrutiny at the club is growing and growing after a draw with Dundee United was followed up by defeat to Falkirk. Previously, the domestic form had been almost flawless, but for losing to Rangers.

Fans are now concerned their lead at the top of the table is precarious and are questioning the direction of the club even more.

Former Parkhead goalkeeper Hart says that scrutiny is always going to be there and it does not affect O'Neill's standing with the support.

He told The Overlap: "It’s flak with love, and that doesn’t make any sense. Of course, we know it doesn’t make any sense, but Martin O’Neill will be, and is, absolutely adored. But he knows what he signed up for as a Celtic manager, and he would expect to be getting a lot of questions asked about him.

Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

And that’s not saying he’s not up to the task or he’s not the man for the job, but that’s what comes with the territory. If you really understand Celtic, you understand that if you’re not borderline perfect domestically and challenging in Europe, you’re going to get it.

"You’re going to get it because they care and they’re intelligent football fans. And, there’s a massive disharmony with the board. The fans really don’t understand what the direction of the club is. And obviously the board have got their opinions in terms of the success that they had and continue to have.

So, it is difficult. That was really shining brightly mid-season last season, but then Martin came back in and the team started to win and it goes away. It’s amazing what winning can do, and at the moment they’re not.

"They had a difficult result at Falkirk. I think they drew at home to Dundee United. And then this Besiktas game felt ominous. It felt like there was going to be a riot, or not even a riot. Do you know what the worst feeling you can have in a stadium is? And I very rarely felt it up there. It wasn’t anger, it was people going, ‘Pfft, we lost again.’ That is the worst feeling you could have.

"You’d rather have someone that impassioned, that embroiled as a Celtic fan. Like I say, domestically, I lost very, very few games, but no matter what run you’d been on, you could feel it. It wasn’t the majority, but you’d feel some people’s whole life and whole livelihood is proudly Celtic.

"And if you don’t perform in that shirt, you’re going to get it. I’ve still got friends up there, and they were flat going into the game. If Celtic had lost, they wouldn’t have been angry, they’d have been like, ‘Oh, God.’ And that’s not a nice feeling. I talked about it for Michael [Carrick], but I think it’s a little bit different up there, because they’re still top of the league.

"If they can take the atmosphere that they generated towards the end of the game last night into the league game this weekend, I think it’s Kilmarnock, then it could be a massive spark to go again.”

#2204. Preston North End vs Southampton - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 05 Nov 2026 23:59, Last updated: 07 Nov 2026 11:10

Southampton will look to extend their six-game unbeaten run when they travel to Deepdale for Saturday’s clash with Preston North End.

The Saints are currently operating in seventh spot in the Championship standings, while PNE are propping up the table after taking just five points from their 14 matches.

Preston boss Graham Alexander has failed to spark an upturn in fortunes since being named as Paul Heckingbottom’s successor at the end of September.

Alexander has lost five of his six matches in charge (D1), including a 2-0 defeat in last Saturday’s away clash against Stoke City.

PNE experienced more disappointment in midweek, despite taking an early lead in their home meeting with Sheffield United.

Sydie Peck’s 19-minute hat-trick left Preston trailing by two goals at half time, and while Callum Lang pulled a goal back in the 77th minute, PNE ultimately failed to find an equaliser as they fell to their 11th Championship defeat of the season.

As a result of their struggles, Preston are already nine points adrift from safety, leaving them with a mountain to climb if they are to extend their 12-season stay in the second tier.

Alexander will be aware that his team needs to improve significantly in both boxes, as Preston currently have the league’s worst defensive record with 30 goals conceded and are among the division’s lowest scorers with just 12 goals to their name.

In contrast to their hosts, Southampton have proven to be a tough team to beat this term, having won eight, drawn four and lost just two of their 14 Championship matches.

In fact, Tonda Eckert's side have put together a six-game unbeaten run since suffering their most recent defeat in an away clash against Wrexham on September 19.

Samuel Edozie made sure Southampton extended that unbeaten streak in Tuesday’s meeting with West Bromwich Albion, netting a 53rd-minute equaliser to cancel out Isaac Price’s penalty at The Hawthorns.

The promotion hopefuls, who were deducted four points at the start of the season, are currently two points clear of Sheffield United in ninth position and three points adrift of second-placed Wolverhampton Wanderers.

They will certainly fancy their chances of adding three points to their tally against a struggling Preston side, especially as they have won on two of their previous three visits to Deepdale, including a 3-1 victory in May.

That said, they have struggled for consistency on their travels, having won two, drawn three and lost two of their seven away league games this season.

Preston are still having to contend without the services of Harry Clarke, Lewis Gibson, Liam Gibbs and Ali McCann.

Johnny Kenny has been added to the injury list after he was forced off in the 12th minute against Sheffield United due to a hamstring problem.

Midfielder Leo Leroy will miss Saturday’s game through suspension after collecting his fifth yellow card of the season in the midweek defeat.

As for the visitors, midfielder Caspar Jandar will have to be assessed after sitting out the last two games with a muscular injury.

Southampton’s top scorer Cyle Larin is another fitness concern, having been forced to miss the last three games with a hamstring problem.

Eckert, meanwhile, is confident Leo Scienza will be ready to feature at Deepdale after being ruled out for the midweek draw with West Brom.

Preston North End possible starting lineup:

Iversen; Offiah, Lindsay, Hughes; Mills, Potts, Thompson, Wiley; Lang, Devine; Erabi

Peretz; Bree, Stephens, Schlotterbeck, Manning; Ward-Prowse, Downes; Matsuki, Azaz, Scienza; Dobbin

While Southampton have tended to save their best performances for St Mary's, they will view Saturday's contest as a great opportunity to claim their third away win of the Championship season.

With the Saints currently the league's third-highest scorers, we ultimately believe they will be too strong for a Preston side with the worst defensive record in the second tier.
