#2203. Sheffield Wednesday star could 'easily depart' to allow Wrexham transfer - Ipswich Town will watch

Sheffield Wednesday went through a massive overhaul of their first team squad during the summer transfer window.

The Owls completed 18 deals in the aftermath of Arise Capital finalising their takeover of the club at the beginning of May.

One of the final deals that they completed was the loan of Davis Keillor-Dunn from Wrexham, with the forward arriving until January 2027.

The 29-year-old has contributed three goals and one assist from his first nine appearances in League One.

However, if the Yorkshire outfit want to keep him on loan for the remainder of the term, then it may require some reshuffling of the squad and their other loan options due to the terms of the business plan agreed to with the EFL.

Sheffield Wednesday’s other loan options include Harry Gray, Mason Burstow, Ali Al-Hamadi, Kieran Morrison and Jaden Heskey.

When asked which loan should be terminated to make room to keep Keillor-Dunn for the second half of the season, FLW’s Sheffield Wednesday fan pundit Patrick McKenna claimed that it’s between Ali Al-Hamadi and Mason Burstow.

However, the latter has started to perform well in the last few weeks, which has put Al-Hamadi’s position at Hillsborough under significant threat, if an exit will be necessary to retain Keillor-Dunn.

“Looking at the loan players we have, I think at the minute the choice I would make would be Al-Hamadi,” McKenna told Football League World.

Sam Smith is set to miss the rest of the season, which could lead Wrexham to keep Davis Keillor-Dunn after his loan spell at Sheffield Wednesday ends

#2201. Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to

Mika Baur is enjoying the demands of being a Celtic player – and was delighted to see the team display their fighting spirit and belief as they broke their Europa League duck.

The 22-year-old impressed as Celtic came from behind to beat Besiktas 3-2 on Thursday and get off the mark in the Europa League at the fourth time of asking.

The Hoops were heading for a seventh defeat in 11 matches when they fell behind early in the second half but the introduction of Callum Osmand, Camilo Duran and Colby Donovan on the hour mark sparked the team into life.

Manager Martin O’Neill recently admitted that some new players were still adapting to the demands of playing for Celtic but Baur and Duran have quickly proved they were successful summer signings.

Baur arrived from Paderborn after helping them to promotion to the Bundesliga and made his Germany debut last month, but moving to Scotland has been a new experience.

Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team Cole Palmer pays England price as Thomas Tuchel explains ruthless omission

“To play every three days is different for me,” the midfielder said. “I think I have played the same amount of games now that I had last season by the winter break.

“But I like it. That’s what you want. You want to play all the time, more playing than training. I am really enjoying it.

“And I think the pressure to win is a good kind of pressure. It’s better than (trying) not to lose. So I really enjoy it.”

Baur has shown pace and skill on the ball but his pressing in the final stages drew massive appreciation from the crowd on Thursday.

“First of all, I want to work hard for the team, for myself, that I can prove myself and I can play on the stage, help the team, put in a shift, hard work,” he said.

“That’s what I have to do first. And then after this, the skill comes, playing with the ball. But first of all, it’s showing heart on the field. I was really happy that it was appreciated.”

The win over the Turkish side felt like it could be a major step in Celtic’s season as they bounced back from losing the opening goal and from a late equaliser, with Duran netting the winner seconds later.

“The second half was pure heart, pure fight,” Baur said. “I’m not here for a long time, but I think that’s what it’s all about. This win feels unbelievable because we deserved it. We fought for it.

“The team is really, really close together. We know that we had a tough couple of weeks, but you can feel it in the training sessions, how the team is working together, the feeling, the believing is always there. And it was there on the pitch. That’s what we have to show week by week, every three days.

“It was not just our win. The atmosphere was great. I’m really, really thankful for every supporter in the stadium. They gave us a lot of energy. We all fight together. That makes me happy.”

#2200. Manchester United player ratings: United failed to win despite advantage

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United and Tottenham Hotspur returned from the international break with plenty to prove after a disastrous start to the season.

United arrived with just one win, while Tottenham came to Old Trafford without a win.

Both teams had half-chances in the first half but little to show for them. Tottenham had the better chances with chances for Dominic Solanke, Connor Gallagher and Kudus, but none could take advantage of them.

Manchester United came close in the first half to score after Bryan Mbeumo raced towards the goal on a counter-attack to find Amad. He had a difficult shot, and eventually the shot was too weak to cause any damage. The first-half display was met by boos from the Old Trafford crowd at half-time and during the first half as well.

Solanke had another chance at the start of the second half but put it over the top, amid a wrestle with Lisandro Martinez in the six-yard box. It was United, however, who started the second half with promise.

Substitutions by Michael Carrick around the hour mark made the difference as United showed intent before Mbeumo got the goal off Cunha’s low cross. Luke Shaw was at the end of a scuffle with Bentancur, who was shown a red card for headbutting Shaw.

United failed to capitalize on their one-man advantage and seemed to sit on their 1-0 lead, but Solanke punished them with a header to level the game in injury time. The draw left United with just 6 points off their first six games. The result leaves United with their worst start in 40 years.

Lenne Sammens-6          Quiet afternoon for United no.1 till the goal, where he saw the ball go into the back of the net.

Leny Yoro-6       Defensively was fine but offered little upfront. Played out of position, and he showed good defensive awareness on the right back till his substitution.

Harry Maguire-5.5            Was decent in the first half but wasn’t much of an answer to Tottenham going forward in the second half

Lisandro Martinez-5      Took his eyes off the ball, resulting in conceding a late goal off Solanke.

Luke Shaw-6      Went up high on the pitch and remained a threat with his overlapping threat. Was involved in the scuffle that got Bentancur a red card for headbutting.

Kobbie Mainoo-6.5        Brilliant defensive tackle to deny Tottenham in the 68th minute. Remained composed in tight spaces against a physical Tottenham midfield.

Youri Tielemans-6          A decent performance and was solid throughout the minutes he was on the field

Bruno Fernandes-5        Another quiet and disappointing evening for the United captain after he failed to impress on a day he needed to get a result for United.

Amad-6              A positive return from injury, showed plenty of promise on the wing but failed to get past a resolute Tottenham side

Matheus Cunha-7           One of the liveliest options for United going forward. Provided an assist to Mbeumo to put United up front

Bryan Mbeumo-7.5        A brilliant finish off Matheus Cunha’s low ball to put United in front. Was dangerous going forward and denied a second on a counter by Kinsky off a brilliant long ball by Baleba.

Carlos Baleba (58’)-6.5  A decent debut overall for the Cameroon international. He put in a couple of dangerous balls up the field, including a clear ball to Mbeumo on a counter, who was denied by Kinsky.

Diogo Dalot (58’)-6      Provided a decent attacking threat when brought on around the hour mark.

Joshua Zirkzee (58’)-6   Showed plenty of promise and was a threat after he was brought in around the hour mark.

#2199. Le Havre vs Lorient - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joel Lefevre | 06 Nov 2026 00:01

A new era of football at Le Havre begins on Sunday when they host Lorient at Stade Oceane on matchday 10.

The Normandy club sit bottom of the Ligue 1 table after being hammered 5-1 by Paris Saint-Germain last week, while Lorient sit 11th following a 2-0 home defeat versus Brest on Monday.

Didier Digard’s tenure as Le Havre boss has been suspended as a precautionary measure, with last week’s defeat leading up to that decision.

It is up to Jair Karam to rejuvenate this squad, which will try to end a six-match Ligue 1 losing run and win for the first time all season.

The former Le Havre U-19 coach has a tall task ahead of him, with the senior squad conceding three or more goals in their last three league encounters.

They are just two games short of matching their longest domestic winless run from last season (11 matches).

Heading into this weekend, they are six points behind Troyes for the relegation playoff spot while trailing Angers by that same margin for Ligue 1 safety.

Les Ciel et Marine can extend their Ligue 1 unbeaten run versus Lorient to six matches on Saturday and earn successive top flight triumphs against them for the first time this century.

A lack of creativity has held Lorient back throughout this season, and that was the case for them on Monday night.

Alexandre Dujeux’s team are goalless in their last two Ligue 1 outings, failing to register a single effort on target in both of those games.

Knowing this, it is perhaps unsurprising to learn that they have created the fewest big chances in the French top flight this season (16).

Away from home, however, the Brittany club have look solid defensively, earning points in three of those four instances this season, collecting three clean sheets along the way.

At the same time, Lorient are currently just three points behind a European spot heading into matchday 10.

Les Merlus have points in three of their last four visits to Normandy, with their last Ligue 1 victory at Le Havre occurring in 2008 (3-1).

Due to adductor pain, Le Havre could be missing Timothee Pembele once again, while Elias Jelert is questionable with a leg injury, Lionel Mpasi and Felix Mambimbi are recovering from Achilles tendon ruptures and Stephan Zagadou is out because of a cruciate ligament tear.

Josh Maja has scored in consecutive league fixtures, netting the opening goal for Le Havre last week, before PSG stormed back with five unanswered.

A muscle strain will likely keep Panos Katseris on the Lorient sidelines, Mohamed Bamba is questionable due to physical discomfort and Bandiougou Fadiga is doubtful because of a hamstring strain.

Theo Le Bris was one of three newcomers who came off the bench Monday after sitting out against Angers, along with Aiyegun Tosin and Noah Mbamba.

Bodart; Ouziad, Seko, Touba, L. N’Diaye; Ebonog, Mwanga, Koffi; Nakamura, Maja, Fofana

Mvogo; Adjei, Talbi, Toure; Sylla, Cadiou, Avom, Kouassi; Makengo, Faye; Balde

Le Havre are unlikely to face many scoring threats from Lorient, and combined with their desire to impress their new interim manager, we believe the Normandy side will finally collect maximum points.

#2198. Michael Carrick reacts to Man Utd boos after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick admitted Manchester United’s 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur was a painful result, after his side failed to protect the lead against 10-man Tottenham at Old Trafford.

Manchester United looked set to claim three points when Bryan Mbeumo scored in the second half off Cunha’s low cross, but Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser in injury time left Carrick facing more questions on United’s performance and his future.

Speaking to BBC Match of the Day, the United manager felt his players improved after the break but failed to handle the situation when victory was within reach.

“It was a mixed game. We found it tough to start with. For a number of reasons, we were not quite there. You could see it was a challenging time,” Carrick said.

“But the lads grew into the game, in the second half I thought we were better in everything. To get the game as we wanted it, in such a good position – we should see it through. We have to manage those situations.”

United have now conceded two late goals that have cost them valuable points, something Carrick believes has made their position look worse than it might have otherwise.

Asked whether a lack of confidence had contributed to United’s slow start, he disagreed but admitted matches can quickly slip away from his side.

The boos at half-time and full-time also clearly affected him.

“It’s not nice, it hurts a lot because I care about this club. But I understand the disappointment, the reality is what it is, we need to get back to winning games.”

#2197. It pushes you forward – Jacob Murrell feeling fuelled by Falkirk fan fervour

Falkirk striker Jacob Murrell believes the passion of Scottish football is fuelling his goalscoring form.

The American has netted five goals since joining from DC United, helping the Bairns go on a four-game winning run ahead of Sunday’s trip to face Hearts.

The 22-year-old said: “It’s a little different just because the games matter a bit more here than they did back in the MLS, just because of the promotion/relegation and how much the fans care about results. So the intensity of the games is much higher, you’re not finding easy games.

“For me, it’s the best thing in the world to play under this type of pressure. It just gives me a purpose, it gives me that fuel.

“I love that aspect of it. I love that the way that our group goes on the field and performs matters to people. That’s the most special thing about what we get to do and it drives us on the hard days when we have to come to training, when we’re tired, when we’re sore.

Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to Neil McCann: Why nothing has changed in my approach to build strong Kilmarnock team

"We’re playing for something more than ourselves, that pushes you forward.

“Like after the Celtic match, when all the fans stayed in their seats, celebrated the win with us after, that wasn’t just a win for us as a team, but for the full club, for the fans, for the community. It was a really great moment for everybody.”

Murrell was among 17 summer signings but manager John McGlynn has quickly fashioned the group into an effective team.

“The people have been really good to me,” he said. “Falkirk in general has been really good to me. The club, the team-mates, but then the fans as well have been really good.

“There’s a bit of learning curve the first month to get up to speed and get all the stuff sorted with a car, an apartment and a bank account. All the stuff that you don’t see behind the scenes.

“Little stuff that’s different, like when I went to the grocery store, I couldn’t find eggs. Back home, they’re only in the fridge. They’re on the shelf here.

“But I’ve got into a really good rhythm with a couple of team-mates going to the gym after training and just getting into good habits where we’re cooking our own food and eating well.

“Just feeling full of confidence that we’re putting in the work and we’re going to reap the benefits of it.”

#2195. "I didn't really like him" - James Trafford's first thoughts on new West Ham boss Scott Parker

Leeds United goalkeeper James Trafford has revealed his initial relationship with new West Ham United boss Scott Parker was difficult during their time together at Burnley.

The duo were part of a record-breaking Burnley side, with the club accumulating 100 points and keeping a record 30 clean sheets in the second tier. They conceded just 16 goals as they were promoted alongside Leeds United.

Now languishing around the relegation zone in the Championship, James Trafford has joined Leeds, while West Ham United have Scott Parker in charge. It's all change across the three clubs, highlighting just how quickly things can change in football.

Trafford has revealed that, despite Burnley's excellent form with him in goal, Parker did not see eye-to-eye with the goalkeeper. The 24-year-old spoke to the BBC and revealed his thoughts.

He said: "Yeah, I had a big one with Scott Parker. We had a very, very weird start to my time at Burnley with him. And I don't think he liked me too much.

"I didn't really like him too much. It was just weird. We didn't like, we couldn't connect, it was hard. But then, by the end of it, he understood me really, really well. And we got along really, really well."

He was asked: "How did you get to that point?"

Trafford added: "I don't know. I actually don't know."

The interviewer replied: "It probably helped that you were playing pretty well on the pitch, didn't it?"

Trafford concluded: "Probably. Yeah, but he really understood me."

Trafford's quirkiness wasn't for everyone but some enjoyed his company at Burnley, including Bristol City's Scott Twine. He spoke of their friendship at Turf Moor, adding: "Well, I mean, I have Pokemon cards. Love Pokemon cards. Oh, I love them."

The interviewer asked: "What do you trade them?"

He said: "No, just open the pack. Cool. Good card. Put them in my binder. Collecting all the sets.

"I've always done it. I remember I used to live with Scott Twine and we were so bored in a pre-season and we did loads of different things.

"And one of them was just go buy them. Everything had to be a competition as well, so we ended up getting big into tennis. That was one thing.

"We used to play card games against each other on our phones and then the other thing was Pokemon cards. We used to just open them. It's just something I've done since."

On his relationship with Lawrence Vigoroux and how that influenced him at Turf Moor, Trafford also said: "Obviously, there's a lot of other goalies that I have watched and really like parts of their game, like Lawrence Vigoroux. I really enjoyed working with him. He had some really good things.

"Then, obviously, everyone talks about [Manuel] Neuer, [Thibaut] Courtois, and like the big hitters, but also some goalies that haven't

played at that level that are still [big influences]. You can take bits from their game."

Burnley supporters may be surprised to hear that Trafford and Parker did not immediately click, given how successful their partnership ultimately became at Turf Moor.

Trafford was outstanding behind a side that set Championship records during the 2024/25 promotion campaign, while Parker's organisation helped produce one of the most formidable defensive units the second tier has seen.

Will Burnley survive this season? Comment your thoughts below!

FLW have ranked the 10 best EFL Championship stadiums for atmosphere so far in the 2026/27 season.

The contrast with the current side could hardly be greater. Burnley have gone from conceding just 16 goals across 46 Championship matches in 2024/25 to shipping 16 in their opening seven games of this season.

Their defensive performances have been particularly poor compared with the standards Trafford and Parker helped establish, with a lack of organisation, consistency, and reliability at the back exposing just how far the club have fallen from that record-breaking team.

#2194. Bryan Mbeumo reacts to Man Utd draw with Tottenham

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Bryan Mbeumo admitted Manchester United have work to do after their frustrating 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, despite scoring a superb second-half goal at Old Trafford.

The Cameroon international put Manchester United ahead in the 73rd minute, finishing in the far corner after Matheus Cunha created the opportunity from a low cross. However, Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory, even though Tottenham had been reduced to 10 men following Rodrigo Bentancur’s red card.

Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2).

3 – Manchester United have failed to win any of their last three Premier League games against sides that have received a red card (D1 L2). Disadvantage? pic.twitter.com/DuSpvymZfu

Speaking to Sky Sports after the game, Mbeumo acknowledged that United had taken too long to find their rhythm.

“We took time to get into the game, but we played well in the second half. In the first half, that wasn’t us, we had to change. We tried to get at them,” he said.

Mbeumo recognised the threat Tottenham posed and admitted the result was disappointing, particularly given United’s position in the game before Solanke’s late goal.

“We know Spurs have good players who can be dangerous. It’s a bit disappointing, we need to work on things.”

United have struggled for consistency in the opening weeks of the season, and dropping points after taking the lead will do little to ease the pressure on Carrick. Mbeumo, however, insisted the team must look ahead rather than dwell on the setback.

“We need to keep going forward, as we have a lot of games.”

Michael Carrick was pleased with Mbeumo’s contribution, highlighting both the quality of his finish and the different roles he has played since joining United.

“Bryan’s done great. It was a fantastic finish again; he’s obviously played different roles for us but we know what he offers,” the United manager said.

“He’s got good quality and obviously the more goals he scores, the better. He’s doing really well for us.”

Mbeumo’s goal was one of the few bright moments in another difficult afternoon for United. Carrick’s team must now find a way to turn things around from here after just six points from six games.

#2193. Houston Dynamo vs St Louis City - prediction, team news, lineups

By Nsidibe Akpan | 06 Nov 2026 00:02, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 00:08

Houston Dynamo host St Louis City in Major League Soccer on Saturday at Shell Energy Stadium on the final day of the regular season.

Both clubs sit on 51 points in the MLS table, so Houston must win to guarantee home-field advantage in the best-of-three first round, while a win or a draw is enough to secure it for St Louis.

Houston arrive on 51 points from 14 wins, nine draws and 10 defeats, and a 3-1 loss at Portland on November 1 dropped them from the top four to fifth, level with St Louis on points but behind on goal difference.

That defeat followed a 1-0 win at Seattle on October 28, Houston’s first road win over the Sounders, courtesy of Lucas Halter’s 26th-minute header from a Mateusz Bogusz corner, his first goal in 20 league appearances this season.

Jonathan Bond made six saves as Seattle outshot Houston 21-2, and the clean sheet was Houston’s 13th of the MLS season, which equals the club record.

The win ended a seven-match winless run in the league that included a 0-0 draw at home to Minnesota United on October 24.

At Portland, Agustin Bouzat’s 71st-minute strike was Houston’s only shot on target, against six for the Timbers, whose goals came from David Da Costa, Kristoffer Velde and Vincent Janssen.

Guilherme was directly involved in 26 of Houston’s 42 league goals before that game, a rate of 61.9%, which helps explain why the Dynamo created so little without him and Hector Herrera, with Guilherme’s 16 goals and 10 assists the best return in the squad.

St Louis also have 51 points from 14 wins, nine draws and 10 defeats, and they hold fourth place on a goal difference of +10 to Houston’s +1.

City arrive on four straight MLS defeats, the first of them a 3-2 loss at Minnesota United on October 17 in which they led 2-0 after 10 minutes and which ended a 21-match unbeaten run across all competitions.

A 3-0 defeat at Real Salt Lake followed on October 24, with Tomas Ostrak sent off for a second yellow card in the 44th minute, then a 3-2 loss to Portland at Energizer Park on October 28 and a 1-0 loss to San Diego on October 31 through Anders Dreyer’s 38th-minute goal, despite City having 16 shots to 12 and seven on target to three.

Yoann Damet blames fatigue after seven matches in 20 days, a run that included winning the US Open Cup final at Columbus on October 21, where a 0-0 draw became a 5-3 penalty shootout win after Ben Lundt saved the opening spot kick and Daniel Edelman scored the decisive one.

Carlo Holse, signed from Samsunspor for a reported $6m, the most expensive signing in club history, scored a hat-trick inside 21 minutes of the 6-2 win over LA Galaxy on October 11, the second regular-season hat-trick in City’s history.

St Louis have beaten Houston twice in 2026, winning the US Open Cup quarter-final on penalties on May 19 after a 2-2 draw in which Lundt saved Guilherme’s and Herrera’s spot kicks, and the league meeting 2-1 on August 22 through Dante Polvara’s 86th-minute winner after Ibrahim Aliyu’s equaliser, while Marcel Hartel, who scored twice in the cup tie, was sold to Hannover on August 11.

Bond was replaced in the starting lineup during the warm-up at Portland and Jimmy Maurer is out with a back injury, so Blake Gillingham started and made three saves.

Guilherme and Herrera both missed the Portland game and neither has been confirmed for Saturday, while Marcelo Saracchi remains out.

Franco Negri was substituted in the 36th minute at Portland and replaced by Duane Holmes, Felipe Andrade returned from injury off the bench, and Agustin Resch has been back in the defence alongside Halter since the Seattle game.

For the visitors, Roman Burki returned to training on Tuesday, November 3 after the concussion he suffered in the Open Cup final, and Damet has said he will not rush the goalkeeper, which leaves Lundt as the alternative.

Alexandru Matan, who has missed the last three league matches with a lower-leg injury, was also back in training on Tuesday and could be limited to a place on the bench.

Ostrak came off the bench against San Diego after his red card at Salt Lake, Holse was left out of the starting lineup for the Minnesota and Salt Lake defeats, and striker Rafael Navarro, who scored the opener at Minnesota, was ineligible for the Open Cup final.

Bond; Negri, Resch, Halter; Aliyu, Artur, Herrera, Bouzat, Ennali; Guilherme, Bogusz

Lundt; Fall, Baumgartl, Polvara; Totland, Edelman, Durkin, Wallem, Holse; Becher, Navarro

Houston must win and they are at home, and their route is the one that beat Seattle despite being outshot 21-2, with Bogusz’s corners producing Halter’s header and five assists for the midfielder this season.

St Louis have scored in only one of their last three league games, having thrown away a 2-0 lead at Minnesota, and a side that needs only a draw with Burki and Matan barely back from injury may be content to protect the point.

The counter-argument is that City have beaten Houston twice this year and outshot San Diego, but Houston’s club-record-equalling 13 clean sheets, and Guilherme if he returns, tilt a one-goal game towards the home side.

#2192. Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp tear into Man Utd after Tottenham draw

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United have come under fresh criticism after their disappointing 1-1 draw with Tottenham Hotspur, with Sky Sports pundits Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp questioning the team’s performance and the challenges facing Michael Carrick.

Manchester United looked on course for a much-needed victory at Old Trafford after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half. However, despite Tottenham being reduced to 10 men, Dominic Solanke’s late equaliser denied Carrick’s side all three points.

Gary Neville felt Tottenham deserved the points, pointing to Solanke’s late goal and his impact as Tottenham captain.

“Tottenham deserved a point. That was a really important moment for Solanke. He’d missed some big chances and was captain for the first time,” Neville said on Sky Sports.

“They were the better team by far. If they’d left without a point, it would have been terrible for them.”

The former United defender also expressed concern about what the result means for Carrick, who faces increasing pressure to improve performances and results.

“Michael Carrick is left with a lot to think about. It was a worrying day in terms of the level of performance and a big week ahead.”

Manchester United spent massively in the summer to overhaul their midfield. However, Jamie Redknapp was critical of the performance he saw on the pitch.

“I do not see enough connections in that midfield, and they look devoid of any confidence, ideas, or patterns of play, playing out the back,” Redknapp said.

“Tottenham were just much better than them in every department, but that is a theme this season.”

The former Liverpool midfielder also questioned United’s energy and attacking organization, suggesting the squad is not strong enough to meet the demands placed upon it.

“Their energy levels are not good enough, and they look loose going forward; there was no real structure.”

Redknapp added that Carrick cannot fix every issue alone, insisting the manager needs backing to improve the squad.

“The squad is not good or strong enough, and Michael [Carrick] needs help in that respect.”

#2191. ‘Embarrassing themselves!’ Jamie Carragher hits back at Rio Ferdinand over Cristiano Ronaldo defence

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Jamie Carragher has taken aim at Rio Ferdinand for defending Cristiano Ronaldo following the Portuguese forward’s latest controversy with his national team.

Ronaldo has found himself at the centre of a growing dispute after abruptly leaving Portugal’s national team camp following a reported disagreement with head coach Jorge Jesus. The incident has prompted strong reactions from former teammates and pundits, with Ferdinand firmly siding with the Al-Nassr star.

Jamie Carragher, however, believes Ronaldo’s reputation should not shield him from criticism when his behaviour becomes difficult to defend.

Rio Ferdinand, who played alongside Cristiano Ronaldo at Manchester United between 2003 and 2009, criticised Jorge Jesus for the way he handled the situation.

Speaking on his Rio Presents podcast, the former United captain criticised Jesus for not showing respect to Cristiano Ronaldo.

“Cristiano Ronaldo was put in a very bad situation and was treated in an inappropriate way. He has an enormous history in football. Jorge Jesus did not deal with Ronaldo well and chose to treat him in that way, but I cannot find any explanation for it,” Ferdinand said.

Despite Jesus being Portugal’s manager, Ferdinand questioned his standing, comparing him with the likes of Sir Alex Ferguson and Pep Guardiola. To say the least, Ferdinand only embarrassed himself doing that.

Jamie Carragher saw the argument very differently and rightfully so.

Speaking on The Overlap Daily, the former Liverpool defender suggested that Ferdinand’s friendship with Ronaldo was influencing his reaction to the incident.

“People think it’s good to be close to people in the game, but this is where it becomes a problem,” Carragher explained.

He then called out both Ferdinand and broadcaster Piers Morgan, who has also been vocal in his support of Ronaldo.

“People like Piers Morgan and Rio Ferdinand, who are big people in the media whether you like them or not, are embarrassing themselves with the nonsense that they’re coming out with because they know Ronaldo. You can know someone and try to look after them, but you cannot defend the indefensible.”

Carragher believes Ronaldo’s achievements should not put the forward beyond criticism, particularly when his actions involve his national team and its management.

Gary Neville, who also played with Cristiano Ronaldo, has questioned Ronaldo’s handling of the situation, despite his longstanding affection for his former Manchester United teammate.

Neville argued that Ronaldo’s public response had made matters worse and the forward needed to accept criticism rather than expect others to defend him.

“With Cristiano, it’s difficult because I love him, but I can’t watch what happened at United in his second period and then see him make a statement like that without saying something. He has to be pulled up on that and told that it isn’t acceptable,” Neville said.

He also suggested Ronaldo’s extraordinary success, wealth and influence could have contributed to a sense that he was beyond challenge.

“When you have a billion pounds in the bank, the achievements he has had and the power he has, you feel you’re unbreakable, you feel you’re unstoppable,” Neville added.

Despite his criticism, Neville does not believe the episode will define Ronaldo’s legacy permanently. He expects the attention to fade once the forward retires, allowing his achievements on the pitch to take centre stage again.

#2189. He played for Wolves and Birmingham City - Then police made shocking discovery worth £160k

Bethany Patrick is a football writer based in Manchester, UK.

Her work focuses on the EFL, with particular interest in football governance, supporter culture, and fan experience and access.

Bethany is a director of the award-winning Sheffield Wednesday Women’s Supporters Group, where she works on fan engagement, strategy and campaigns for greater inclusion across the game.

She regularly represents supporters in governance spaces across Europe, including government panels and discussions around football regulation and the supporter's voice.

She has previously worked as a researcher for The Stretford Paddock and is a regular contributor to The Wednesday Week.

Michael Branch was once regarded as one of English football's brightest young prospects, earning comparisons with Robbie Fowler after breaking into Everton's first team as a teenager.

The former striker went on to represent Wolverhampton Wanderers, Birmingham City and Manchester City, as well as earning international recognition with England Under-21s.

However, his promising career eventually took a dramatic turn, culminating in a seven-year prison sentence in 2012 after police uncovered his involvement in serious drug offences.

Born in Liverpool in October 1978, Branch came through Everton's academy before making his senior debut against Manchester United at Old Trafford in February 1996, aged just 17.

His emergence generated considerable excitement at Goodison Park, with the young forward quickly attracting comparisons to Liverpool striker Robbie Fowler.

However, Branch struggled to fulfil the expectations placed upon him and gradually fell out of favour at Everton.

Loan spells at Manchester City and Birmingham City followed before he joined Wolves on loan in late 1999. After scoring four times in 11 appearances, the Midlands club made his move permanent for £500,000.

Branch initially featured regularly at Molineux but struggled to maintain his form, managing just six more goals in 67 appearances following his permanent arrival.

Further loan spells at Reading and Hull City followed before he departed Wolves in 2003, subsequently representing Bradford City and Chester City.

His professional career eventually came to an end in 2006, with spells at non-league Halifax Town and Burscough following as he attempted to prolong his playing days.

Following his retirement from professional football, Branch found himself struggling with financial difficulties and became involved in the criminal underworld.

On July 10, 2012, he was arrested by officers from the Serious Organised Crime Agency following an investigation into drug supply.

The investigation uncovered two separate incidents. In March 2012, Branch had handed over almost three kilograms of amphetamine to another man in a Liverpool car park.

Andre has revealed he turned down 'life-changing money' from Liverpool when he was at Fluminense, a year before he left to join Wolves.

Four months later, officers searched his home in Otterspool, Liverpool, where they discovered one kilogram of high-purity cocaine.

The drugs were reported to have an estimated street value of approximately £160,000, making for a shocking discovery involving a former professional footballer who had once represented his country at youth level.

Branch subsequently admitted charges of supplying Class A and Class B drugs at Chester Crown Court. On November 8, 2012, he was sentenced to seven years in prison.

He ultimately served three-and-a-half years behind bars, with his release coming in 2016.

However, Branch's story did not end with his imprisonment, with the former Everton forward working hard to rebuild his life following his release.

He joined Everton in the Community in 2016, initially working on programmes supporting former offenders as they attempted to reintegrate into society.

His experiences also helped him connect with vulnerable young people.

Branch has since taken on various roles within the charity, including outreach work supporting young people into education, training and employment.

More recently, he established Social Striders Liverpool, a free running group designed to promote positive mental health through exercise and conversation.

Having previously spoken openly about the difficulties he experienced following his imprisonment, Branch has also become an advocate for mental health awareness and rehabilitation.

#2188. Gian Piero Gasperini 'demands' Chelsea fix to solve Roma second-half crisis

By Seye Omidiora | 06 Nov 2026 00:05

Roma are understood to be considering a move for Chelsea winger Jamie Gittens when the January transfer window opens as they look to add further depth to their attack.

Under head coach Gian Piero Gasperini this season, the Giallorossi have found it difficult to sustain their intensity in the closing stages of major fixtures.

Their latest setback saw them surrender a two-goal cushion in a frustrating 3-2 Champions League loss to Manchester United.

The result came after a comparable European collapse against Real Madrid and a squandered 2-0 lead against domestic rivals Inter Milan.

With Devyne Rensch now part of an expanding injury list, Roma are keen to bring in suitable cover amid a packed schedule.

La Gazzetta dello Sport reports that Roma sporting director Tony D'Amico intends to strengthen the squad once the winter transfer window opens.

D'Amico is said to be prioritising a lively left winger and a technically accomplished midfielder who could offer the side greater tactical flexibility.

Gittens has reportedly moved to the top of Roma's list after the club pursued the Chelsea forward during the summer.

The Serie A side are believed to be seeking a loan deal for the young Englishman, whose pace could add a new dimension to their attack.

Should a move prove beyond their reach, Roma are also keeping tabs on Real Madrid youngster Endrick and Stuttgart winger Jamie Leweling.

Frosinone prospect Giogi Kvernadze is another player under consideration as Gasperini seeks recruits who can make a difference in demanding matches.

Gasperini's approach relies on an unrelenting press, placing considerable physical and mental demands on his starting players.

A shortage of options on the bench has left the manager with limited scope to vary his substitutions or change the pace of matches late on.

The above source reports that Roma have shipped eight of their 10 league goals after the interval this season, underlining concerns over their ability to maintain energy levels.

The addition of a direct winger such as Gittens alongside a composed midfielder could give Gasperini the rotation options required to sustain his intense style.

Roma's hierarchy are said to be keen to repeat the impact of Donyell Malen's arrival last January and potentially build a squad capable of challenging for major honours.

#2187. Is Mainoo-Tielemans the right midfield option for Man Utd?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United drew 1-1 at home to Tottenham despite having an extra man advantage. The Reds saw Mbeumo put them in front, but Solanke scored a late leveller in injury time after Bentancur was shown a red card for headbutting Luke Shaw.

Manchester United’s worrisome season continues after accumulating just six points off the first six games, their first start in 40 years. But more than the results, what worries Manchester United is their midfield pairing.

United spent massively on their midfield rebuild and somehow can’t find the best combination in the midfield. Michael Carrick has gone with the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership, but they have failed to control the midfield. Individually, they both come with decent numbers, but collectively they fall short.

Mainoo and Tielemans struggled against Tottenham while closing the midfield and at times showed a lack of energy. On the day when nothing clicked for United, the Mainoo-Tielemans partnership particularly raised eyebrows.

Gary Neville has also raised serious concerns about Manchester United’s Mainoo-Tielemans partnership.

Neville was particularly unimpressed by the midfield balance, arguing that the problems went beyond one disappointing performance.

The former United captain stopped short of ruling out the combination altogether, but he suggested he has serious doubts about its suitability.

“Tielemans and Mainoo, I’m not calling time on it, that’s probably too drastic, but I’ve seen enough of it to think that’s not a midfield that’s dynamic enough to play at this football club in the Premier League.”

Former Spurs midfielder Redknapp felt United struggled to connect their midfield with the rest of the team during the opening stages.

He added: “I didn’t see enough connections in that midfield in the first hour. Baleba came on and did OK and showed a bit more.

“They look devoid of any confidence, any idea, any patterns of play, playing out from the back. Tottenham were much better than them in every single department.”

While Carlos Baleba was a particular highlight on the day, he showed great potential when brought on. He brought a new sense of energy in the midfield and sent a few balls over the top for a couple of quick counterattacks as well.

Redknapp also pointed to the physical demands of modern Premier League football, arguing that United cannot afford to be outworked by their opponents.

Tottenham ran a staggering 5 km more than Manchester United, something Redknapp felt was a pattern.

"Spurs ran 5km more than Man United… it's like that EVERY GAME!"Jamie Redknapp is less than impressed with the lack of ground covered this season by Manchester United 😬 pic.twitter.com/BxWkFuyGJ1

“There is a pattern that I don’t really like when I watch teams, and that’s when every time the stats don’t lie and the other team runs more than you.

“I feel in Premier League football right now there are a couple of non-negotiables, and one is you have to run more than the other team if you want to win or at least get close enough. Tonight Spurs ran 5km more than Manchester United.”

He believes Tottenham’s pressing exposed the lack of pace in United’s midfield, making it difficult for the hosts to move the ball safely through the centre.

“In terms of how they played today, I think they have an issue with Tielemans and Mainoo in midfield. I don’t see enough energy in there. Baleba will add a lot and will give them a tremendous amount of energy but right now, when things aren’t going well… and Tottenham knew it!

“Every time United played the ball out from the back they pounced. They pressed them because they knew United don’t have the pace and speed in midfield to get away.”

“In isolation, one of them is absolutely fine, the two of them together doesn’t work for me. They haven’t got the pace or the energy to get around the pitch for 90 minutes.”

#2186. Marink Reedijk lays down Aberdeen challenge to Hibs team after Hearts win

Hibernian manager Marink Reedijk has challenged his players to replicate their Hampden hunger against Aberdeen as they get back in the “rat race”.

Reedijk told his players “I want music, I want beer, I want everything” when they returned to the dressing room after beating Hearts in the Premier Sport Cup semi-finals and joined in a rendition of Sunshine on Leith with their fans.

But the Dutchman is now determined to secure a first William Hill Premiership win when Hibs travel to Pittodrie.

“It was a very beautiful moment,” he said. “After the match, you saw what it meant to the fans, what it meant to the club and what it meant to the players. That is important because this is what football is about.

“The best thing for me was that we showed we can get a result in the way we want to play. We can come back from 1-0 down, play beautiful football but also do the dirty work. That was important to see.

It pushes you forward – Jacob Murrell feeling fuelled by Falkirk fan fervour Mika Baur identifies the Celtic challenge he is ready to rise to

“However, it’s history now. We need to make sure we can do it over multiple matches and for a longer period of time.

“We’re in a sort of rat race, with game after game after game. We do everything at the highest possible intensity, from training sessions to matches. Therefore, you need to celebrate your victories sometimes.”

He added: “For me, it’s important that we make another step in the way we want to play. We need to do things better than we did in the last match. Whether it’s Hearts or someone else, we need to have the same hunger for every match.”

#2185. One lesson Senne Lammens learned after Tottenham’s late equaliser

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Senne Lammens believes Manchester United must learn to finish games off after their late collapse against Tottenham Hotspur.

The result left the goalkeeper frustrated at Old Trafford despite leading the game and with a player more on the field.

Manchester United looked set for three points after Bryan Mbeumo put them ahead in the second half, but Dominic Solanke’s stoppage-time equaliser denied Michael Carrick’s side a much-needed victory.

Speaking to MUTV, Senne Lammens admitted the team had struggled early on but had done enough after the break to put themselves in a winning position.

“Football can be a bit cruel at times,” the Belgian goalkeeper said. “There are a lot of ups and downs, especially in a game like that.”

Senne Lammens felt United failed to take advantage of Tottenham’s red card, allowing the visitors too much space to find a late equaliser.

“We’ve won a lot of games against 10 men [in the past], but you also know there can be a tricky situation when you’re playing against 10 men, because they have nothing to lose and they know that.”

He added that United should have scored again to put the result beyond doubt or, at the very least, prevented Tottenham from creating further chances.

“I think that’s what we did wrong. We gave them a little bit too much breathing room to create those chances, and then one ball has to go right for them, one bounce has to go their way, and they can score like that.”

The goalkeeper also pointed to similarities with United’s earlier disappointment against Everton, where points slipped away late in the match.

“You feel like those games just slip away from you, and there are one or two situations where you don’t really have control,” he said.

“I think there’s a lot to learn from today. It wasn’t all negative, not at all, but especially the way it ended left a very bad feeling.”

“I think the easy answer is that we have to be able to kill the game faster and not put ourselves in that position to give them a chance.”

Despite the frustration, Lammens said there were positives to take from the performance. However, the late goal had left a bitter feeling, and United now need to turn those lessons into better results.

#2184. Manchester United defence: Why Carrick must trust Yoro and Heaven

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United endured another embarrassing draw against 10-man Tottenham after failing to register their second win despite bringing in a plethora of players.

Manchester United invested heavily last season in their attacking options, bringing in Bryan Mbeumo and Matheus Cunha.

This season, they overhauled their midfield. United spent massively on Carlos Baleba, Youri Tielemans and Andrey Santos. Despite spending massively, they have failed to produce the control they would have expected in the midfield. The lack of control was evident in the recent Tottenham game, where Mainoo-Tielemans partnership failed to control the game collectively.

The midfield question was covered in our recent report as well, highlighting a lack of control and carried comments from Gary Neville and Jamie Redknapp.

The bigger question is when United will strengthen their defence. As per our previous report as well, Manchester United are planning to overhaul their defence in the upcoming transfer windows.

United were heavily linked with several left backs but eventually failed to bring any one. The situation left Luke Shaw as the only senior option in the position, while Patrick Dorgu is used upfront by Michael Carrick.

Harry Amass is another option for Michael Carrick, but he is only here because of a failed transfer move. He has been rarely used at left back as Dalot and Mazraoui get the nod in the position ahead of him.

The situation at right-back isn’t all good either. Diogo Dalot is the first option there, but he rarely provides the assurance that puts Michael Carrick at ease. Noussair Mazraoui is the second choice at right-back, but his attacking numbers aren’t great either.

Harry Maguire and Lisandro Martinez form up United’s centre-back pairing while Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven are used all over the defensive line in rotation when needed. Yoro was used at right-back against Tottenham while Heaven arrived as left-back towards the end of the game.

Manchester United’s season hasn’t been defensively strong or anywhere near being good. They have conceded goals in all Premier League games and only kept one clean sheet against Sabah in the Champions League.

Lisandro Martinez has come under scrutiny after errors on four occasions. Against Ipswich Town, Manchester United won 5-2, but his error resulted in Ipswich’s second goal.

His own goal against Fulham gave away two points at Craven Cottage in the 1-1 draw. Another error led to Solanke’s goal against Tottenham, when he took his eyes off the ball.

His injury concerns are another aspect, and maybe Michael Carrick needs to make a decision.

Manchester United’s success depended heavily on youth over the years. When pundits questioned them, Sir Alex Ferguson gave them the chance to prove them wrong. Alan Hansen made the famous quote “You can’t win anything with kids” on the BBC’s Match of the Day on August 19, 1995. United did exactly that, played youth and went on to win everything.

Michael Carrick needs to do exactly that. He needs to trust youth and allow them to make mistakes. Leny Yoro and Ayden Heaven arrived as massive potentials, but they haven’t been given the license to express themselves, without fearing for their places. Both the youngsters have rarely started together or in a stretch of games. It is maybe time for them to take their chances.

Michael Carrick needs to keep Harry Maguire as a senior experienced figure around them. There is no doubt over Martinez’s ability, but he is not the answer to United’s long-term problem. United need to blend youth with experience as their identity moving forward. Lisandro Martinez perhaps isn’t the answer to United’s problem.

A defensive revival expected in the upcoming transfer windows needs to happen now. It must happen with chances to the Youth. Michael Carrick needs to be brave enough and throw the youngsters in the mix. What it will bring is a long-term solution. They need to experience the errors and pressures that will shape them in football.

By Joel Lefevre | 06 Nov 2026 00:05

A 2-0 defeat at Lyon last weekend dropped Les Scoistes down to 15th in the table, while Nice climbed up to eighth after a convincing 5-2 home win versus Rennes.

The fall international window seemed to kill any momentum Angers might have had leading up to that as they aim to get back in the win column this weekend for the first time since September.

Stephane Gilli’s men have claimed just one point from their last four Ligue 1 affairs since the season resumed in October, putting them a point above Le Mans who are currently in the last automatic relegation place.

This team have won just one of their five home games in the competition this season, going without a point in three of those outings.

So far in 2026-27 they have allowed nine goals at home in Ligue 1, one more than they gave up in their final eight league fixtures a season ago.

Le Gym can also claim their second away triumph of the campaign, which would match their total from the 2026 portion of last season.

After nine Ligue 1 matchdays, they have amassed the same number of points that they had at this stage of last season (14).

At Nice, Laurent Abergel and Antoine Mendy are recovering from cruciate ligament tears, Moise Bombito remains doubtful with a lower leg fracture, Ali Abdi has a leg issue, while Axel Witsel is questionable because of a muscle strain.

Elye Wahi netted a hat-trick for them against Rennes, with Jonathan Clauss and Mohamed Amoura also scoring at Allianz Riviera.

#2182. £5m Wolves man faces fresh dilemma: Cardiff City & Norwich City must act

The recruitment team at Wolves will already have one eye on the January transfer window as they look to strengthen Cesar Peixoto’s squad to help their promotion push.

It seems inevitable that Wanderers will try to bring in a new striker, but, aside from the lack of depth up top, the Portuguese head coach will be pleased with the overall state of the squad.

However, one problem facing Wolves is the size of the squad, as they are already at the 25-man limit. Therefore, to secure new additions, players will have to leave.

And, one man who may be open to a move is Tommy Doyle, with the midfielder having only played 281 minutes in the Championship this season heading out of the midweek fixtures.

As well as that, Peixoto doesn’t seem to know Doyle’s best position, as he had operated higher up the pitch before coming on as a number six in the recent win over Bristol City.

So, the £5m signing may feel he is at a stage in his career where he can play regularly and nail down a permanent home.

With that in mind, we look at TWO Championship clubs who should be targeting the 25-year-old in the New Year…

The Bluebirds have had some fantastic moments back in the Championship under the guidance of Brian Barry-Murphy, who has implemented a clear style of play with the Welsh side.

Yet, the one major problem Cardiff have right now is a lack of depth, and, in truth, they could do with strengthening in various areas in the January transfer window.

Football League World ranks all 24 Championship clubs by the longest-serving member of their squad.

A new midfielder would certainly be welcome, even if it isn’t a priority, and Doyle is ideally suited to the way that Barry-Murphy wants to play, as he is excellent on the ball.

The Cardiff fans got to see Doyle’s qualities during a loan spell in the 2021/22 campaign, but he has matured a lot as a player since then, and he could now slot in as an important figure in the XI.

Again, he should be able to flourish in a possession-based approach, as Doyle can help control the tempo, and he would get the ball forward quickly. Plus, his mentality and experience at this level should help a relatively young Bluebirds squad.

After a slow start to the season, Norwich look to have finally turned a corner, and there will be a belief that they can still win promotion this season, which was always the aim under Philippe Clement.

But, the first few months of the season have shown that Norwich are still lacking depth, even if they have been very unfortunate with injuries.

With Kenny McLean missing too many games through injury, Norwich are in need of a new midfielder who can hold the ball, move it quickly, and help control games - which is what Doyle is all about.

Should Wolves let Tommy Doyle leave the club in January? Let us know in the comments below.

It remains to be seen whether Wolves would be willing to sell to the Canaries, as they could be promotion rivals, but it’s certainly something that Norwich should explore.

That’s because, on paper, it could be a great move for Doyle and Norwich, and he is the sort of addition that Clement should look to make to ensure his group can last the distance in the promotion race this season.

#2178. Why must Manchester United move beyond the Glazers?

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Glazers took control of Manchester United in 2005. That takeover has since caused so many disruptions and protests by the fans over massive debts and insufficient backing of the team in the transfer market, despite successful periods in the Premier League and Europe.

Now Manchester United need to think about who should control the club in the future. Laurie Whitwell has now shared his views on why Manchester United need an ownership change. He simply wants the Glazers gone from Old Trafford. However, he also recognizes that replacing the current ownership doesn’t automatically mean things will improve for United.

The journalist believes the change in ownership has had a lasting effect on United, with the club still feeling its consequences today. A fresh start might be welcome, but the identity of the next owners would matter just as much as the departure of the existing ones.

“Personally, I would like to see the Glazers gone from Manchester United,” Laurie Whitwell said on The Rest is Football podcast with Gary Lineker.

“That change, you know, just really affected and still impacts United now.”

The desire for different ownership is one thing, but finding people capable of taking the club forward is another.

Whitwell admitted there is no perfect answer when it comes to who should take charge.

That leaves United facing a difficult choice. A sale could offer the chance to change how the club is run, but simply bringing in a new owner would not guarantee better decisions or a brighter future.

Whitwell also raised the idea of United supporters having a greater say in the club’s ownership and decisions, much like the Barcelona model where fans own the club itself. However, he highlighted that the Barcelona model isn’t ideal either and has its own shortcomings.

Changing the names at the top would only be one part of the change. The club would also need an ownership structure capable of making sound decisions and providing a direction.

The issue of ownership goes beyond whether the Glazers should stay or leave. Manchester United needs to find the right people and the right model to secure the club’s future. Getting that decision wrong could leave them facing the same circus the fans are enduring under the Glazers.

#2177. Celtic's 'Che Guevaras' should show their fellow fans some respect at this year's AGM

There is about as much chance of Dermot Desmond taking a seat amongst the Union Bears ultras group the next time Celtic play Rangers at Ibrox in January as there is of the billionaire Irish financier rocking up at their AGM at Parkhead next month.

Desmond, who has been the Glasgow club’s principal shareholder for more than two decades, has not attended the gathering since way back in 2006 and he is not, with disaffection about the stewardship of the Scottish champions still rife in the stands, about to break with tradition.

The greeting which chief executive Michael Nicholson, chief financial officer Chris McKay, interim chairman Brian Wilson and manager Martin O’Neill received from some protesters when they attended a question-and-answer session at the Greenock Supporters’ Club this week gave an indication of the sort of treatment he would receive if he did.

Speaking at his pre-match press conference ahead of the Europa League encounter with Besiktas on Thursday night, O’Neill expressed his dismay at the fact that around 25 supporters appeared wearing face coverings. “That was the saddest thing,” he said. “I would never have thought that fans would be clothed in balaclavas at a meeting.”

The frustration in the statement which the Celtic Fans Collective, an umbrella coalition of supporters’ clubs and organisations, published this week demanding that Desmond make a rare appearance at the meeting was palpable and understandable.

That the man who, rightly or wrongly but unquestionably, wields the most power at Parkhead is an annual no-show highlights everything that is wrong with how the former European Cup winners are being run. Are accountability and dialogue too much to ask for?

Is the famously publicity-shy man from Macroom in County Cork, though, really going to trap when the same individuals who hurled tennis balls at him when he was playing golf in the Alfred Dunhill Championship at St Andrews last month are likely to be in attendance?

There has been fevered speculation about when Celtic directors will face their shareholders in recent weeks. Supporters are desperate to know what their long-term football strategy is, who has been driving recruitment, how governance is going to be modernised, why tens of millions of pounds continue to sit in the bank and much else besides.

But are they likely to get answers to those questions now the AGM date has been set for Tuesday, December 1? The actions of a certain sub-section of their support in Inverclyde in midweek suggested that it could well be another bad-tempered, ugly and ultimately futile get-together.

Celtic interim chairman Brian Wilson, left, chief executive Michael Nicholson and chief financial officer Chris McKay at Parkhead (Image: Stuart Wallace / Shutterstock)

Ill-feeling about then-chairman Peter Lawwell bringing last year's annual meeting to a premature end amid persistent disorder still lingers. It has been suggested once again in recent days that the move was a cynical ploy by the besieged board to avoid public scrutiny.

However, David Low, the financial analyst who was a driving force behind the Fergus McCann takeover back in 1994 and is now associated with the Celtic Supporters Limited pressure group, rejected that interpretation of events when he appeared on the A Celtic State of Mind podcast last week.

“You are allowed to hold the people who shareholders elected to account once a year,” he said. “But it is a professional environment. Last year’s meeting was a bun fight. Nobody came out of that looking well. It is a professional forum and you have to respect the forum you are in.

“Give them red cards if you have to if they give an unsatisfactory answer. But listen to what is said and then react. That is the way it has always been at Celtic, even when it was a private company.

“That was a horrible meeting last year. People go there who want to learn. That is why they are there. You turn up, you ask questions and you try to learn something. If you don’t like it you can boo and do whatever within reason. But you have got to listen.

“It disintegrated into a shambles last year. It was over-the-top. There was a lot of unacceptable behaviour from the hardcore. It was not on. It was the biggest AGM in 20 years.”

There has been widespread unhappiness about the course Celtic are on for some time and a substantial section of the support wants meaningful change in how the Parkhead club is run. But some of the more vocal and militant members of the rebel alliance can be arrogant and selfish.

They did not merely show disrespect to the board last year. They showed disrespect to the hundreds of fellow shareholders who had turned up, many from far afield, wanting to exercise the same right to scrutinise those running their club.

The club later alleged that its head of safety, security and operations, Mark Hargreaves, had been threatened and intimidated by two prominent members of the Green Brigade. Whatever the truth of those allegations, it was a sorry affair from start to premature finish.

Celtic fans hold up banners referencing their teams failures against teams in Europe (Image: Andrew Milligan)

One lifelong fan voiced his exasperation at the leaders of the civil war that was raging at his beloved club in these pages as he attended the Scottish Gas Scottish Cup game against Dundee back in February.

“We don't need modern-day Che Guevaras,” he said before he ignored the boycott that had been called. “We want people who love Celtic.” He is not alone in taking that view.

The Celtic Fans Collective appealed for the “focus to be firmly on scrutiny rather than protest” at the AGM in their latest missive. They are right. The board should be made to answer searching questions. Those who shout directors down before their answers can be heard are serving neither their cause nor their fellow supporters.

It would perhaps help the Parkhead club to move forward on and off the park if those words were heeded and there were no disruptions from “aggressive and irrational factions” hellbent on “stirring up toxicity”.

#2176. Orlando City vs New England Revolution - prediction, team news, lineups

By Nsidibe Akpan | 06 Nov 2026 00:13

Orlando City SC host New England Revolution in MLS regular-season action at Inter&Co Stadium on Saturday on Decision Day.

Orlando City SC are guaranteed a seventh consecutive playoff appearance, but they sit ninth in the Eastern Conference table on 43 points, and only a win can lift them level with seventh-placed FC Cincinnati on 46 and, with help from other results, out of the wild-card game.

New England Revolution have a different incentive, as they are third on 58 points, one behind Inter Miami, and a result better than Miami's at home to Charlotte FC would secure the second seed and home advantage through the conference semi-finals.

Orlando City SC won only four of their first 15 league games before the World Cup break, so the club replaced head coach Oscar Pareja with assistant Martin Perelman as interim in March.

The Lions have won eight, lost five and drawn five of their 18 games since the restart, but they are now winless in three, after a 2-2 home draw with New York City FC on October 24 in which they conceded on the last kick.

They then drew 2-2 at Chicago Fire on October 29 after leading 2-0 at half time, and lost 4-0 at Charlotte FC on October 31.

Orlando have conceded 14 goals in their last six league games, and the Charlotte defeat, where Maxime Crepeau saved a penalty only for Abdoulaye Doucoure to score the rebound in the 84th minute, was their first scoreless game in 26 matches across all competitions.

Antoine Griezmann has 14 goals and seven assists in 17 MLS games since arriving in the summer, although his run of 12 straight league games with a goal contribution, which equalled the club record set by Martin Ojeda in 2025, ended at Charlotte.

Perelman can still rely on home form, as Orlando are unbeaten in their last six home league games (five wins, one draw) and have scored 15 goals to six conceded in a run that began on August 22.

New England Revolution have 58 points from 18 wins, 11 defeats and four draws in the first season of head coach Marko Mitrovic, who replaced Caleb Porter after two seasons without a playoff place.

The Revs clinched a playoff berth on October 14 with a 3-2 win at FC Cincinnati and then beat Chicago Fire 1-0, Philadelphia Union 2-1 away and Red Bull New York 3-2 to make it four straight wins.

Daniel Gazdag put the Revolution ahead in the 53rd minute against Inter Miami on November 1, but Lionel Messi scored the winner in the 83rd minute of a 2-1 defeat in front of a season-high 54,112 at Gillette Stadium, which dropped New England from second to third.

Matt Turner made eight saves in the win over Red Bull New York, one short of his career high, and his 18 wins are a Revolution single-season record for a goalkeeper, although the Revs also let a two-goal lead slip to 2-2 by the 64th minute that night.

Dor Turgeman has 12 goals, 10 of them since the World Cup break, while captain Carles Gil has 13 assists this season and reached 100 regular-season assists against Red Bull New York.

Mitrovic has built around young players, with Peyton Miller, who scored twice against Orlando on September 19, starting alongside the 21-year-olds Brooklyn Raines, Ethan Kohler and Joshua Wynder against Miami.

New England Revolution Major League Soccer form:

Orlando were without defenders Griffin Dorsey (back) and captain Robin Jansson (foot) and forward Tyrese Spicer (foot) at Charlotte, and none of them has been confirmed fit to return.

David Brekalo is back after serving a suspension for yellow-card accumulation at Charlotte, but The Mane Land reports that Iago, who was booked there, will serve a suspension in this game.

That leaves Wilder Cartagena, who covered at centre-back for Brekalo at Charlotte, and Brekalo as the likeliest central pairing, with Vinicius Nogueira under pressure at right back after he was substituted at half time following a rough first half.

Griezmann, Martin Ojeda and Ivan Angulo all started at Charlotte with no injury reported, and Angulo's seventh goal of the season at Chicago on October 28 was also his 19th for the club in all competitions.

New England had not published an injury report at the time of writing, but Jack Harrison (quadriceps) and Leonardo Campana continue to be out since March with a lower-body injury.

Alhassan Yusuf (hamstring) has returned from injury and was on the bench against Inter Miami.

Mitrovic started Turner behind Ilay Feingold, Kohler, Wynder and Will Sands against Miami, with Matt Polster and Raines in central midfield and Miller, Gil and Gazdag supporting Turgeman.

Crepeau; Nogueira, Brekalo, Cartagena, Taifi; Angulo, Braian Ojeda, Otavio, Pasalic; Martin Ojeda, Griezmann

New England Revolution possible starting lineup:

Turner; Sands, Wynder, Kohler, Feingold; Polster, Raines; Miller, Gil, Gazdag; Turgeman

New England should edge a high-scoring game because their quick attacks, which produced four goals in the September 19 meeting, face an Orlando defence that has conceded 14 goals in six league games and is short of Jansson, Dorsey and, reportedly, Iago.

Orlando's case rests on five wins in six home league games and on Griezmann, but those results came against sides such as Toronto FC and D.C. United, while their latest games against New England, Chicago and Charlotte brought one point from nine and 10 goals conceded.

Orlando had scored in every match in all competitions since May before the 4-0 defeat at Charlotte, so a goal for Griezmann or Martin Ojeda is more likely than not, which is why we expect the Lions to score twice and still fall one short.

#2175. Charlie Austin reacts to awkward Derby County, Sammie Szmodics interview

Former QPR, Burnley and Southampton striker Charlie Austin has backed Derby County forward Sammie Szmodics after his tense interview with a local reporter last weekend.

It has been a turbulent start to the season for Derby, and Szmodics made his frustration clear after the 4-1 defeat at Millwall last Saturday when he was involved in a heated exchange with BBC Radio Derby's Ed Dawes.

It was another day to forget for Derby last Saturday as they were comprehensively beaten by Millwall at The Den, with three of the Lions' goals coming in the opening 17 minutes, and Szmodics fronted up to the media post-match.

However, when Dawes suggested to Szmodics that he would be particularly angry about the situation as he was 'John Eustace's boy', it is fair to say that the 31-year-old was less than impressed, responding: "Yeah, of course, because I'm a professional footballer that wants to win. I'm not John Eustace's boy."

Dawes later attempted to clarify his comments, stating that he was referring to the fact that Szmodics had played for Eustace previously, to which he replied: "Yes, I've played under the manager, I'm not his boy, but we're all in it together."

After spending the second half of last season on loan at Pride Park, Szmodics returned to Derby on a season-long loan in August, and the move will become permanent in the summer for a fee of around £2.7 million — or £5 million if the club win promoted to the Premier League — after an appearance-related clause in the deal was triggered.

Szmodics has scored four goals and provided one assist in 14 appearances this season, but he is set to be out for six weeks after aggravating a knee injury that had been troubling him for much of the campaign in the 4-0 defeat to Norwich City on Tuesday night.

Szmodics' interview has caused plenty of debate over the past week, and Austin has become the latest pundit to give his opinion on the saga, praising the Derby forward for his reaction to what he believes was an inappropriate question.

"They know they're not performing," Austin said on his Chaz Kicks Off! podcast.

"Eustace was the assistant manager at QPR when I went back there, but he was also in the Derby starting XI when Bobby Zamora scored in that play-off final to beat them 1-0 and Richard Keogh cried.

"Hear me out here, Sammie Szmodics saying to that reporter 'I'm not his boy', fair play to you mate, I've got a lot of time for it.

"The reporter was being an absolute one of them mate, let me tell you, suggesting 'you're getting picked when you're not performing because you're his boy'.

"Fair play to Sammie Szmodics for giving him a little bit back.

"Players know they're underperfoming, they don't need someone from the radio coming out with nonsense questions.

"He's had a little nibble back, fair play to him.

"The problem you've got is, you can only do the talking on the pitch, and that's what matters.

"At the moment, Derby are not talking, they're not performing, and they are in trouble."

Szmodics is one of a number of Derby players who have worked under Eustace at one of his previous clubs, and while the pair have a strong relationship, it is understandable that he took exception to Dawes' question after the defeat at Millwall.

After a 10-game winless run, the Rams lifted some of the pressure on Eustace with a 3-1 home victory over Bolton Wanderers on Friday night in Szmodics' absence, but they will still head into the international break sitting in the Championship relegation zone.

Should Derby County part company with John Eustace during the upcoming international break? Tell us in the comments below!

It seems likely that Eustace will now be given more time to turn Derby's fortunes around, and he will be hoping that he is still in charge at Pride Park once Szmodics returns from injury in the New Year.

#2174. Fabrizio Romano shares latest on Martinez contract after explosive rant

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Argentinian defender has also apologized to the United higher ups as well over his comments, as shared by Fabrizio Romano.

Lisandro Martinez has held discussions with Jason Wilcox and Michael Carrick after speaking out following United’s 2-0 defeat to Atletico Madrid.

Martinez had questioned whether Carrick was backed enough in the summer and suggested the club needed changes.

Fabrizio Romano has now confirmed that United consider the matter resolved for now and expect Martinez to remain available for selection.

Speaking about the situation on his YouTube channel, as quoted by Football 365, Romano explained why Martinez’s comments caused concern inside the club.

“He spoke very strongly after the game against Atletico Madrid, which Man Utd lost. It was a very negative moment for the Red Devils. We know that Licha was very strong in his comments. He said that Michael Carrick was not backed enough during the summer transfer window.”

Romano added that Martinez had spoken directly to those at the club after the incident.

“Licha apologised to Man Utd as a club. He spoke to the directors, he spoke to Jason Wilcox, and he spoke to Michael Carrick, who was also involved in this conversation.”

“So, the underlying message remains the same. Lisandro is apologising for the way he expressed himself after a game during a difficult moment for Man Utd, but there was a discussion about the substance of what he said.”

Romano also revealed that United had imposed a financial penalty on the centre-back. However, the punishment does not appear to involve removing him from the squad.

“According to the information available tonight, Lisandro Martinez has been fined.”

He added that Martinez is expected to return to the squad as normal, with the fine considered sufficient punishment for the incident.

“The internal discussion also helped to resolve all the issues. For Man Utd, the case is closed.”

That should bring some immediate clarity to the situation, although questions remain about how the episode could affect Martinez’s longer-term position at Old Trafford.

Romano also pointed towards Martinez’s contract, which expires next summer, with United holding an option to extend it.

“So, let’s see what is going to happen there. For sure, this is the kind of situation that will have an impact on the situation surrounding Lisandro, but we have to see what happens next in terms of future transfers and everything else.”
