#2172. Tuttosport: Foster could follow Battersea Power Station lead to preserve San Siro – the idea

It is possible that the plans for the existing San Siro – once the new stadium is constructed – could change slightly.

Milan and Inter unveiled the Generali Stadium last Thursday, which will be the new 71,500-seat stadium for the two clubs, but the future of their current home is still uncertain according to Tuttosport (via MilanPress).

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The idea was to demolish a large part of the San Siro, keeping standing only a south-east portion, around 30% of the external ramps. A piece of the Curva Sud would be preserved and a part of the orange stand, areas that will be used for new purposes.

Some politicians – especially Senate President Ignazio La Russa – have always maintained that demolition isn’t necessary even with the new stadium. This concept has never been discussed by the two clubs or the council, but it could serve as an example to follow.

The paper adds that Norman Foster – one of the design architects behind the Generali Stadium – has declared that he wants to preserve a larger part of San Siro. It could follow the route of one of the previous projects in which he took part at Battersea Power Station in London.

Frank Gehry y Norman Foster construirán la fase final de Battersea Power Station. http://t.co/odgwaii4nz pic.twitter.com/1zyFjMu8P1

— Arquitectura Viva (@arquitect_viva) October 23, 2013

The building was restored by WilkinsonEye, but Foster’s studio played a key role in the area redevelopment with the Battersea Roof Gardens and 50 Electric Boulevard.

Battersea has thus remained a hub in London with a shopping centre inside and a whole neighbourhood around it on the river front, a move that could perhaps also be applied to the Meazza.

#2171. Leeds vs Man Utd preview: Team news, form, injuries and where to watch

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Leeds United welcome Manchester United to Elland Road on Sunday, with both sides looking to put a difficult run of results behind them and get their Premier League season on track.

Leeds United suffered a 2-1 defeat away to Arsenal last weekend despite James Justin scoring to put them in front in the 55th minute.

Manchester United also dropped points in the Premier League, drawing 1-1 with Tottenham, before losing 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in the Champions League away from home.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to find consistency in recent weeks and have come under immense pressure to get in results. The Whites meanwhile will hope that home advantage can make the difference when United face their bitter rivals on Elland Road.

Leeds have recorded one win, two draws and two defeats in their last five matches in all competitions. Their best result came against Newcastle, where they won, but a 6-3 defeat to Chelsea in the Carabao Cup and the loss to Arsenal have left them searching for a response.

Manchester United have also won just once in their last five matches. There is better news regarding Matthijs de Ligt, who could return to contention. Leny Yoro is also available for selection despite his Champions League suspension.

The match takes place at Elland Road on Sunday, October 18. Kick-off is scheduled for 2 pm BST (1pm GMT).

#2170. SBV Excelsior vs Go Ahead Eagles - prediction, team news, lineups

By Nsidibe Akpan | 06 Nov 2026 00:22

SBV Excelsior host Go Ahead Eagles at the Van Donge & De Roo Stadion in Rotterdam on Saturday, in round 12 of the 2026-27 Eredivisie.

Excelsior have conceded 19 goals in their last four league games, while Go Ahead Eagles have the Eredivisie's joint-top scorer in Soren Tengstedt but have conceded four times in each of their last two away league matches.

Excelsior collected 10 points from their first five league games to sit fourth in the Eredivisie table after a 2-2 draw at NEC on September 8, and they have taken one point from the 18 available since.

The 0-9 defeat by Twente on November 1 was the heaviest in Excelsior's league history, and it began to unravel when Nesto Groen was sent off after five minutes for a high challenge on Robin Propper, the quickest red card in a league match in the club's history.

Excelsior did not register a single shot and conceded two penalties, and head coach Ruben den Uil said his players had collapsed mentally and stopped carrying out their individual tasks once they fell 2-0 or 3-0 behind.

Excelsior have won one of their five home league games, scoring five goals and conceding 19, nine of them against Twente, while they have taken eight points from six away league matches.

Their 16 league goals include four at Cambuur on August 7, when Noah Naujoks scored twice, so Excelsior have managed 12 in the 10 games since.

That leaves Excelsior 15th on 11 points, two clear of 16th-placed Telstar, who currently hold the relegation play-off place.

Go Ahead Eagles have won three of their 11 league matches without ever winning two in a row, and they sit 10th on 14 points with a goal difference of minus four.

Their latest outing, at promoted Cambuur on October 31, finished 4-0 and was abandoned late in the second half after fireworks were thrown from the away end for a second time, with the KNVB ruling that the scoreline stands.

General director Jan Willem van Dop described the behaviour of the travelling fans as deeply sad, and Go Ahead Eagles are withdrawing away-match registration from supporters identified on footage of the fireworks and the disorder afterwards.

Tengstedt has scored eight league goals, level with Groningen's Brynjolfur Willumsson and Twente's Wout Weghorst at the top of the scoring charts, and he has scored in all five of Go Ahead Eagles' home games, including twice in the 3-2 win over Telstar on October 23.

Head coach Joseph Oosting moved Tengstedt into a free role behind striker Victor Edvardsen against Telstar, with Oskar Sivertsen on the right flank, and the same front four started at Cambuur.

Go Ahead Eagles have conceded in all 11 league games, including four at PEC Zwolle on October 18 and four at Cambuur, the latter with Jasper Cillessen starting in goal for the second time since his debut in the KNVB Beker.

Excelsior are without Groen, who was given a three-match ban with one match suspended, so he misses this game and the trip to Feyenoord on November 21.

Groen scored a stoppage-time winner in the 2-1 victory over Sparta Rotterdam on August 29, and David Garden, who has scored at Cambuur, Willem II and NEC this season, is the likeliest replacement.

Go Ahead Eagles remain without goalkeeper Nick Marsman, who has a knee injury and is not expected back until early January, and defender Pim Saathof, who has not played since May 2025 and is due back around the turn of the year.

Eus Waayers has been sidelined since mid-September.

Stefan Sigurdarson, who has scored against Feyenoord, ADO Den Haag and PEC Zwolle, was among the substitutes at Cambuur and against Telstar.

Cillessen is expected to keep his place in goal ahead of Kjetil Haug after starting the last two matches, and no Go Ahead Eagles player is suspended for the trip to Rotterdam.

Van Gassel; Bos, Widell, Plug, Meissen; Van Vianen, Hartjes, Naujoks; Yegoian, Garden, Sliti

Go Ahead Eagles possible starting lineup:

Cillessen; Sampsted, Dirksen, Kramer, James; Linthorst, Meulensteen; Sivertsen, Tengstedt, Suray; Edvardsen

Go Ahead Eagles have scored 23 league goals to Excelsior's 16 and have the Eredivisie's joint-top scorer in Tengstedt, against an Excelsior back line that has conceded four or more in three of its last four league matches.

The history points the same way, as Go Ahead Eagles have won the last three meetings without conceding, including a 1-0 victory at this ground on March 1, when Tengstedt scored the only goal despite Excelsior having more than half of the possession.

The case for Excelsior is that Go Ahead Eagles are winless in six away league games, have conceded in all 11 league matches, and rely on a scorer who had four goals in his first 41 Eredivisie appearances before this season.

We still expect Go Ahead Eagles to win, because Excelsior have failed to score in three of their last four league matches and are without Groen, so a visiting side that scores twice should be enough even if Excelsior find one goal at home.

#2168. The Celtic 'rollercoaster' Mika Baur is getting used to & how he spends his days off

It is easy to forget that Mika Baur only turned 22 over the summer. The German midfielder displayed maturity beyond his years in Celtic's captivating Europa League victory over Besiktas on Thursday evening, chasing lost causes, contributing defensively, and progressing play forward when the opportunity arrived.

It was his finest performance since joining the club for an initial fee of £4.5 million from Paderborn in August.

Baur compared his first few months in Glasgow to riding a rollercoaster, with downtime away from the football pitch proving rare.

"There’s no time to think about it! We are playing every three days and I really try to fight and bring my heart on the field all the time," Baur said. "Sometimes it works, sometimes it doesn’t, but I hope people understand as a young player it can be tough to be comfortable in a new country, a new culture and new club.

"But I really try my best. I have felt good in my first few months but it is a bit of a rollercoaster.

"Playing in this unbelievable stadium in front of these fans, getting the call-up to the national team was a big honour. You feel the pressure with some of the games we have lost but I am getting used to it."

This is Baur's first time living away from home. He credits his partner for making the transition to Scotland smoother, even if chances to play some golf are limited.

"I’m grateful to my girlfriend as she does most things and makes it comfortable at home," Baur continued. "The club also have people who have helped us a lot to settle.

"But you don’t get much time – you get home and lie on the couch!

"It’s not all stressful though. It’s also nice to go somewhere new and learn the city, get to coffee shops and restaurants.

"We’ve been to the beach, we’ve been to Edinburgh, we try to see a lot on our day off.

"I also play golf – so I’ve come to the home of golf! I’ve only managed to get to the driving range though. When you are playing every three days it’s a bit much to play nine or 18 holes.

"My father and brother play so hopefully the weather will be okay around Christmas to maybe manage a few holes."

Thursday's win over Besiktas wasn't quite a hole-in-one, but it marked a pivotal forward step in Celtic's Europa League campaign.

Finally up and running after consecutive defeats to Ferencvaros, Benfica and Celta Vigo, Baur gave credit to a raucous home support.

"The fans helped a lot," he explained. "You could feel the energy. It was not just us players on the field who deserved the win – it was also everyone around the stadium and they brought the energy as well.

"It’s an unbelievable feeling as a player to have everyone pushing you and being there for you. It gives you that last percentage of energy you need at the end.

"There is always pressure to win here, to win every three days. That’s what we all want. In the last two games it wasn’t possible for us. We knew it wasn’t good enough but here we played well and deserved it."

"The win was important after some tough weeks," Baur continued. "But the belief and the way we work together as a team has always been good.

"There was never any problem with that because we always believed in ourselves. We showed how we can leave our hearts on the field and put in a shift.

"It was important to get those three points and we deserved it. Hopefully we can go forward with confidence from this game."

Despite his stellar showing on Thursday, Baur missed out on Jurgen Klopp's latest Germany squad. He made his senior international debut against Serbia last month as the former Liverpool manager had named two separate squads for four fixtures.

Klopp's group was reduced this time around, though Baur will return to the under-21s.

"I can understand," Baur said of the snub. "There are so many unbelievable players in the squad, especially in my position.

"We’re talking about guys from Bayern Munich and Borussia Dortmund. I just do my best and hopefully they can see I am developing and improving.

"I’m still with the under-21s for the games against Belgium and Croatia, so that will be good. European matches like Thursday can only help and I have to hope they see.

"I’m still young but I’ll keep doing my best and we’ll see what happens next time."

#2167. Middlesbrough, QPR & West Brom have all dodged Bamba Dieng transfer bullet

Middlesbrough, Queens Park Rangers, and West Bromwich Albion were all linked with Senegal international Bamba Dieng during the summer transfer window, but none ultimately pursued a deal.

The 26-year-old instead joined Serbian giants Red Star Belgrade on a free transfer, where he has enjoyed some encouraging moments in front of goal after not joining Middlesbrough, QPR, or West Brom.

However, there is a growing argument that the Championship trio might have been wise to steer clear, particularly when looking beyond Bamba Dieng's domestic scoring record and assessing how he has performed on the European stage.

Dieng has certainly found the net a reasonable number of times since arriving in Serbia, but there is a significant difference between scoring in the Serbian SuperLiga and delivering consistently in the Championship.

More importantly, his performances in the UEFA Conference League have hardly suggested he would have been a transformative signing for the likes of Middlesbrough, West Brom, or QPR this season. Boro were even in talks with Dieng at one stage of the transfer window.

All three clubs should arguably be targeting promotion and, given their size and stature, would surely fancy their chances of competing for glory in a European competition of that nature if they were playing at that level.

FLW have ranked all 24 Championship clubs on how far they have fallen from their historical glory days, including Blackburn and West Ham.

A high-wage arrival such as Dieng should be demonstrating genuine upside against continental opposition, rather than simply padding his statistics and goal involvements in domestic fixtures for a club the size and stature of Red Star Belgrade in Serbia's top flight.

Red Star have faced Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes, with that trio being opponents that Championship sides would reasonably expect to beat or at least give a very good account of themselves against.

Dieng has failed to register a goal or assist in any of those three matches. Perhaps all three English clubs had the right idea by ultimately looking elsewhere or sticking with what they had in the end. The fact he limped off the pitch assisted by medial staff in their most recent fixture underlines this, too.

Dieng began his professional career in Senegal with Diambars before earning a move to Marseille in 2021, where he scored eight goals in 40 appearances. He subsequently joined Lorient permanently in January 2023, spending time on loan at Angers before enjoying his most productive campaign yet in 2025/26.

The 24-cap Senegal international may well have made the move to the Championship over the recent summer but he has also experienced missing out on a Premier League move, having agreed to join Leeds United from Marseille in September 2022 before a dramatic deadline-day change of plans saw Nice attempt to hijack the deal (BBC Sport).

Are you happy with your club's striking options with January just around the corner? Comment below!

Since joining Red Star on a free transfer in August, he has scored five goals in seven Serbian league appearances. However, he has no goals or assists in three Conference League outings, totalling just over 200 minutes, against opposition of the stature and quality of Lugano, Copenhagen, and Inter Club d'Escaldes.

Dieng would not have been somebody who could comfortably arrive as an expensive substitute at any of these three clubs, meaning his signing would have carried significant consequences for their existing attacking options.

At Middlesbrough, he could have been brought in instead of Will Lankshear, which would have been a considerable mistake given the former Tottenham Hotspur youngster's extraordinary goalscoring form in the Championship.

Type the top scorer next to each season. Surnames count. Two seasons had joint winners, so they need two names.

Top league scorers in the regular season, per season since the second tier became the Championship in 2004–05.

QPR might have been tempted to install him ahead of Richard Kone, potentially stunting the development of one of their most exciting young attackers. Meanwhile, West Brom already have Jimmy-Jay Morgan performing well, with Aune Heggebo also contributing positively.

All three clubs have younger forwards worth developing rather than blocking with an expensive, high-wage alternative. Dieng might yet prove to be a success in Serbia, but the Championship trio arguably made the right decisions for their respective squads.

#2166. Jamie Carragher names Man Utd Star who impressed him against Leeds

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United had a difficult evening once again as they were held to a 1-1 draw at Leeds United. Pressure mounts on Michael Carrick as United have just 7 points from 7 games.

Manchester United took the lead through Bryan Mbeumo in the first half, with the forward finishing Youri Tielemans’ excellent cross from the right. However, the visitors couldn’t hold on to their advantage as Dominic Calvert-Lewin equalised for Leeds United from a corner.

The draw extended United’s frustrating run of results, leaving Michael Carrick still searching for the consistency his side need.

Carragher, though, believes there were positives to take from the trip, particularly one performance at the back.

Jamie Carragher has singled out Harry Maguire for praise after Manchester United drew 1-1 with Leeds United at Elland Road on Sunday.

Speaking on Sky Sports, the former Liverpool defender admitted that picking up a point at Elland Road was not necessarily a bad result in isolation, even if United’s recent form made it feel like a missed opportunity.

“Going away to Leeds is going to be tricky for any team except for Manchester City and Arsenal at the moment,” Carragher said.

“A lot of teams will take a point at Leeds this season but the problem for Manchester United is the results have been so poor that they need to start winning games so it almost feels like two points dropped.”

“But in isolation, getting a point at Leeds is a decent result. I thought Harry Maguire was fantastic, especially in the second half.”

Leeds United grew into the contest after Calvert-Lewin brought them level, putting United under pressure during the closing stages. Carragher felt Maguire’s efforts in defence helped prevent the visitors from falling apart.

“After Leeds got that goal back there was a stage of the game where it felt like Man United could go under, but Maguire at the back held it together for them. He was fantastic.”

The result may feel like another opportunity missed for Carrick, but Maguire’s performance gave United something positive to take away from Elland Road.

#2165. Liverpool transfer news: Brazilian heavyweights 'plot audacious swoop' for Anfield star as bitter rivals face the drop

By Seye Omidiora | 06 Nov 2026 00:28

Liverpool goalkeeper Alisson Becker has reportedly been identified as a possible target for Brazilian giants Flamengo.

The 34-year-old goalkeeper has recently entered the final 12 months of his current Anfield contract.

Liverpool activated a clause in March to extend his deal by a year, keeping him at the club until June 2027.

However, should the two parties fail to agree another extension, the veteran could leave on a free transfer next summer.

Liverpool face no immediate risk of losing their first-choice goalkeeper, but Flamengo are understood to be assessing whether a bold move is viable.

According to Sport Witness, Alisson's name has been seriously discussed by senior figures involved in Flamengo's long-term plans.

The Rio de Janeiro club are weighing up whether to make a concerted attempt to bring the experienced international back to Brazil.

Some executives feel his proven quality and elite standing would make him a strong addition to the squad.

Others are said to be more sceptical, citing his age and recent underwhelming displays for Brazil.

Flamengo are also facing financial challenges that could significantly limit their spending ahead of next season.

Even without a transfer fee, the club would need to decide whether they could meet Alisson's substantial wage demands.

Should Flamengo choose to pursue their interest, they would face a major geographical and emotional obstacle.

Alisson developed in Internacional’s academy and has previously expressed a wish to return to his boyhood club one day.

Internacional are widely considered his likeliest destination if he eventually moves back to Brazil, but they are currently fighting to avoid relegation.

According to the report, some Flamengo figures are hoping their rivals fall into the second division.

Relegation would weaken Internacional’s ability to persuade Alisson to return or meet his wage demands, potentially putting Flamengo in a stronger negotiating position.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Manchester United travel to Italy on Wednesday, looking for their first win since September 10, when they face Como in the Champions League.

Michael Carrick’s side have struggled to build any momentum this season. Sunday’s 1-1 draw against Leeds extended their winless run to six matches in all competitions, leaving United with just seven points from their opening seven Premier League games.

The pressure on Carrick continues to grow. A win in Europe would offer some relief, but Como will not make life easy for Manchester United.

Manchester United began their Champions League campaign with a convincing 4-0 win over Sabah FK but lost 2-0 to Atletico Madrid in their next match. They have since drawn with Leeds, having also failed to beat Fulham and Tottenham in the Premier League.

Their last six matches across all competitions have produced three draws and three defeats. Michael Carrick is in pressure of his own and needs a response from his players, particularly with United sitting 13th in the Premier League and 13th in the Champions League standings.

Como have shown more promise. Cesc Fabregas’ side beat RB Leipzig 4-1 in Europe before losing 3-2 to Feyenoord. They returned to winning ways against Fiorentina, claiming a 3-1 victory that moved them up to fifth in Serie A, with 14 points from seven games.

Como have scored 11 goals and conceded eight across their last six matches in all competitions. United have struggled, with their only clean sheet of the season coming against Sabah FK.

#2163. GdS: Milan enjoying the ‘best ever’ version of Rabiot – renewal imminent as figures emerge

Adrien Rabiot continues to dazzle with his performances and goal contributions, which AC Milan intend to reward him for.

La Gazzetta dello Sport confirm that the discussions between Rabiot and Milan have been underway for some time regarding an ‘update’ to the terms of his contract. Gerry Cardinale has very clear ideas about his priorities, and the Frenchman is next on the list.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

In recent months, several players have ascended to the fourth floor of Casa Milan to sign extensions. There have been the young talents (Camarda and Comotto, 2031), veterans (Modric, 2027) and core pieces within the squad (Pavlovic, Pulisic, 2031).

Rabiot clearly belongs to the latter category (but also to a certain extent the same one as Modric) and has become the most urgent task to complete in terms of contracts.

The parties are in fact talking based on a shared desire to move forward together. For Cardinale, Rabiot represents one of the pillars on which the team’s rebuilding will continue.

Rabiot is seen as a player with the wisdom of experience, a perfect example for the younger players, and someone with the qualities of a leader even without the captain’s armband. Milan want to get back to the top, by Cardinale’s admission, and they need players like Adrien to help the cause.

So, a contract extension until 2030 has been ready for him for some time now (his current one expires in 2028). After the summer Napoli rumours, Rabiot remained at Milan partly because of his existing deal, but also partly because Cardinale convinced him.

What happens now? The next crucial step will be with his mother. Indeed, it is Mrs. Véronique who manages the former PSG midfielder’s interests, and a meeting will take place shortly. A pay rise from €3.5m net per season to €5m per year – what Pulisic earns – seems reasonable to presume.

With Rabiot on nine goal contributions for the season (five goals, four assists) he needs just one more to match his total from the entirety of 2025-26. So, he seems to be ageing like a fine French wine. All that’s needed is a final push on the contract front to continue an undoubtedly happy marriage.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

Michael Carrick has come under immense pressure as a disjointed Manchester United sit just two places above the relegation places.

Despite the scrutiny and the news of a possible sacking, Michael Carrick has been given another chance at Manchester United.

Michael Carrick will remain in charge of Manchester United for their Premier League trip to Chelsea on Saturday after the club decided to give their under-pressure head coach another opportunity to turn things around.

United have gone eight games without a win in all competitions, with Sunday’s humiliating 3-0 home defeat to Bournemouth leaving them 16th in the Premier League table, just one point above the relegation zone.

Carrick’s position looked increasingly uncertain after the Bournemouth loss. As a result, United chief executive Omar Berrada and director of football Jason Wilcox were at Carrington on Monday to discuss the situation.

It has been reported by Sky Sports that United have considered other options internally as well to replace Micahel Carrick but for the weekend, they are sticking with Michael Carrick.

Carrick took training on Tuesday and the decision means he has at least one more match to convince the club’s hierarchy that he can get United back on track.

Gary Neville admitted he was surprised that Carrick had survived the immediate threat of losing his job, although he welcomed the decision.

“I am surprised, but I am happy because I was absolutely certain that what I witnessed on Sunday would be the end of Michael,” Neville said in Sky Sports.

The former United captain explained that Carrick’s actions after the Bournemouth defeat had made him think the manager was preparing to say goodbye to the supporters.

“It looked like he was walking round at the end on his own with the players behind him, like it was almost a goodbye lap,” Neville added.

Fans continued singing the manager’s name after the final whistle, while the rest of the team drew boos.

Despite his surprise, Gary Neville hopes Micahel Carrick can use the extra time to get a response from his squad.

“I hope he can somehow get those players to start playing for him again because they haven’t played for him in the last few weeks,” he said.

The trip to Chelsea now becomes a crucial test for Micahel Carrick but United need a result and a far better performance to ease the pressure at Old Trafford.

#2160. Jim Goodwin says Dundee Utd are eager to keep momentum going against Motherwell

Jim Goodwin insists Dundee United are raring to go against Motherwell following their unwanted free weekend.

After a morale-boosting 2-2 draw with Celtic at Parkhead which extended their unbeaten run to six games, the Tannadice side had to miss their next William Hill Premiership fixture against Hearts as the Jambos had a Premier Sports Cup semi-final match against Hibernian.

That can be a little bit frustrating because, especially off the back of such a good performance against Celtic, you’re desperate for the next game to come around quick.

“But we’ve had a solid week’s training. The boys have done seven or eight sessions over the four days that we’ve been in. We’re having to drag them off the training pitch at times because they’re so keen to stay out there.

“Hopefully, there should be a lot of fresh legs there and hopefully we can go and give a good account of ourselves and maintain this unbeaten run that we’re on.”

Celtic's 'Che Guevaras' should show their fellow fans some respect at this year's AGM Marink Reedijk lays down Aberdeen challenge to Hibs team after Hearts win

United’s last defeat, coincidentally, was a 3-0 loss to Motherwell at Fir Park in September where Goodwin’s side were reduced to 10 men in 14 minutes when Emmanuel Agyei was sent off for a foul on Alex Lowry.

Goodwin is reading little into that defeat. He said: “For the first 15 minutes I didn’t think there was a great deal between the two teams. But then we get the red card to Emmanuel Agyei and that changes the whole game.

“They’ve got good pace. With the man extra they can really find pockets of space all over the pitch and they punished us on the night. But it’s a very hard game for me to analyse because of the fact that we were down to 10 men for 75 minutes.

“So I think tomorrow, if we can keep 11 men on the pitch, that would certainly be a start. I think 11 v 11, it makes for a very entertaining game of football. Two really good teams, two technical teams.

“They’ve got that attacking threat and that bit of firepower at the top with the pace they have. But we also believe we’ve got real firepower ourselves and real quality in the middle of the pitch where hopefully we can get that bit of control that we want.”

#2159. Lens vs Marseille - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joel Lefevre | 06 Nov 2026 00:42

Fresh off a pulverizing Champions League defeat in midweek, Lens will try to build themselves back up when they welcome Marseille to Stade Bollaert-Delelis on Sunday.

After nine matchdays, Lens sit ninth in Ligue 1, losing 1-0 at Lille last weekend, with OM now two points above them in sixth thanks to a 2-0 win over Toulouse.

Just when Lens seemed to be building some confidence, showing much more consistency domestically, that was all seemingly shattered in midweek.

With a 9-1 Champions League defeat versus Como on Wednesday, Yannick Cahuzac’s men became the first side to concede that many goals in that competition.

After Wednesday’s match, their resilience will be tested like never before, as they hope to avoid losing three successive competitive games for the first time all season.

Their European form has been messy, losing three consecutive Champions League outings by a combined margin of 14-1, though Lens have won three of their last four Ligue 1 affairs.

While they have scored just one Champions League goal at home this season, nine of their 16 domestic goals have occurred at Stade Bollaert-Delelis.

Les Sang et Or have won three of their last four Ligue 1 home meetings against Marseille, coming back to beat them 2-1 in this exact fixture last season.

While Lens are reeling following their midweek fixture, Marseille are feeling much better about their overall form of late.

Bruno Genesio’s men have won four of their last five competitive fixtures, including their first in the Europa League this season at Bayer Leverkusen on Thursday (1-0).

That was their first Europa League away win since 2023 at AEK Athens (2-0), with OM having now won three of their last four competitive games as the visitors.

On Sunday they can collect three successive competitive clean sheets for the first time in 2026, and also pick up consecutive shutouts in Ligue 1 for the first time since March.

Les Olympiens rank second in the league regarding goals per game (2.2) and average possession (55.7%), while only three points currently separate them from second-place Lille.

Marseille have scored seven goals in their last two Ligue 1 away fixtures, two more than they managed in their 10 domestic outings outside Stade Velodrome before that.

Due to adductor pain, Odsonne Edouard is doubtful for Lens, while Ismaelo Ganiou is recovering from knee surgery, Abdallah Sima has a leg issue, Jhoanner Chavez has a sore thigh and Maik Nawrocki is recovering from a cruciate ligament tear.

Ruben Aguilar had their only goal in that embarrassing defeat to Como on Wednesday, while Florian Thauvin has three goal involvements in their last two Ligue 1 affairs at Stade Bollaert-Delelis.

As for Marseille, Derek Cornelius is dealing with a sore hip, Keyliane Abdallah has a knock and Emerson Palmieri is questionable because of a knee issue.

Amine Gouiri has goals in back-to-back competitive outings for OM, netting the winner versus Leverkusen on Thursday, while he and Amine Harit scored in their victory over Toulouse.

Risser; Aguilar, Baidoo, Antonio; Abdulhamid, Bulatovic, Cuisance, Udol; Thauvin, Sotoca; Hazard

De Lange; Weah, Egan-Riley, Aguerd, Garcia, Hojbjerg, Nnadi, Abdelli; Mmadi, Gouiri, Paixao

Lens may be shell-shocked when they take to the field on Sunday after that lopsided defeat in Europe this week.

As such, we expect them to play rather timidly, which will play right into Marseille's hands, who are starting to round into form, especially in this competition.

#2157. Le Mans vs Monaco - prediction, team news, lineups

By Joel Lefevre | 06 Nov 2026 00:47

Le Mans will hope to dig themselves out of the Ligue 1 relegation zone Sunday when they host Monaco at Stade Marie-Mavingt.

A 2-0 defeat at Troyes last week dropped MUC72 down to 17th in the table, while Monaco sit seventh after a 3-1 loss at Paris FC.

The wheels appear to have fallen off Le Mans since the season resumed after the fall international break.

Before the October window, Patrick Videira’s men had earned points in four of their first five league contests, but they will enter this weekend on a four-match winless run in this competition.

They have just one point domestically since October, which is part of the reason they sit in the final relegation spot heading into this game.

Despite their recent poor form, they are only a point below Angers and Ligue 1 safety heading into matchday 10, with only three points currently separating them from Toulouse, who are 12th.

This team have been at their best domestically at home this season, earning points in three of those four outings, scoring six of their 11 goals at Stade Marie-Mavingt.

Les Mucistes will try to avoid suffering consecutive defeats at home in the French top flight this weekend for the first time since March 2010.

The Monaco team that we saw begin this season so well have been a shadow of themselves in recent weeks domestically.

Filipe Luis has seen his side drop points in four successive Ligue 1 affairs, with the Brazilian particularly annoyed with their performance against Paris FC.

His team bounced back in midweek, defeating Nordsjaelland in the Conference League (2-0) to maintain their 100% record in that competition.

Their triumph on Thursday made Luis just the fifth Ligue 1 manager to win his first five European matches in charge of a French club and only the second this century after Peter Bosz did so at Lyon in 2021.

On Sunday, Monaco can end a two-match losing run away from home in Ligue 1, netting just once over that stretch.

Les Monegasques can stretch their competitive unbeaten run versus Le Mans to nine matches this weekend, having come away with points in their last four away games against them.

Former Monaco defender Djibril Sidibe could miss another match for Le Mans this weekend due to a foot injury, while Erwan Colas remains questionable because of a knock.

Adil Bourabaa will not be available as the central midfielder is still recovering from ankle surgery with his return likely to be sometime in early 2027.

At Monaco, Stanis Idumbo has a knock and could miss this game, and Ansu Fati remains questionable with a sore hamstring.

Folarin Balogun netted his first of the season in their loss to Paris FC, with Mathys Detourbet and Matthis Abline scoring in their Conference League triumph on Thursday, the first for the Manchester City loanee with the Principality club.

Kocik; Torrente, Yohou, Boisse; Lauray; Vercruysse, Rabillard, Bretelle, Calodat; Mafouta, Diallo

Hradecky; Vanderson, Teze, Sane, Nazinho; Camara, Zakaria; Coulibaly, Abline, Golovin; Brunner

Monaco wasted many scoring chances in their last Conference League match, but should find more clear-cut opportunities against a Le Mans side that are a little over-aggressive at the back.

#2155. Melvine Malard becomes Chelsea’s leading scorer as FOUR-goal start strengthens her claim to the No.9 role

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Melvine Malard’s Chelsea goals are beginning to provide a persuasive answer to the club’s centre-forward question.

Her opener in Sunday’s 3-0 victory over West Ham took the summer signing to four competitive goals, making her Chelsea’s leading scorer this season.

That total comprises two goals in Champions League qualifying and two in the Women’s Super League.

The circumstances make those contributions particularly useful. Across three scoring appearances, Malard has broken a deadlock, restored a lead and helped Chelsea put difficult matches beyond their opponents.

Three of those goals arrived while Chelsea were level or protecting a one-goal advantage.

That gives the early return significance beyond its size. Malard has repeatedly contributed while there was still a contest to resolve.

Sunday provided the latest example. Chelsea had plenty of possession but needed somebody to turn an opening into a lead.

Turn and finish 💥Melvine Malard finishes for @ChelseaFCW 🔵#BarclaysWSL pic.twitter.com/eicBNrYMYk

According to Chelsea’s official match report, the Blues reached the half-hour mark without a shot on target despite having 64 per cent possession.

Sjoeke Nüsken created the breakthrough by winning possession and finding Malard.

The striker received the ball with her back to goal, held her position, turned and finished into the bottom corner.

It was an important demonstration of what she can offer centrally. Chelsea could find her under pressure and still get a shot away, rather than needing to release her into open space.

Nüsken’s contribution deserves equal recognition. Her pressing created the opportunity that Malard converted.

The sequence offers Bompastor something useful to build on: winning possession higher up the pitch and finding a forward capable of finishing before the opposition recover.

Malard’s first three Chelsea goals came as a substitute.

Her two-goal competitive debut against Real Sociedad immediately demonstrated that capacity to change a match.

Nüsken released her for the goal that restored Chelsea’s lead at 3-2. Ellie Carpenter subsequently supplied her second, extending the advantage to three goals.

The official account of that European victory records two different routes to goal: a run beyond the defence and a finish from a delivery across the box.

Against Birmingham, her alertness produced another valuable contribution. Malard punished hesitation over a backpass to score Chelsea’s second, eight minutes after coming on, as detailed in the club’s match report.

Those appearances give Bompastor flexibility. Malard has already influenced matches when introduced against tiring opponents, while West Ham supplied a scoring return from the starting role.

Chelsea signed Malard from Manchester United on a four-year contract, bringing her back together with a coach who first identified her potential at 14.

Bompastor later coached her in Lyon’s senior side. Their relationship therefore predates this summer’s recruitment by years.

Speaking when Chelsea announced her signing, Malard said:

“My objective is to become better every single day and to win trophies.”

Her next task is sustaining this contribution across a longer run of matches.

Competition remains productive. Aggie Beever-Jones replaced her at West Ham and supplied Maika Hamano’s late goal, giving Bompastor another reason to value both forwards.

Malard’s strongest argument is the range of situations she has already influenced. She has attacked space, finished crosses, punished hesitation and scored with a defender close behind her.

Four goals offer an encouraging early return. The variety behind them gives Chelsea something substantial to develop.

#2154. Ex-Rangers star signs for new club in second tier after weeks as free agent

Former Rangers midfielder Greg Docherty has signed for Preston North End as a free agent.

The 30-year-old has been without a club since leaving Charlton Athletic by mutual agreement near the end of the summer transfer window, despite having captained them to promotion from League One a year before.

With the Addicks continually strengthening for the Championship, he found himself out of favour and it was agreed he could move on in search of first team football.

Former Motherwell boss Graham Alexander recently took over at Preston and was tipped in recent weeks to make a move for Docherty, after admitting he was looking at free agents. That has now come to fruition.

Preston announced the signing today and Docherty told their website: “I’m really proud to be here, to have this badge on my chest and to be part of a club that’s steeped in history. When I told my family, they were all excited and willing me to get it done.

“I’m just thankful that we managed to do it. Coming down from Scotland and playing in England, Preston’s a very good team to get to for my family and I’ve always kept an eye on them.

Derek McInnes news, interviews and updates on the Rangers manager The quietly effective Rangers star McInnes says inspires dressing-room confidence

“I’ve always loved playing at Deepdale but haven’t won very often there, so hopefully that will change now. When I was an opposition player, I always found it really difficult. I’ve got family in the north of England and this move just really ticks all the boxes.

“To be a Preston player is something I’ve always wanted to do and I’m really proud to have finally done it.”

Docherty has been training with Alexander's side already and added: “From the moment I walked in here, I got a really good feeling from speaking to the manager and all the coaching staff.

“When I see how passionate the manager is about turning our current situation around, it excites me and I want to also help change things. I see real quality in the dressing room and I know we can improve if we keep working hard.

“The manager’s training demands are very high, and I’ve come in and really enjoyed it. I’ve enjoyed the soreness after it, I’ve enjoyed the tiredness after it, but I feel much better for it and all the lads do too.

“The boys are saying they’re getting fitter, which is massive in this league, and I just can’t wait to get going alongside them out on the pitch.”

Alexander said: “We’re really pleased to bring Greg in. He’s been a captain, won promotions and knows the game inside out.

“Greg’s vocal, fit and motivated, which are all the key things you look for in a professional footballer.

“He has settled into the group really quickly and has a real willingness to show what he can do.”

#2153. Arsenal transfer news: Mikel Arteta 'plots' January swoop for Premier League star as Man Utd 'join' race

By Seye Omidiora | 06 Nov 2026 00:55, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 00:59

Arsenal and Manchester United are reportedly competing to bring highly rated Bournemouth forward Marcus Tavernier to their clubs.

The 27-year-old has become one of the Premier League’s most coveted players after an outstanding opening to the season.

Tavernier has scored six times in 12 appearances for the Cherries this campaign, further enhancing his growing reputation under Marco Rose.

The former Middlesbrough player is contracted to Bournemouth until the summer of 2029 on a lucrative long-term deal.

Nonetheless, the wide attacker's impressive domestic performances have caught the eye of several domestic clubs ahead of the January transfer window.

TEAMtalk reports that Arsenal have prepared detailed scouting reports on Tavernier as they consider ways to bolster their left flank.

Mikel Arteta is understood to be a keen admirer of the versatile attacker, who can perform across the forward line, in midfield or at wing-back.

The Gunners are also believed to be keeping a close watch on Tavernier's teenage Bournemouth teammate Eli Junior Kroupi and promising midfielder Alex Scott.

At Man United, technical director Jason Wilcox has reportedly instructed the recruitment team to assess Tavernier as they plan for the winter window.

The Red Devils want to add a left-back and a forward in the new year, though their priorities could change if manager Michael Carrick loses his job amid mounting pressure.

Both clubs are expected to monitor Tavernier’s progress before deciding whether to make a formal approach during the mid-season window.

Arsenal and Man United may be at the front of the queue, but Newcastle United are understood to have admired the dynamic Englishman for some time.

The Magpies have tracked his development over a lengthy period and have a family link, with his brother James having previously played for the club.

Tottenham Hotspur also considered a move for Tavernier during the summer window but ultimately chose not to make a formal offer.

Any club hoping to sign Bournemouth’s talisman is likely to have to put forward a sizeable offer to secure a mid-season deal.

#2152. Danny Drinkwater on Chelsea move he ‘could never turn down’

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

The Danny Drinkwater Chelsea story still carries a sting. Five years at Stamford Bridge gave him plenty of time to think about how it all turned out, and his own verdict is not a happy one.

Drinkwater opened up in a Truth or Padel appearance alongside David Ornstein. He said the move was one he could never have turned down, even with the benefit of hindsight. “No, I don’t regret moving. I regret, in hindsight, how it’s turned out. I think I could never turn it down,” he said.

Strip away the headlines and the numbers alone tell an uncomfortable story. Drinkwater spent five years on Chelsea’s books. In that time he made just 23 appearances, according to the original interview report.

Do the maths and it barely comes to five games a year. That is a thin return for a central midfielder signed to compete for a first-team place. It is also the kind of record that rarely sits well at a club of Chelsea’s size and ambition.

Chelsea’s best attacking spells over a similar stretch of seasons look far more productive, as our striker rankings show. Set next to those numbers, Drinkwater’s five quiet years stand out even more starkly.

Five years. Twenty three games. These numbers alone explains why this transfer still gets talked about by Drinkwater, long after the headlines faded.

The numbers only tell part of the story. A string of incidents turned a quiet, underwhelming spell into something far more pointed.

Drinkwater had a training-ground bust-up while out on loan. He was also involved in an on-pitch brawl with Chelsea’s own under-23s side. Flashpoints between a first-team player and a development squad are rare at any club, let alone one with Chelsea’s standards.

Taken alone, either incident might have passed as a bad day. Taken together, it became harder to see Drinkwater simply as an unlucky squad player who could not catch a break.

The most serious moment came during his time at Aston Villa. Drinkwater headbutted team-mate Jota and was fined two weeks’ wages for it. He says the consequences at Chelsea did not stop there.

“They didn’t formally ban me, but I got removed from the first team building after the incident at Villa,” Drinkwater said.

One off-field incident, at another club, pushed him out of the manager’s day-to-day plans back home. No formal punishment at Chelsea followed. The effect was much the same.

Chelsea fans saw a similar breakdown of trust play out when Graham Potter struggled to win over the dressing room during another difficult Chelsea transition. Trust can break down fast once results or behaviour turn against the person at the centre of it.

Drinkwater’s comments matter beyond his own career. They are a reminder that saying yes to a big move does not guarantee it will work once a player walks through the door.

That tension has not gone away. Chelsea’s current transfer business, covered in our transfer watch, shows the club still weighing big calls on players whose futures depend on more than form alone.

Squad management under Xabi Alonso, assessed in our bomb squad verdict, raises a familiar issue. Players who fall outside a manager’s plans rarely find a quick way back in, whether the exile is formal or not.

Drinkwater never found his way back into Chelsea’s plans after the Villa incident. His account of the Danny Drinkwater Chelsea years stands as a blunt warning. A move that looks impossible to refuse can still become one of the hardest spells of a career.

Shumail Sajid writes about Manchester United for Read Man Utd and Crystal Palace for Read Crystal Palace, with occasional pieces on other Premier League clubs. He has also written for FanSided's Red Devils Dispatch, Yardbarker, Anfield Central and The Sports Side.

#2151. CM: Dalot could reunite with Amorim at Milan as Man Utd deal winds down

Diogo Dalot has once again been linked with a potential return to AC Milan, where he spent the 2020-21 season on loan.

Calciomercato.com report that Roma’s visit to face Manchester United at Old Trafford will not only be an opportunity for the Giallorossi to face and perhaps erase the ghosts of the past, but also to watch someone up close who could be a name for the not-too-distant future: Diogo Dalot.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

The Portuguese full-back’s contract is expiring at the end of next season and although he is happy in Manchester, many clubs are beginning to ask his entourage for details.

Roma were one step away from landing the defender in the summer of 2021 when the Portuguese coach José Mourinho was on the bench. The negotiations dragged on for a long time, but in the end it didn’t go through.

The reason ended up being quite simple: the Red Devils never agreed to a loan with option to buy formula while Roma – who were still severely limited by Financial Fair Play limitations – could not offer anything more.

Dalot knows Italy and Serie A well, and he recently spoke about his loan experience with Milan in an interview with The Player’s Tribune.

“It was one of the most important years of my career. I remember the way the club welcomed me and how welcome I felt in a year that was important to me, one in which I needed to regain my confidence in my football. Milan helped me get back to those levels,” he said.

“Every day, Paolo Maldini was on the sidelines to watch us train. Rain, shine, or cold, he was always there, in a suit and tie. He would say to me: ‘Mark like this’ or ‘Tackle it.’ Other times, he would scold me for trying too hard. ‘You’re an extraordinary player, don’t worry, you’ll see, everything will be fine’.”

As mentioned, Dalot’s deal expires in 2028 but there is the presence of a one-year clause that United can trigger. There are a number of teams monitoring his situation including Inter, Milan and Juventus, who have all enquired in recent weeks.

Nothing concrete has emerged yet, as Dalot happy in Manchester and isn’t thinking about the future. However, if things don’t change, a departure – perhaps even for a low-cost fee – could become a realistic possibility.

With the Rossoneri needing defenders, could they try pounce on a reunion for a versatile player that they and Ruben Amorim know well?

#2150. Keira Walsh adds another Chelsea weapon as former Arsenal target scores THIRD goal of the season

Graduate Sports Journalist with a love for football.

Keira Walsh is giving Chelsea opponents another decision to make: protect the penalty area or step out to stop her shooting.

Her goal in Sunday’s 3-0 victory at West Ham took her to three for the season across all competitions. Each has come from outside the box.

For a player whose passing helps organise Chelsea’s attacks, that threat adds another way to influence matches. Recent performances show her contributing both to the construction of chances and their finish.

Against Sociedad, Walsh struck from distance after a corner, helping Chelsea complete their passage into the Champions League’s league phase.

The club’s account of that qualifying victory describes a tight encounter in which clear opportunities were limited.

At Manchester United, Lucy Bronze found her near the edge of the box and Walsh drove her finish into the bottom corner.

She also supplied Lauren James’ goal that afternoon, as Chelsea’s match report records. It was a useful illustration of the choices she offers: attempt the finish herself or release somebody closer to goal.

West Ham provided another example of that balance.

Walsh had already struck the post before Alyssa Thompson carried the ball in from the left and found Wieke Kaptein. Kaptein moved it on to Walsh, whose finish gave Chelsea breathing space.

The official report also records an earlier Walsh run down the channel and delivery towards Sjoeke Nüsken.

Her involvement therefore extended beyond waiting for opportunities to shoot. She helped Chelsea approach the penalty area from different positions.

There was a similar reward against Arsenal a week earlier. Walsh’s saved effort fell to Thompson for the only goal, as covered in our report of Chelsea’s derby victory.

That sequence illustrates why a dangerous shot can matter even when the goalkeeper stops it. Chelsea had a runner ready to attack the rebound.

Our assessment is that Walsh’s shooting gives defenders an additional problem. Closing her down can open a passing route; allowing her time leaves the goalkeeper exposed to a player already finding her range.

Walsh has also explained why she feels comfortable alongside Potter.

Speaking after the Arsenal victory in an interview published by Chelsea, she said:

“Lexi is really intelligent. She makes me feel really comfortable when I’m playing next to her.”

Her wider assessment emphasised collective responsibility, with team-mates covering for each other and working to make Chelsea harder to beat.

That matters when assessing midfield influence. Walsh’s freedom to help an attack depends partly on the positioning and decisions of those around her.

Potter subsequently started alongside her in Chelsea’s confirmed team against Lyon, giving their partnership another demanding European assignment.

The encouraging feature is the trust Walsh describes. Bompastor has an experienced organiser working with a younger midfielder whose reading of the game she clearly values.

BBC Sport reported that Barcelona rejected Arsenal’s bid for Walsh in September 2024.

Chelsea completed her signing in January 2025 on a four-and-a-half-year contract. Her official club profile outlines the technical quality and ability to dictate tempo that underpinned that recruitment.

Those qualities remain central to her value. Three early goals strengthen the attacking contribution around them, without establishing that she has changed position or should abandon her deeper responsibilities.

For Bompastor, the opportunity is to preserve that balance: Walsh helping Chelsea progress through midfield, supporting those around her and recognising when the space opens for a finish.

#2147. Austin FC vs Portland Timbers - prediction, team news, lineups

By Nsidibe Akpan | 06 Nov 2026 01:09, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 08:59

Austin FC host Portland Timbers in a Major League Soccer Decision Day fixture at Q2 Stadium on Saturday.

A win would clinch a playoff place for Portland, while Austin, already eliminated, play the last match of Davy Arnaud’s interim spell before Jim Curtin takes over.

Austin FC sit 14th in the Western Conference and were eliminated from playoff contention by consecutive defeats to Real Salt Lake on October 28 and LA Galaxy on October 31.

Arnaud has been in charge on an interim basis since Nico Estevez was dismissed on May 18, and Curtin, the former Philadelphia Union head coach, takes over on December 1 or at the end of the season, whichever is later.

The Galaxy defeat was settled early, with Joao Klauss scoring in the 10th minute, Hirving Lozano in the 38th and Robert Taylor in the 48th to leave Austin three goals down before the hour.

Ervin Torres pulled one back in the 54th minute with a long-range strike for his second career MLS goal, set up by Micah Burton - who was making his first MLS start - and Jorge Alastuey, before Sergi Roberto made it 4-1 in the 66th minute.

Damian Las made two saves in the 35th minute to keep the score at 2-0 at that stage, and Riley Thomas came off the bench to make his MLS debut.

On a brighter note, Austin have scored in each of their last seven matches, having last been held scoreless by FC Dallas on September 20.

Myrto Uzuni and Christian Ramirez both have nine goals and would reach double figures with a goal on Saturday.

Austin also won the last meeting 2-1 at Providence Park on August 29, with Facundo Torres scoring the opener and Oleksandr Svatok netting the winner in stoppage time.

Portland Timbers are eighth in the West on 43 points, with 12 wins, seven draws and 14 defeats from their 33 matches, and a run of three straight victories has lifted them into a wild-card position.

Marti Cifuentes’s side were in a seven-game winless run between early September and mid-October, so a third successive playoff appearance in his first season would mark a sharp turnaround.

The three wins came against Charlotte FC, St. Louis City and Houston Dynamo, all of whom are heading to the post-season, and the Timbers scored nine goals across those fixtures.

The 3-1 victory over Houston on November 1 featured a 18-8 shot advantage, with six efforts on target compared with one for the visitors.

Vincent Janssen, a mid-season signing, scored his fifth goal of the season against Houston and added two assists, the first of his MLS career.

Kristoffer Velde scored his 11th goal of the season in the same game, and American Soccer Analysis rated him the most valuable outfield player in MLS outside Lionel Messi since the World Cup break.

David Da Costa scored the opener and took his team-leading assist tally to 11 from the corner that Janssen headed in for the third.

The Timbers rank ninth in MLS for goals scored but 22nd for goals conceded, and they have kept only two clean sheets this season.

A win clinches a playoff place and keeps seventh spot and a round-one berth, within reach, while a draw only secures qualification with help from other results.

Portland Timbers Major League Soccer form:

Austin will do without Chris Applewhite (quad) and Jon Bell (lower abdomen) for Matchday 34, while Zan Kolmanic is suspended following his red card against LA Galaxy.

Uzuni and Ramirez are both available to chase double figures, and Facundo Torres, scorer of the August opener in Portland, is also available.

Portland have six players listed as out: Alex Bonetig (shoulder), Brandon Bye (knee), Jimer Fory (concussion), Zac McGraw (back), Juan Mosquera (foot/ankle) and goalkeeper Trey Muse (concussion).

Janssen, Velde, Da Costa and Ariel Lassiter are all available again.

Las; Desler, Sanchez, Biro; Burton, Sabovic, E. Torres, Alastuey; Placheta, Gallagher, Ramirez

Portland Timbers possible starting lineup:

Pantemis; Hernandez, Surman, Waterman, Smith; Izoita, Caicedo, Da Costa; Lassiter, Velde, Janssen

Portland arrive with the clearer motivation and the stronger recent evidence, as nine goals in three wins against playoff-bound opponents sit alongside a front line in which Janssen, Velde and Da Costa are all producing.

Austin conceded three goals inside 48 minutes at the Galaxy, are heading for a bottom-two finish in the West, and have nothing to play for beyond individual milestones and the chance to give their academy players further minutes.

The Timbers have not lost in Austin since 2021, and their need for a result should decide a game in which both sides score.

Joe Hart has branded Camilo Duran an "absolute saviour" for Celtic, and Callum Osmand a player with a "magic moment" in his locker.

The former Parkhead goalkeeper now watches on as a fan and was delighted to see his old club get their mojo back with a win over Besiktas - a first victory in this year's Europa League.

Hart has been taking a closer look at the domestic form and insists Celtic are not doing any worse than in previous title-winning campaigns.

And he has picked out Duran and Osmand as two matchwinners in the side.

The turning point that inspired Celtic to big European victory against Besiktas How Celtic trio fared in B Team defeat to Ross County as O'Neill watches on

"That’s the thing about that club, and that is why I adore it, and I’m so pleased that I got to spend time there and be a part of it. The expectancy and the expectation, unless you are a Celtic man or a Celtic person, you wouldn’t understand, because, like you say, they’re still top," the Englishman told the Overlap Daily.

"I think Rangers have a game in hand because they played in a cup semi-final at the weekend, but even if they win that, they’re still a point behind. And domestically, Celtic have lost two and drawn one in 11 games. It’s not the reason. I was looking at how they’ve started leagues in the past eight, nine years, and they’re right up there with what they do, but they’re not flowing. The strikers aren’t scoring.

"Camilo Durán has been an absolute saviour this season. Him and Callum Osmand came on, I think, around the 60th minute, with it looking ominous, 1-0 down against Besiktas, who were flying high, a good team.

"And Callum Osmand’s first touch, he smashed it through bodies. He’s got that magic moment in him. He had it last year. He’s the one who scored when they beat Heart of Midlothian 3-1, and he got to run the length of the field on his own. He scored a couple of big goals in the title run-in as well.

"But it made me happy to feel the stadium shake through the TV, because that stadium does go wild, and people are talking with their feet. It wasn’t a sold-out crowd, which is so unlike Celtic.”

#2144. Arsenal transfer news: Bournemouth make Eli Junior Kroupi decision ahead of January window

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have been dealt a potential setback in their pursuit of Bournemouth forward Eli Junior Kroupi. The Cherries are reportedly refusing to consider a January sale.

The Gunners are not alone in keeping an eye on the 20-year-old after he returned from injury. Manchester United have also made enquiries, and Tottenham remains a strong possibility after chasing him over the summer.

However, Bournemouth are keen to keep Kroupi at the Vitality Stadium following the January transfer window. His return will provide new head coach Marco Rose with another attacking threat. Helping to improve their position in the Premier League.

This could mean that Arsenal will have to plan a long-term pursuit of the player.

Kroupi provided a positive impression in his debut season in English football. Becoming one of the most exciting young forwards in the Premier League.

Overall, he hit the target on 13 occasions in the previous league campaign. Attracting the attention of a number of top European clubs.

Paris Saint Germain, Barcelona, Chelsea and Tottenham were among the clubs that were said to keep following his progress over the summer months.

However, he was unable to make the move after suffering an injury to his foot which required surgery. Kroupi has now returned to action. He came on as a late substitute in Bournemouth’s 3-0 victory against Manchester United last weekend.

Arsenal have again been linked. The Gunners are now known to be one of a number of clubs to have asked about the player’s injury and fitness ahead of improving their attacking options.

Tottenham are also interested, having had previous interest in signing him. There is no real suggestion an offer has been made.

Bournemouth are the main problem for Arsenal, with Kroupa said to be unwilling to leave the Cherries in January. He is seen as an important asset for them in the second half of the season.

However, Rose’s side has had a difficult start to the season so far. Having him at his disposal could prove to be vital. The forward can bring goals and quality into the attack. Bournemouth believes it will help change their fortunes in the coming months.

They will also look to keep their attacking unit intact. Players such as Rayan and Marcus Tavernier are ones that Bournemouth will look to keep hold of. Alex Scott may also catch the eye of other clubs ahead of the New Year.

Bournemouth will be desperate to avoid losing a number of key players in one transfer window.

That stance leaves Arsenal facing a difficult decision if they want to pursue Kroupi. The Gunners can continue monitoring his performances and fitness, but a January move currently looks unlikely.

The forward’s return to regular football will give interested clubs another opportunity to assess his progress. Arsenal, Tottenham and Manchester United could therefore remain attentive to developments as the season continues.

Bournemouth are currently in the best position and Arsenal might have to wait until the summer to see how the Cherries will hold up when faced with losing one of their most promising young players.

#2140. Arsenal could expose Liverpool’s biggest weakness at Anfield

Lucie Turner writes about Arsenal for Read Arsenal and Newcastle United for Read Newcastle. A long-time Arsenal supporter, she also follows rugby, snooker, darts and Formula 1. Her work covers match previews and reports, breaking news, player profiles and opinion pieces.

Arsenal have spent years struggling to impose themselves at Anfield. However, Mikel Arteta could have an opportunity to change that record when his side face Liverpool on Sunday.

The Gunners have often struggled against the physicality of Liverpool on Merseyside. Since Jurgen Klopp took charge, Liverpool have built a reputation for being high pressing, fast, and making their opponents make mistakes.

However, looking at the latest stats, it seems that Liverpool are no longer as physical, meaning that Arsenal could target their weaknesses.

The disparity is stark between the two teams, with Liverpool having run the least distance of any Premier League side this season. Meanwhile, Arsenal are running the most.

If Arteta’s side can capitalise on this and create fast attacks from possession, then they can utilise this to their advantage.

Liverpool have shifted away from their high-octane style associated with much of Klopp’s reign. Arne Slot has slowly moved towards a more possession-based approach, focusing on keeping the ball.

Andoni Iraola looks keen to add more intensity to their pressing. The Reds have had some of the best pressing stats in the Premier League this season (Opta Analyst).

However, they are not getting the desired intensity from the system.

In addition to chasing the opposition when they have the ball, players must quickly recover, cut off passing lanes and return to their defensive positions.

This is where Arsenal might have an opportunity. If they can get past Liverpool’s first line of pressure, they may have space to push forward before the home side can get back into position.

Another area that the Gunners could target is Liverpool’s midfield.

Alexis MacAllister and Ryan Gravenberch are technically gifted and very composed when they have the ball. But neither are a true defensive midfielder, which raises questions over their ability to stop teams playing through the middle.

Arsenal have the players to exploit this. Declan Rice can put in an energetic, physical performance and drive the play forward. Myles Lewis-Skelly provides another option who can run with the ball into dangerous areas.

This can cause problems for the Liverpool midfield. Arsenal can try to disrupt Mac Allister and Gravenberch, not letting them set the tempo of the game.

If they win these individual battles, it can stop Liverpool from getting the ball control they want.

The task is certainly a tall one. Arsenal have lost their last 16 games at Anfield, including seven in Arteta’s reign.

Whilst that may be the case, the current Liverpool team are very different to the one Arsenal have faced on a regular basis during Klopp’s time in charge.

They certainly can’t expect to be outplayed, but they must take advantage of Liverpool’s failings.

Arsenal are currently second in the Premier League with 21 points from eight games. They will want to win here but will need to do more than just run them into the ground.

If Rice and Lewis-Skelly can push forward and pressurise them, forcing them to make mistakes, then Arteta could finally have his winning recipe at Anfield.

#2139. FC Cincinnati vs Nashville SC - prediction, team news, lineups

By Ben Sully | 06 Nov 2026 01:11, Last updated: 06 Nov 2026 09:06

FC Cincinnati will look to secure a spot in the first round of the MLS playoffs when they take on Nashville SC at TQL Stadium on Saturday.

Nashville head into MLS Decision Day with their fate already sealed, having clinched their first-ever Supporters' Shield.

Cincinnati are sitting in seventh spot in the Eastern Conference after winning 12, losing 11 and drawing 10 of their 33 matches this season.

Pat Noonan's side have come into form at just the right time, having dropped just two points from their last four matches since suffering consecutive defeats against New England Revolution and Columbus Crew.

Cincinnati recorded home and away wins in back-to-back games against DC United, before they played out a thrilling 2-2 draw against Inter Miami at Nu Stadium.

The Orange and Blue then returned to winning ways with a narrow 1-0 home victory against Philadelphia Union, which came courtesy of Ayoub Jabbari's third goal of the season.

Having already guaranteed some form of post-season action, Cincinnati will secure a spot in the first round with a win against Nashville, but a failure to claim all three points will leave them open to being overtaken by Columbus and Orlando City, who currently occupy the two wild-card round positions.

The hosts can take some confidence from the fact they have won two of their last three MLS home games against Nashville, including a 2-1 success in September 2025 at TQL Stadium.

Nashville will already have one eye on the first round of the playoffs after clinching their first MLS Supporters' Shield with a 2-0 win over Montreal last month.

BJ Callaghan's side went on to play out a goalless draw against Philadelphia Union before coming from two goals down to pull off a 3-2 victory over New York Red Bulls last time out.

Sam Surridge netted a stoppage-time winner in that comeback success, which extended the club's impressive 17-game unbeaten home run in the MLS.

In fact, they have gone nine matches without defeat at any venue since losing away to Toronto FC on September 27, so they will be desperate to carry that undefeated streak into the playoffs as they continue to work towards their ultimate goal of winning the MLS Cup for the first time in their history.

Having tied the MLS single-season points record, Nashville will take the outright record if they are able to add at least a point to their 74-point tally in their final outing of the regular season.

Nashville will certainly fancy their chances of claiming a positive result after running out 4-0 winners in their most recent meeting with Cincinnati at Geodis Park in August.

Cincinnati defender Tyrell Malacia is unlikely to prove his fitness in time, having been forced to sit out the last three matches with an ankle injury.

The hosts are also without the services of Julian Malatini and Miles Robinson, while Teenage Hadebe is hoping to prove his fitness after being withdrawn with an apparent injury issue in the win over Philadelphia.

Evander is in contention to start the final regular season game after featuring as a half-time substitute last weekend.

As for the visitors, they have reported no fresh injury concerns ahead of Saturday's away trip, which should give Callaghan a full squad to choose from.

The likes of Daniel Lovitz, Edvard Tagseth, Alex Muyl and Warren Madrigal could come into Callaghan's thinking if he wants to manage minutes ahead of the playoffs.

That said, Surridge will surely be keen to retain his place as he looks to add to the 19 goals he has scored in the regular season.

Celentano; Smith, Hagglund, Hadebe; Mboma, Bucha, Nwobodo, Evander, Ramirez; Barlow, Denkey

Schwake; Najar, Palacios, Woledzi, Lovitz; Espinoza, Yazbek, Tagseth, Muyl; Madrigal, Surridge

Nashville carry a nine-game unbeaten run into Saturday's fixture, and while there may be changes to their starting lineup, we think they will do enough to extend their undefeated streak with a draw.
